Mitigated by widening the Android-only timeout from 600ms to 1200ms (`Platform.OS === 'android' ? 1200 : 600`) — iOS hasn't shown this failure mode, so it keeps the tighter bound rather than slowing every unlock down for everyone. This is a mitigation, not a guaranteed fix (only one data point so far) — if `user_cancel` recurs on Android after this, the next lever is distinguishing the background-return entry path from cold-start and only widening the delay on that specific path, or listening for a real native focus-restored signal instead of a flat timeout.

**Screen redesign + `disableDeviceFallback` reversal (2026-09-26).** `verrouille.tsx` was rebuilt on a design brief: opaque full-screen "Bon retour" (brand mark, business name, no padlock icon, no blurred content behind it), Face ID still auto-triggered on mount via the same InteractionManager+timeout race documented above (untouched, still load-bearing), a failed attempt shown as one calm inline line with the whole content block as the retry tap target (replacing the old dedicated "Réessayer" button), and a permanent, always-visible "Utiliser le code" text button at the bottom instead of only appearing after a hard failure. `BACKGROUND_MS` (`app/(app)/_layout.tsx`) also dropped from 3 minutes to 120s (2 min) in the same pass — both are now a single tunable constant per screen, matching the brief.

That shorter 2-minute window is what motivated the second change: `disableDeviceFallback` flipped from `true` to `false` in `unlockWithBiometric()` (`stores/auth.ts`), reversing the reasoning two bullets above ("undercutting 'no PIN fallback anywhere'"). Routing every routine Face ID miss (sunglasses, a bad angle, a hand in the way) through a full WhatsApp OTP re-login — the only fallback `disableDeviceFallback: true` allowed — was disproportionate friction for a lock that now fires this often, and a real, recurring WhatsApp/Twilio send cost for something this frequent. With `false`, iOS/Android now offer their own native "Enter Passcode" option inside the same biometric sheet; a passcode success flows through the identical `result.success` branch as a real biometric match, no new code needed. **This is a deliberate, narrower security tradeoff, not an oversight**: anyone who knows the device's own screen-lock code can now unlock Patron too, not just whoever its biometrics are enrolled to — a real consideration on a device shared between staff. Accepted for this routine short-lock case on explicit product direction; `'unavailable'` (no biometric hardware/enrollment at all, so `authenticateAsync` is never reached) and the "Utiliser le code" WhatsApp OTP path are otherwise unchanged, and remain the only fallback when biometrics can't be attempted at all.

### Runtime version policy — fingerprint, not appVersion (`app.json`)

`runtimeVersion.policy` was `"appVersion"` (compatibility tied to the manually-bumped `expo.version` string) until 2026-07-14, switched to `"fingerprint"`. Under `appVersion`, EAS Update has no idea whether the native shell actually changed — it trusts you to remember to bump `version` whenever a native-relevant file changes, and to *not* bump it when nothing native changed. Both directions have already bitten this app: the Face ID and SMS-retriever bugs documented above (`missing_usage_description`, the Android autofill receiver) are exactly the failure mode of an OTA reaching a device whose native binary doesn't actually contain the change the OTA assumes. `fingerprint` policy replaces the manual version string with a hash (`@expo/fingerprint`) computed from the real native-relevant sources — plugins, Info.plist/AndroidManifest-affecting config, native modules, `eas.json` — so EAS Update only serves a JS update to a build whose native fingerprint actually matches; a build that needs a native rebuild simply won't receive it, instead of silently "working" with the old native code underneath.

- `npm run fingerprint:ios` / `npm run fingerprint:android` (`expo-updates fingerprint:generate --platform <platform>`) computes the hash locally — useful to sanity-check before a push whether a change is JS-only (safe for `eas update`) or touches native surface (needs a fresh `eas build`). `@expo/fingerprint` is pinned as an explicit devDependency (was previously only a transitive one via `eas-cli`/`expo-updates`) so this resolves the same way locally and in CI/EAS.
- `build:android`'s manual `expo.version` patch-bump-before-build (`package.json`) predates this switch and was working around `appVersion` policy specifically. It's harmless left in place (App Store/Play Store still show `expo.version` as the marketing version), but it no longer has any effect on OTA/native compatibility — that's now entirely fingerprint-driven.
- No DB migration involved; this is Expo/EAS config only, not app or backend logic.
- **This change itself only takes effect on the next native build, for both platforms.** The resolved runtime version (whether a literal `appVersion` string or a computed fingerprint hash) is baked into the native binary at `eas build`/prebuild time, same as any other native config — every app currently installed from a build made before 2026-07-14 still carries the old `appVersion`-policy value natively and will keep matching OTA updates by `expo.version` until it's replaced by a fresh build. Until both an iOS and an Android build ship under the new policy, `eas update` pushes are still effectively governed by the old scheme for existing users.

**A real fingerprint-drift incident, found and worked around 2026-09-27 — `npm run fingerprint:*` matching "known good" values is not something to assume, it has to be re-checked against the actual latest build every time.** Mid-session, a routine `eas update:list` check surfaced an update ("Simplify Plus menu...") tagged with a runtime version that matched *no* build on file for either platform — initially assumed to be a wasted/accidental publish. It wasn't: a from-scratch `git worktree` + `npm ci` + `expo-updates fingerprint:generate` at the exact same commit as the last real production build (`55b8254`, built 2026-09-22) computed a **different** fingerprint than that build actually shipped with — confirming HEAD's true current fingerprint had already drifted away from the last native build, for a reason never fully diagnosed (same commit, same lockfile, so not a git-history change — most likely an environment difference between local/EAS-cloud fingerprint computation, never confirmed). That "wasted" update was actually correctly tagged for HEAD's *real* current state; it just couldn't reach anyone because no native build existed yet with that fingerprint. **Practical fix used**: rather than chase the drift's root cause, published under the *literal* runtime version of the actual installed test device (from `eas build:list --channel production --limit 1`) instead of the freshly-computed one — temporarily hardcoding `runtimeVersion` to that exact hash string in `app.json` (overriding the `{"policy":"fingerprint"}` object) for one `eas update --platform <ios|android>` call per platform, from an isolated `git worktree` containing only the intended files, never touching the main working tree's `app.json` at all. See the OTA publish gate below — this is now the standing procedure, not a one-off.

**`git stash push` does not reliably revert the working tree when scoped to a large pathspec, on this machine's git version (2.39.2) — confirmed twice, two different ways, same session.** Both `git stash push -u --pathspec-from-file=<file>` (103 files) and `git stash push -u -- "${array[@]}"` (a bash array of the same 103 paths) reported success and correctly *captured* the stash content, but silently left the working tree completely unchanged — verified by checking whether `package.json` still contained a since-added dependency after each supposedly-successful stash. A single-file `git stash push -- package.json` worked correctly every time. Root cause never fully diagnosed (plausibly a `--pathspec-from-file`/positional-pathspec + `-u` interaction bug at this file-count scale, possibly related to the pathspec also including several deleted files, which separately threw a `fatal: pathspec ... did not match any files` error mid-operation on one attempt). **Don't trust pathspec-scoped `git stash push` at scale — verify the working tree actually changed after, every time, or use a `git worktree` instead** (checkout the target commit in a separate directory, copy in just the intended files, `npm ci` there) — the worktree approach never touched the main directory's git state at all and was fully reliable.

**A `git worktree`-isolated publish silently rolled back a live update a few hours later, same day (2026-09-27) — the isolation technique recommended just above is not safe by default, and needed a correction the same day it was written.** Two OTA publishes (small, deliberately-scoped signup/join-flow copy fixes) were built from `git worktree add <dir> HEAD` with only the 4-5 intended files copied in, exactly per the gate below — a clean, minimal, easy-to-reason-about bundle. What that missed: a full day of real, already-shipped work (`fournisseurs/reception.tsx`'s reception refonte, a color-discipline pass, several other screens) existed **only as uncommitted changes in the main working tree** — never committed, so `HEAD` never contained it — and had already been published live the previous evening (2026-09-26 20:40 UTC) by publishing directly from that dirty working tree. Since `eas update` always ships a complete bundle, not a diff, and `expo-updates` always serves the *newest* update matching a device's runtime version, the two small isolated publishes — newer, but built from a bundle that only contained `HEAD` + a handful of copied files — silently became the new "latest," overwriting a full day of live, working functionality for every device that picked either one up. The user caught it from their own phone within hours ("yesterday's Réception Intelligente update was working this morning, now it's gone"), not from any check on this end. **Root cause: isolating a publish to "just the intended files" is only safe when nothing else already live depends on uncommitted state that isolation would drop.** In a repo where real feature work regularly sits uncommitted for a full day or more (this project's actual, observed pattern — see "Git hygiene" in memory, added the same day, arguing against exactly this), a clean-`HEAD`-plus-copied-files worktree is not a neutral, minimal choice — it's a guaranteed regression to whatever was true before that uncommitted work existed. The fix that day: republish directly from the full, current main working tree (all of it, not an isolated copy) with only `app.json`'s `runtimeVersion` temporarily pinned to the target fingerprint for the publish step, then reverted — i.e., skip the worktree isolation entirely once the actual goal is "ship everything currently true," not "ship only this one change."

**OTA publish gate — run this exact sequence before every `eas update`, no exceptions:**
1. `git status` — know exactly what's in the working tree, staged or not. Decide deliberately whether this publish should ship (a) *only* specific new files (isolate via a `git worktree` — safe **only** if nothing else already live on the channel depends on uncommitted work that a bare `HEAD` checkout would drop) or (b) *everything currently true* (publish straight from the main working tree, no isolation — the correct choice whenever real uncommitted work is already live, which is the normal state of this repo). Never default to (a) without checking which situation actually applies.
2. Before publishing, compare against the *latest already-published update on that channel*, not just the last native build: `eas update:list --branch <channel> --limit 4 --json`. If anything already live was built from a state (commit or working tree) that included work your new bundle's source *doesn't* contain, your publish is a rollback, full stop, regardless of timestamp or fingerprint correctness — a newer, "smaller" update is still newer, and will win.
3. Compute the fingerprint fresh (`npx expo-updates fingerprint:generate --platform ios`/`android`, after `npm ci` if using an isolated worktree) and compare against the *actual last finished build* on that platform/channel — `eas build:list --platform <p> --channel production --limit 1 --json`, not against a previous OTA push's claimed runtime version, and not against a "known good" value that hasn't been re-checked this session.
4. **If the fingerprints don't match, stop.** The update would be tagged with a runtime version no installed device has and would reach nobody. Report it; don't publish speculatively. If a specific test device is the real target, publish under that device's own literal fingerprint (temporarily hardcode `app.json`'s `runtimeVersion` to that exact string for the publish step only, then revert) rather than a freshly-computed one.
5. After publishing, confirm via `eas update:list --branch <channel> --limit 2 --json` that the runtime version — and, per step 2, the *content* — actually landed as expected. A clean "Published!" message is proof of neither reachability nor completeness.

**Standing rule: never publish a bundle built from a commit or working-tree state older than — or missing anything present in — the latest live update on the channel.** A stale or narrower source is a silent rollback, even when the fingerprint is perfectly correct and the publish command reports success. Check step 2 above every time, not just when something feels off.

**Standing rule, added after the `submit_quick_sale` gap (see "Quick sale redesign" below): if the JS being published calls a new or changed RPC, confirm that migration is actually live in production — by a direct query, not memory or assumption — before or immediately after publishing.** This gate already checks git state, channel superset, and native fingerprint match; none of those would ever catch "the client code is fine, but the database function it calls doesn't exist yet." This session confirmed `migration_v189.sql` was applied before publishing its dependent OTA, then didn't repeat that check for `migration_v190.sql` — which shipped live, calling a function that turned out to not exist in production, for as long as the gap lasted. `npx supabase db query "SELECT proname FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = '<function>'" --linked` (returns rows only if it's actually deployed) is the fast, direct check — cheaper than asking and waiting, and doesn't rely on remembering which migrations were confirmed earlier in a long session.

**Production build fingerprint log (append on every native build):**

| Version | Build | Date | iOS fingerprint | Android fingerprint |

| 1.0.7 | 27 | 2026-09-22 | `6225318604f504bed12ed32f509553db3c06557a` | `56cbc540fb4545162daa92a314be3314941bd828` |
| — (HEAD `55b8254`, no matching native build) | — | 2026-09-27 | `b82fea33a0a56728e122804c5fe8ab082b384a51` | `e4d154d5276d77c5e3571e024e541bc050177899` |

### Monetary amounts — BIGINT cents (×100)

**All monetary columns in the DB are stored as BIGINT integer cents (×100).** 1500 GNF is stored as 150000. This was applied in v24 to eliminate floating-point precision errors.

- Always multiply by 100 before writing to DB; divide by 100 before display.
- Use `formatAmount(n, currency)` from `src/utils/format.ts` to display — it handles whole-unit currencies (GNF, XOF) vs decimal currencies automatically.
- This applies to: `products.sale_price`, `products.cost_price`, `products.bulk_price`, `sale_orders.total_amount`, `sale_orders.discount_amount`, `so_lines.unit_price`, `payments.amount`, `expenses.amount`, `supplier_debts.amount`.

**`formatAmountInput`/`parseAmountInput` (the TextInput-facing counterparts of `formatAmount`, also in `src/utils/format.ts`) require a `currency` argument — never call them with just one.** Found via a real merchant-reported bug: a sale showed a cash payment of "960 GNF" and a "rabais accordé" of −1,039,040 GNF on a 1,040,000 GNF sale, when the seller had actually typed 960 000. Root cause: these two functions used to unconditionally treat `.`/`,` as a *decimal* separator, with no currency awareness at all (unlike `formatAmount`, which already branches on `WHOLE_UNIT_CURRENCIES`). GNF has no subunit, so Guinea merchants routinely type "960.000" or "960,000" to mean 960 000 — that got parsed as `960.000` → `parseFloat` → `960`, silently truncating the amount by ~1000x. In `vendre.tsx`'s pay sheet specifically, the resulting tiny `parsedAmount` became both the stored `payment.amount` *and* (via `shortfall = total - parsedAmount`) an almost-100%-of-total `discount_amount`, so the sale still showed as "Payé en entier" — nothing about the flow signaled anything had gone wrong. Fixed by threading `currency` through both functions: for `WHOLE_UNIT_CURRENCIES` (GNF, XOF, XAF, JPY, KRW), `.`/`,` are now stripped as thousands-grouping noise, never reinterpreted as a decimal point; other currencies keep the original decimal-separator behavior. This touched every amount `TextInput` in the app (vendre, ventes, fournisseurs ×2, apports, catalogue, equipe, credits, depenses, onboarding/carnet, clients, tabs/index) — `currency` was already in scope at every call site (a prop or a local `session?.activeBusiness?.currency ?? 'GNF'`), so the fix is mechanical, but `catalogue.tsx`'s module-level helpers (`productToForm`, `totalCost`, `validateForm`, `formToData`, `makeVariantItem`) had no `currency` parameter at all and needed one threaded through, since they sit outside any component closure.

**`formatAmountInput(String(n), currency)` on a *computed* (not user-typed) whole-GNF value must round `n` first, or a fractional remainder silently multiplies the displayed number by 10x+ (found 2026-07-17, real production data).** A merchant saw a product's "Prix d'achat unitaire" showing 575,808 GNF (implying a −495,808 GNF/unit loss) when the real landed cost — confirmed against `stock_moves`/`expenses` history via `supabase db query --linked` — was 57,580.80 GNF (a healthy +22,419 GNF/unit margin). The AVCO shipping-allocation in `receive_purchase_order` (see `migration_v101.sql`) was working exactly as designed, dividing the shipping cost by the real received quantity for that batch; the bug was purely downstream. `stores/products.ts` converts DB cents to a display-unit float with a bare `p.cost_price / 100`, no rounding — since AVCO landed cost rarely lands on a clean multiple of 100 cents, this routinely produces a fractional GNF value (e.g. `57580.8`). `formatAmountInput`'s whole-unit-currency branch (`raw.replace(/[^\d]/g, '')`) is correct for its actual contract — a *user-typed* string, where a `.`/`,` is always thousands-grouping noise per the fix above — but a machine-produced `String(57580.8)` also contains a `.`, and stripping it while keeping the trailing digit turns `"57580.8"` into `"575808"`. The fix is not in `formatAmountInput` (changing its stripping behavior would break the documented thousands-grouping fix above); it's at each call site that feeds it a *computed* number: wrap in `Math.round(...)` before `String(...)`, same as several call sites already did (`clients/[name].tsx`, `ventes/index.tsx`, `vendre.tsx`) — those just hadn't been applied consistently. Fixed in all 11 remaining call sites across catalogue.tsx (`productToForm`'s `cost_price`/`sale_price` — the one that manifested here), fournisseurs/index.tsx ×3, fournisseurs/[id].tsx ×4, apports/index.tsx, tabs/index.tsx, equipe/index.tsx, depenses/index.tsx. Any *new* `formatAmountInput(String(x), currency)` call must round `x` first unless `x` is guaranteed to already be a whole number (e.g. a raw cents value, or something the user just typed).

### Variant products — out-of-stock display (fixed 2026-07-28)

A merchant with an all-variants-zero-stock product (e.g. every size/color at 0) saw it still rendering as a normal, available product in both Catalogue's product list and Vendre's sell grid — tappable, no "Épuisé" label, no dimming. Root cause was in the two card-level components, `ProductRow`/`StockStatus` (`catalogue.tsx`) and `ProductTile` (`vendre.tsx`): both computed their out-of-stock flag as `!product.has_variants && product.stock_qty === 0` — i.e. **unconditionally `false` for any variant product**, not because someone tried to sum variant stock and got it wrong, but because the parent row's own `stock_qty` is *always* 0 for a variant product (a real, documented invariant — see `cancel_sale`'s stock-restore note under migration_v125), and whoever wrote these two components correctly avoided using that meaningless field but never added the obvious follow-up: aggregate the real per-variant stock instead. The two components simply never looked at `variantsByProduct` at all.

This had no real oversell risk — `submit_sale` enforces `stock_qty >= qty` per variant server-side (`RAISE EXCEPTION 'Stock insuffisant'`), and `VariantPickerSheet` already correctly disables +/− and shows "Épuisé" per-row once opened — so a merchant could tap into a fully-empty variant product and would find every row blocked, just with no warning before tapping. The list-level filters that decide *which* products even reach these components (`inStockFiltered` in vendre.tsx, `inStockActive`/`outOfStockActive` in catalogue.tsx) already did the correct `variants.every/some(v => v.stock_qty ...)` aggregation — but a product whose `variantsByProduct[id]` hadn't loaded yet (or never successfully loaded — `stores/products.ts`'s `fetchVariants` silently resolves to `[]` on any Supabase error without ever updating store state, so a persistent fetch failure leaves that product permanently in the fail-open "assume in stock" branch) slips through those filters and reaches the card component with no defense-in-depth, since the card itself never checked.

Fixed by threading `variantsByProduct[product.id]` into both `ProductRow`/`StockStatus` and `ProductTile` as a `variants` prop, and computing `outOfStock` for a variant product as `variants.length > 0 && variants.every(v => v.stock_qty <= 0)` — `variants` undefined (not loaded yet) intentionally still reads as "not out of stock," the same fail-open default already used everywhere else in this codebase for variant stock, rather than flashing every variant tile as unavailable while its stock is still being fetched. Any new component that renders a product card and needs to know real availability must take `variants` as a prop and do this same check — `product.stock_qty` is meaningless for `has_variants: true`.

### First-run hero action (`src/components/FirstRunHeroOverlay.tsx`, `db/migration_v189.sql`, added 2026-09-27)

Replaces landing on an empty dashboard right after "Ouvrir mon commerce" with one real question — "Qui vous doit de l'argent ?" — name + amount, nothing else. Soft gate, always skippable via a real "Passer," never a forced wall (a vendor with nobody owing her today must never stare at one). Persisted per business, not per account, as `businesses.first_run_hero_completed_at` — a nullable timestamp, not a boolean, so it can double as an audit "when" for free.

**Three non-obvious things make this actually correct, not just present:**

1. **Backfill is load-bearing.** The migration stamps every pre-existing business to `now()` in the same statement that adds the column — without it, every business that existed before this shipped would read `NULL` and start showing this "first-run" flow retroactively to established shop owners on their next app open. This is a first-run-only flow; nothing about it should ever be a surprise to an already-running shop.

2. **A manager/vendeur/investisseur never creates a business — they only ever arrive via `join_business()`.** Rather than branch on role client-side to decide "does this person own the ledger," `join_business()` itself (migration_v189.sql) stamps `first_run_hero_completed_at = now()` the instant anyone joins a business whose flag is still `NULL` — one `WHERE ... IS NULL` guard, applied server-side, at the one function that's the *only* way a non-creator ever gets a membership row. This closes the race a client-side "am I the owner" check couldn't: an admin who shares an invite code before ever completing (or skipping) their own gate still can't leak the gate to whoever joins next, since the join itself closes it for that business immediately. The client-side gate condition (`app/(app)/_layout.tsx`'s `showFirstRunHero`) still checks `role === 'administrateur'` too, but that's defense-in-depth on top of a server guarantee, not the real enforcement — same posture as every other role check in this app.

3. **The persisted flag and "is the overlay currently visible" are deliberately two different pieces of state, and conflating them is a real bug that was caught before shipping.** `first_run_hero_completed_at` gets stamped the moment the *first* save succeeds — that has to happen before the payoff screen ("Noté ✓ · Total à recevoir : …") can render at all. If `showFirstRunHero` reacted to that persisted flag alone, the Modal would unmount itself the instant a save succeeded, before the merchant ever saw the payoff — the flag exists to prevent the gate from reappearing on a *future* render/launch, not to control the *current* one. `app/(app)/_layout.tsx` holds a separate local `heroDismissedLocally` boolean (reset on business switch) that only flips true on an explicit exit — "Passer," or "Voir mon commerce" after the payoff screen — and that local flag, not the DB one, is what actually closes the overlay in the moment. "Ajouter une autre dette" loops back to the ask screen without touching either flag, accumulating a running `total à recevoir` in local component state across repeat entries in the same sitting.

**Found on-device, same day, before the ink was dry: skipping the gate on a genuinely empty business immediately triggered `ActivationForkOverlay` — the OLD, hard, non-dismissible 3-button wall — right behind it.** `showFork`'s own condition (no product, no sale, business under 24h old) was still true after "Passer," since skipping deliberately leaves the business exactly as empty as it was. Net effect: a soft, real-exit gate followed instantly by a forced wall with no bail-out at all — strictly worse than not having built the soft gate in the first place, and a direct contradiction of its whole design premise. A completed hero save already avoided this by accident (a debt is a credit sale, so `forkStep3Done` goes true) — only the skip path was exposed. Fixed by adding `!activeBusiness.first_run_hero_completed_at` to `showFork`'s own condition (`app/(app)/_layout.tsx`) — once a business has been through the hero gate at all, skip or save, the old fork never fires for it again. **Any future onboarding surface built to sit "in front of" `ActivationForkOverlay` needs this same explicit suppression** — the fork's condition is purely data-driven (product/sale counts + age) and has no innate awareness that a newer, narrower gate already had its turn.

**A second, more subtle same-shape bug found on-device the same day: the payoff screen ("Noté ✓ · Total à recevoir : …") never actually appeared after a successful save — the overlay closed itself instantly instead.** The fix above (checking `!activeBusiness.first_run_hero_completed_at` for the fork) was correct, but `showFirstRunHero` itself was *also* still directly checking that same live flag, alongside the separate `heroDismissedLocally` boolean meant to control "is this instance still open." Two conditions ANDed together, either one going false hides the overlay — and the flag genuinely does go non-null the moment `markFirstRunHeroCompleted()` runs inside `handleSave()`, which has to happen *before* the payoff phase can render at all. So the Modal was unmounting itself mid-flow, exactly the failure mode already anticipated and supposedly guarded against, just not fully. **Fixed by removing the live flag from the visibility decision entirely.** `app/(app)/_layout.tsx` now latches eligibility into a plain `heroBusinessId: string | null` exactly once, in a `useEffect` deliberately keyed *only* on `activeBusiness.id` — never on `first_run_hero_completed_at` itself, since that's precisely the value the gated overlay is allowed to change out from under its own visibility check. `showFirstRunHero` is then just `heroBusinessId === activeBusiness.id`, closed only by an explicit `onDone()` (Passer, or "Voir mon commerce"). The persisted flag keeps doing its real job — deciding eligibility the *next* time this business's id becomes active — it now has zero power to affect an already-open instance. **Lesson for any gate whose own completion is signaled by the same flag that controls its visibility: decide "should this be showing" once, on entry, and never let the thing that marks completion also be capable of reactively closing the UI before completion has actually been shown to the user.**

**A third, unrelated bug found in the same on-device pass: the dashboard's debt total didn't update after the hero flow, only after a real business switch.** `(tabs)/index.tsx` already reloads its KPIs `useFocusEffect` — "every time this tab gains focus" — but `FirstRunHeroOverlay` is a plain RN Modal rendered outside the tab navigator entirely; closing it never triggers a real react-navigation focus transition, since Accueil was never actually unfocused while it was open (a business switch "fixed" it only because that's a much bigger, genuinely reactive state change, unrelated to focus). Fixed with a minimal cross-cutting signal, following the same lightweight pattern `suppressActivationFork` already established: a plain `homeRefreshToken: number` on `useAuthStore`, bumped directly via `.setState()` (no dedicated action) from `_layout.tsx`'s `onDone`, watched by a small `useEffect` in Accueil that calls the existing `loadAll()` — skipping the very first render via a ref, so it doesn't duplicate the `useFocusEffect`-driven load already covering initial mount. **Any future overlay that mutates data Accueil displays, while rendered as a Modal outside the tab navigator, needs the identical signal** — `useFocusEffect` alone cannot see it close.

**Demo mode is excluded via `isDemoMode`, not by matching a business name.** An earlier version of this spec named a specific "Exemple — Données de test" business as a carve-out — no such business exists in this codebase (the real demo business, seeded by `seed-demo-business`, is named "Boutique Démo," and is created via an anonymous session that never touches `createBusiness()`/`join_business()` at all, so it structurally can't reach this gate regardless). Excluded here the same way `NotificationPrimer` already excludes it a few lines below, via the session's own `isDemoMode` flag — the more robust check, and the one this codebase already trusts for the identical concern.

**Reuses `submitCarnetDebt` (the same offline-first RPC `CreditRapideCapture`/`vendre.tsx`'s Crédit tab call) rather than building new save plumbing.** The "upsert a client by name, then submit" sequence is copied from `CreditRapideCapture`'s own new-client path so this first entry is a real client + real credit sale from the moment it saves — visible in Clients/Crédits like any other, not a toy value that has to be re-entered "for real" later. Deliberately its own minimal component rather than a reuse of `CreditRapideCapture` itself: no client picker (nobody exists yet on a brand-new business), no Vente/Crédit toggle, no phone field — all three are explicit spec exclusions for this specific moment, not omissions.

**Payoff-screen layout pass, same feature, one day later — the "Noté ✓" screen visibly jumped down and had no real hierarchy between the two lines of text.** Three fixes, layout-only, no copy or color changes:
- **"Passer" removed from the payoff screen entirely** (still shown on the ask screen only) — the save already happened by the time payoff renders, so there's nothing left to skip; header's exit affordance is now `{phase === 'ask' && ...}`.
- **The downward jump was `scrollContent`'s `justifyContent: 'center'`** — both phases shared one centered container, so each phase re-centered independently around its *own* content height, and the payoff phase (shorter) landed its title lower than the ask phase's title sat, reading as a jump instead of a crossfade-in-place. Fixed by making `scrollContent` top-aligned (`flexGrow: 1`, no `justifyContent`) and giving the payoff phase its own `payoffContent` wrapper with the *same* top padding as the ask phase's `content` — both phases' title blocks now land at the identical Y regardless of content height. A trailing `payoffSpacer` (`flex: 1`) — inside an `Animated.View` that itself had to switch from a fixed `gap` to `flex: 1` for the spacer to have anything to expand into — pushes the button block down into the lower third (thumb zone) instead of it following the text immediately.
- **Hierarchy**: the debt line ("{Nom} vous doit {montant}.") stays ink/primary; a new `totalLine` style (`marginTop: spacing[6]`, larger than the tight header gap) plus `color="secondary"` on "Total à recevoir" demotes it to a quieter running total instead of equal-weight with the headline.

**Found while making that last change: `styles.sub`'s hardcoded `color: p.textPrimary` had been silently defeating every caller's own `color` prop since this component was first written.** `Text.tsx` merges style as `[typography[variant], { color: colorMap[color] }, style]` — whatever `style` prop a caller passes is *last* in that array, so a hardcoded `color` inside it always wins over the `color` prop, silently. `FirstRunHeroOverlay`'s ask-phase subtitle had been passing `color="secondary"` and getting ink-colored text anyway, unnoticed until this pass needed "Total à recevoir" to actually render secondary and it didn't, either — same shared `sub` style, same bug, just newly load-bearing. Fixed by removing the hardcoded color from `styles.sub` entirely (`{ lineHeight: 22 }` only) and relying on each call site's explicit `color` prop, which is what every call site already intended. **Any `<Text style={...}>` usage in this codebase should never hardcode `color` inside that `style` object — it silently overrides the `color` prop and there's no warning when it does.** This is worth checking for elsewhere if a `color` prop on `Text` ever appears to have no effect.

**Empty-home redesign, same week: the dashboard's two top cards used to show a literal "0" (or vanish entirely) for a fresh business, instead of an invitation to act.** Two independent fixes in `app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx`, both scoped to the default admin/manager/vendeur home branch (investisseur/vendeur-no-products branches are untouched):
- **Day-card zero state.** The hero card's caption used to always append "· {salesCount} ventes" — printing "· 0 ventes" on any day with no sales yet, on any business, fresh or established — and its giant `amountLarge` figure always rendered `rawOrMask(revenue_today)`, i.e. a literal giant "0" with no sales. Fixed: `hasSoldToday = salesCount > 0` now gates both — the caption drops the count entirely when zero ("Ce soir" / "Bonjour" / "Aujourd'hui", no number), and the amount row is replaced by "Aucune vente pour le moment." plus a quiet link-button "Enregistrer une vente" (→ Vendre, default vente mode). The `heroComparison` row below (delta pill / "Bienvenue" / "Même niveau qu'hier") is deliberately untouched — it already handles zero correctly (no delta pill fires with nothing to compare, "Bienvenue" already covers a business's first day) and doesn't itself print a bare "0" anywhere.
- **Debt-card zero state — "the deferred hero action."** The Zone 2 attention card for outstanding debts used to only render at all when `credit_count > 0`; when it was 0 (and low-stock was also 0), the whole zone rendered nothing — deliberate before this change ("nothing that needs attention" → no placeholder), but that swallowed a real opportunity: `FirstRunHeroOverlay`'s "Qui vous doit de l'argent ?" form is genuinely useful again any time a business is back down to zero outstanding debts, not just once at first run. Fixed by making the debt card unconditional: `credit_count > 0` still shows the existing data card unchanged, but `credit_count === 0` now shows a CTA card ("Qui vous doit de l'argent ?" / "Écrivez son nom et le montant." / "Enregistrer une dette") that opens `FirstRunHeroOverlay` directly via a local `showDebtCapture` boolean — same component the first-run gate itself uses, reused rather than duplicated, since the spec calls for "the same single-purpose form." This is a second, independent trigger for that component, unrelated to `_layout.tsx`'s `heroBusinessId` gate-eligibility latch — it can't double-render with the gate itself, since the gate's own Modal (when eligible) fully covers Accueil, so the CTA button underneath can never be reachable at the same time. The low-stock card keeps its old purely-conditional behavior (a real "needs attention" signal has no equivalent always-useful zero-state); the two resolve independently, exactly per spec — a business with sales but no debts still sees the debt CTA, and vice versa.

Neither fix attempts to make the zero-state and data-state variants the same height — they're genuinely different content (a two-line message + link vs. a giant amount figure; a headline + subtitle + button vs. a label + big number), and forcing an artificial fixed height to avoid any size change on the transition would look more contrived than the size change itself, which only ever happens across a data refetch, never a live in-place animation.

**A real gap between this feature's own state-table spec and the actual lock mechanism, found and fixed the same week: locking mid-hero-flow silently wiped whatever the merchant had typed.** The spec's premise was "the lock is an overlay, not a navigation — unlock restores exactly what was showing." The code doesn't work that way: `app/(app)/_layout.tsx`'s `if (locked) return <Redirect href="/(auth)/verrouille" />;` is a real navigation, which unmounts `FirstRunHeroOverlay` for real — its own local `useState` (phase, name, amount, totalCents, lastEntry) is gone, and unlocking simply mounts a fresh instance defaulting back to the empty ask screen. `heroBusinessId` (the latch controlling *whether* the overlay is eligible to show, discussed above) survives this fine, since it lives in `_layout.tsx`'s own component instance rather than the overlay's — only the overlay's own in-progress form state was the actual casualty. Fixed by mirroring that in-progress state into `useAuthStore.heroDraft` (`{ businessId, phase, name, amount, totalCents, lastEntry } | null`) on every change, keyed on `businessId` so a stale or completed draft is never mistakenly rehydrated into a different business's session; `FirstRunHeroOverlay` reads it once at mount to decide its initial state instead of always starting from defaults, and clears it on both real exits (`handleSkip`, "Voir mon commerce" → `handleFinish`). This only fixes the hero overlay specifically, not the lock mechanism itself or any other screen's local state under the same `<Redirect>` — a genuine general-purpose "lock is truly just an overlay" rearchitecture would be a much larger change than this one feature needed.

**Two more real, on-device-reported flashes fixed the same week, both a variant of "the underlying data hasn't caught up yet, but the thing that was hiding a competing overlay already stopped hiding it."**

- **Right after creating a business, whatever was underneath the hero gate was visible for a few real milliseconds before the gate itself appeared.** `heroBusinessId`'s eligibility was decided in a `useEffect` keyed on `activeBusinessId` — correct in *what* it computes (see the latch design above), but a `useEffect` only runs *after* React has already committed and painted a frame. The very first render for a brand-new business therefore always painted with `heroBusinessId` still holding its previous value (null, or a different business's), before the effect corrected it a moment later — a real, visible flash, not a hypothetical one ("you can feel it for some ms," reported live 2026-09-28). Fixed by moving the identical computation out of `useEffect` and into the render body directly, guarded by comparing `activeBusinessId` against a new `heroCheckedBusinessId` state (React's own documented "adjust state while rendering" pattern — calling a setter directly in the render body, guarded so it only fires once per real input change, makes React discard the current render and re-render synchronously with the corrected state before anything is ever painted). Still keyed on the business id alone, never on `first_run_hero_completed_at` — the guard is what changed, not what's being guarded against.
- **After adding a credit or a quick sale via `QuickCaptureSheet` and closing it, `ActivationForkOverlay`'s 3-button wall would flash in for a moment then disappear.** `QuickCaptureSheet` suppresses the fork (`suppressActivationFork: true`) for as long as it's open, and used to un-suppress the instant it closed. But `showFork`'s own `!forkStep3Done` condition depends on `useVentesStore.sales`, which doesn't update synchronously when a credit/sale is recorded — Accueil's `onClose` handler calls `loadAll()`, a real network round trip, not an instant local update. In the gap between "suppression lifts" and "the fetch actually resolves," `showFork` could briefly read true again on stale data, exactly the same race `app/(app)/_layout.tsx`'s own `beginForkNavigation()` already exists to close for the fork's *own* buttons — just never applied to this second trigger. Fixed the same way: `QuickCaptureSheet`'s un-suppress is now delayed 1200ms (matching `beginForkNavigation`'s own window) instead of firing the instant the sheet closes, giving the refetch time to land first. Scoped to `QuickCaptureSheet` only — `vendre.tsx`'s own inline Crédit tab suppresses via a `useFocusEffect` with a different trigger shape (losing screen focus, not closing a sheet) and wasn't the surface being reported.

### Quick sale redesign — "Vente rapide" (`src/components/VenteRapideCapture.tsx`, `db/migration_v190.sql`, added 2026-09-27)

Replaces the old name-typing "Nouvelle vente" form (`app/(app)/onboarding/vente-rapide.tsx`, deleted) — that screen asked for a product name, a quantity stepper, a unit price, and Payé/Crédit, then on save it called `createProduct()` to clone whatever was typed into a **real, visible, stocked catalog product** before running the normal `submit_sale()` RPC. Real friction for a rush cash sale, and a real, if minor, catalog-pollution problem — every quick sale left behind a permanent product row the merchant never asked for. Final shape (after one same-week revision — see below): name **optional** ("Qu'avez-vous vendu ? (optionnel)"), a real quantity stepper (default 1), "Prix unitaire," and an "Ajouter · {total}" button that names the consequence before she taps it — paid-only, no payment picker, credit stays its own separate path. The hard data rule underneath all of it: "Pas de produit, pas de quantité [inventée]" — a real qty and an optional label are hers to give, but neither ever becomes a real catalog product or a real stock movement.

**First version shipped fully amount-only (no name, no qty) — revised the same week once the product spec added them back, and caught before the revision ever reached a live database.** The very first cut of this feature had zero fields beyond the amount, on the theory that speed meant stripping everything; the actual spec (once written out in full) wanted the name kept as an optional, skippable field and the quantity kept real, not discarded — "Pas de produit, pas de quantité" turned out to mean "never *invent* a product or a fake quantity," not "never let her enter one." `submit_quick_sale()`'s signature changed from `(business_id, seller_id, amount, idempotency_key)` to `(business_id, seller_id, unit_price, qty DEFAULT 1, label DEFAULT NULL, idempotency_key)` — and since this was caught by re-checking production directly (see below) before the first version was ever applied there, the fix went into `migration_v190.sql` **in place**, not a superseding `v191` — same posture this file's own history already established for a migration corrected before it ever went live (`migration_v156.sql`, `migration_v161.sql`): the numbered sequence should only ever reflect what was actually true in production at some point, not every draft along the way.

**"Vente rapide" is a mode of the rapid capture sheet (`QuickCaptureSheet.tsx`), not a separate screen.** That sheet's existing "Crédit | Vente" segmented control used to have Vente just hand off to the old screen (`router.push` + close the sheet); it now renders `VenteRapideCapture` in place, exactly the way Crédit already renders `CreditRapideCapture` in place — switching segments no longer navigates anywhere. `VenteRapideCapture` is deliberately its own small component, not a mode flag inside `CreditRapideCapture`: the two forms share almost nothing (no client picker, no phone, no credit/cash choice) beyond the general "amount box + Ajouter button, stay in the loop" shape, and forcing them into one component would mean more conditional branches than either form actually has fields.

**`submit_sale()` cannot record a product-less line — this is why a new RPC exists, not a new mode on the old one.** `migration_v187`'s fuzz-pass validation requires every cart line's `product_id` to reference a real row in `products` owned by the business; that's the correct behavior for a real cart of real items, and it structurally cannot accept "no product." `submit_carnet_debt()` already solved the identical problem on the credit side years earlier: a single hidden `is_system` placeholder product ("Solde reporté") that every credit debt's one `so_lines` row points at, satisfying the FK without ever creating something visible in Catalogue. `submit_quick_sale()` (`migration_v190.sql`) is the same pattern for a **paid** cash sale — its own separate placeholder, "Vente rapide," not shared with the debt side's "Solde reporté," so a human spot-checking raw data (or a future report) can tell "this was a quick cash sale" apart from "this was a carried-over credit balance" at a glance. The placeholder *product's own name* always stays "Vente rapide" (one shared, get-or-create row per business); what the merchant actually typed (if anything) lives separately on `so_lines.product_name` — exactly the per-line denormalized snapshot column `migration_v54.sql` added for this reason, since a line's own label can differ from its product's live name. The total is computed server-side as `qty × unit_price`, never trusted from a client-supplied total, same "compute, don't trust" posture `submit_sale`/`edit_sale` already use. Like `submit_carnet_debt`, it writes zero `stock_moves` rows and never touches `products.stock_qty` on any real product — the placeholder's own `stock_qty` is a fixed `999999` that nothing ever reads as a real balance (already excluded from every stock-consistency reconciliation check via `is_system`, same as "Solde reporté" always has been, `migration_v104.sql`). It also always writes a real `payments` row (`method: 'especes'`, the fixed default — there's no payment picker in this mode, a Vente is always paid), which `submit_carnet_debt` never needed to since a debt is never "paid" at creation.

**A pre-existing, previously-undiscovered gap fixed in the same migration: `get_best_sellers()` has never filtered `is_system`.** Every credit debt ever recorded has been silently contributing its amount to "Solde reporté" as if it were a real top-selling product in the "meilleurs produits" ranking — a real bug, not introduced by this migration, just never caught before because nobody had reason to look this closely at that ranking's inputs. Left alone, adding a second `is_system` placeholder for quick sales would have doubled the leak (two fake "products" competing for the top spot instead of one). Fixed with one added `AND p.is_system = false` — no signature change, safe as a plain `CREATE OR REPLACE`. Verified directly: `__tests__/integration/quick-sale.integration.test.ts` creates a quick sale, a credit debt, and one real product, then asserts the real product appears in `get_best_sellers()` and neither placeholder does.

**A real production gap, found and closed the same week: the migration this whole feature depends on was never actually applied to production, even after an OTA carrying the dependent client code had already published and gone live.** The standing OTA gate (see "OTA publish gate" above) checks git state, channel superset, and fingerprint match — it has never included "and is the RPC this update calls actually deployed." Caught only by directly querying the linked production database (`npx supabase db query "SELECT proname FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = 'submit_quick_sale'" --linked`) while starting the very next round of work on this feature, and finding zero rows — meaning the "Ajouter" button in the already-live Vente-mode sheet would have failed with a bare "function not found" error for any real merchant who tapped it, for as long as the gap lasted. **Any OTA that calls a new or changed RPC needs the migration confirmed live in production — by direct query, not by assumption or memory — before or immediately after that publish, the same way `migration_v189.sql` was explicitly confirmed applied before its own dependent OTA went out.** This session had done that once (v189) and then didn't repeat it for v190, which is exactly the kind of one-off discipline that needs to be a standing step, not something remembered per-feature.

**Two real dead ends found and fixed the same week, both matching the same shape: "record a sale" routes were landing on an empty product grid instead of the actual quick-sale form.** Neither was a hypothetical — both were the literal, load-bearing point of the original "Vente rapide" request (a merchant hitting a wall trying to record her first sale):
- **Accueil's own empty day-card "Enregistrer une vente" link** (added in the "Empty-home redesign" note above, the same week) opened `router.push('/(app)/(tabs)/vendre')` — landing on Vendre's full product-grid POS screen, which for a brand-new business is empty, with only a small, easy-to-miss "+" FAB as a way forward into full product creation. Fixed to open `QuickCaptureSheet` directly in Vente mode (`setQuickCaptureMode('vente'); setShowQuickCapture(true)`), the same local state this screen's own "+" FAB already drives — recording a sale no longer requires creating a catalog product first.
- **`vendre.tsx`'s own Vente-mode empty state, admin/manager, online, zero products** rendered *nothing at all* in its content area except that same small FAB — real, working, but easy to miss, and its only destination was the full "Nouveau produit" form regardless. Fixed with an explicit "Aucun produit pour le moment." message plus two buttons: "Vente rapide" (opens a `QuickCaptureSheet` instance mounted locally on this screen, defaulting to Vente mode) and "Ajouter un produit" (the same Catalogue-form deep link the FAB already used). The FAB itself is now scoped to the offline branch only (`!isVendeur && offline`) — the online case's own explicit "Ajouter un produit" button made it a redundant second way to do the identical thing on the same screen. The offline/vendeur empty-state message above this block, and the online product grid/cart/tabs/chevrons below it, are all untouched.

**Entry points repointed, not left dangling.** `ActivationForkOverlay`'s "Une vente" button (`app/(app)/_layout.tsx`) used to `router.push` straight to the now-deleted screen. The fork is evaluated at the root layout, above the tab navigator, with no direct reference to Accueil's own `QuickCaptureSheet` open-state — so repointing it needed a small cross-cutting signal, the same lightweight "plain field on `useAuthStore`, set via `.setState()`, no dedicated action" pattern already established for `suppressActivationFork`/`homeRefreshToken`: a new `requestQuickCapture: 'credit' | 'vente' | null` field. `onSelectSale` now sets it to `'vente'` and navigates to Accueil (same "land on the screen that will act on this, then ask it to" convention `onSelectProduct`/`onSelectDebt` already use); Accueil watches the field, opens `QuickCaptureSheet` with `initialMode` set accordingly, and clears the signal. `vendre.tsx`'s own "Vente" mode (the full cart/catalog-based POS flow, its main tab content) is a **completely different feature** from "Vente rapide" despite the name overlap — nothing about it needed to change, and it was never an entry point to the deleted screen in the first place.

**Found while wiring this up: closing `QuickCaptureSheet` never refreshed Accueil's own KPIs — the identical "Modal doesn't trigger a real focus transition" bug already fixed once for `FirstRunHeroOverlay`, just never noticed here because nothing had looked for it.** This sheet is a plain RN Modal (via `FormSheet`) rendered as Accueil's own child, but per that earlier fix's own lesson, a native Modal opening/closing never causes the underlying screen to blur/refocus in react-navigation's terms regardless of where in the tree it's mounted — `useFocusEffect` alone can't see it close. This means a credit debt recorded via `CreditRapideCapture` from this exact sheet had the same staleness bug all along (the day-card/debt-card wouldn't reflect it until a business switch), not just the new Vente mode — it just hadn't been noticed because nobody had specifically checked "does Accueil update the instant I close this." Fixed once, for both modes at no extra cost: `(tabs)/index.tsx`'s `onClose` handler for `QuickCaptureSheet` now also calls the screen's own `loadAll()`.

**Visual save confirmation, same week — the haptic-only feedback wasn't enough on its own, and the original Vente-mode toast was dropped entirely rather than kept alongside the fix.** Two additions, both explicitly "no modals, no navigation, no toast popups — the loop must never break":
- **Button morph.** Both `VenteRapideCapture` and `CreditRapideCapture`'s "Ajouter" buttons now swap their own label to "✓ Ajouté" for ~900ms on a successful save, always in the default brand-purple `Button` styling — no color change. This replaces two different prior behaviors: Vente mode's transient toast (`"+{montant} · Total : {session}"`, now fully removed), and Credit mode's color-swapping "✓ Noté !"/"⏳ Enregistré hors ligne" (the queued/synced distinction is dropped from this specific confirmation — the persistent `SyncBanner` elsewhere in the app already surfaces pending-sync state, so this isn't a coverage gap, just no longer duplicated here). **The morph is deliberately decoupled from form-clearing in Vente mode**: the form (name/qty/price) resets immediately on success, before the 900ms even starts, specifically so typing the next entry is never gated by a cosmetic timer — the "✓ Ajouté" label is a pure overlay on the button, cancelled early (via `cancelConfirm()`) the moment she edits any field again, so it never lingers past being true. **Credit mode's existing phase-based flow couldn't use the same decoupling** (its "stay in the loop" target is the client-picker grid, not a cleared inline field — going back to `phase: 'pick'` necessarily unmounts the very button showing the confirmation, so the label and the reset are structurally tied together there, same as before this change) — but doing this pass surfaced a real, latent double-submit bug: `handleAdd` had no guard against being called again while `success` was still true and `name`/`amount` hadn't been cleared yet (they only clear inside the same 900ms timeout), so a fast second tap in that window would have silently recorded the same debt twice, with two distinct idempotency keys (each generated fresh inside `submitCarnetDebt`, so the RPC's own dedup guard couldn't have caught it either). Fixed with a one-line `if (success) return;` guard at the top of `handleAdd`.
- **Session ticker.** A quiet centered line under the Crédit/Vente toggle — plain tally copy, no label or colon ("2 ventes de {total}", "1 crédit de {total}") — with a subtle scale pulse on every add (skipped under reduce-motion; the number itself still updates instantly either way). Lives in `QuickCaptureSheet` itself, not either capture component, specifically so it survives a Crédit↔Vente switch mid-sitting: each mode keeps its own independent count/total (`venteCount`/`venteTotalCents`, `creditCount`/`creditTotalCents`), switching between them just changes which one is displayed, and only actually closing the sheet resets both to zero. Fed by a new `onAdded?: (amountCents: number) => void` prop on both capture components, called once per successful save — the one new piece of plumbing this required, since neither component's own pre-existing session state (Credit's `sessionCount`, kept as-is for its own separate "N crédits noté(s) · Voir →" link) lived anywhere the parent could read it directly.

**Revised the same week: the ticker used to always render, showing a bare "Total de la session : 0 vente · 0 GNF" the instant the sheet opened — direct feedback said it should never show a zero, and should announce itself, not just silently already be there.** Fixed with `{tickerCount > 0 && <SessionTicker ... />}` — the component doesn't exist in the tree at all (no reserved space, no invisible placeholder) until the active mode's count crosses from 0 to 1, at which point it genuinely mounts for the first time and plays a one-time ~250ms fade + slight slide-up (`SessionTicker`'s own `appearAnim`, distinct from the per-entry `pulseAnim`) — never a hard pop. A `mountedRef` inside `SessionTicker` is what tells "this is the component's own first render, play the entrance" apart from "the text just changed while already mounted, play the pulse instead" — the same component handles both cases by checking which one it's currently in, rather than the parent trying to compute "is this literally the very first save of the whole sitting" and thread that timing-sensitive fact down as a prop (a harder problem than it looks: the state update that flips the count and the render where the child mounts happen in the same React batch, so a naively-computed "was it already visible" boolean read at prop-construction time doesn't reliably distinguish the two moments). One accepted, untested-by-design corner case: switching between two modes that *both* already have entries from earlier in the same sitting re-triggers a pulse (not a fresh fade-in, since the component stays mounted the whole time in that specific path) rather than an instant, silent swap — harmless-looking in practice and not part of the feature's own on-device verification steps, so left as the simpler implementation rather than threading extra state through to special-case it.

**Verified against a real Postgres instance before shipping, not just the mocked-Supabase unit suite** (`__tests__/integration/quick-sale.integration.test.ts`, 7 cases): a quick sale creates exactly one `sale_orders` row (`status: 'paye'`), one `so_lines` row against the hidden placeholder, one `payments` row, and zero `stock_moves` rows; repeated quick sales reuse the same placeholder (get-or-create, never create-every-time); the idempotency key prevents a double-record on retry; a zero/negative amount is rejected; a vendeur can only record their own sale (not impersonate another seller); an investisseur is rejected outright (read-only role). All pass against `supabase start`'s real local Postgres, the same discipline this session's earlier `submit_sale`/cross-business fuzz passes established — a new SECURITY DEFINER RPC is not done until a live test has actually exercised the rows it produces, not just read as looking correct.

### Dashboard "Bienvenue" line — first-sale acknowledgment, not a business-age check (`db/migration_v191.sql`, added 2026-09-27)

The hero card's bottom line used to show "Bienvenue" whenever `businesses.created_at` was today — the wrong signal, confirmed by a real screenshot of a test business ("Exemple 5") showing "Bienvenue" directly under "7 ventes aujourd'hui · 35 USD": a business created today but already busy (real activity from hour one, or test data) still read as if nothing had happened yet, and — the flip side of the same bug — once a single calendar day passed, "Bienvenue" silently stopped being possible at all, even for a business that still hadn't made its actual first sale.

**The real signal is lifetime "has this business ever recorded a real sale," not "was it created today."** `get_dashboard_kpis()` (`migration_v191.sql`) gains `first_sale_at` — `MIN(paid_at) WHERE status = 'paye'`, business-wide, `NULL` if none yet. Business-wide matters specifically because this function is `SECURITY DEFINER` (bypasses the caller's own RLS) the same as every other field it already returns — a vendeur correctly sees the business's true first sale even if someone else made it, or it happened before they joined, since the acknowledgment is a fact about the business, not about whoever's currently looking at Accueil. `status = 'paye'` specifically **excludes** `submit_carnet_debt`'s `status = 'credit'` rows on purpose — confirmed by direct product spec ("if her first action was a debt and she has no sales yet, the sales slot stays Bienvenue") — so a business whose only activity so far is a carnet debt still correctly reads "Bienvenue," not a false-positive acknowledgment. No write-side stamping needed anywhere (`submit_sale`/`submit_quick_sale` are both already unmodified) — this is a plain read-only aggregate recomputed on every call, the same posture as every other KPI field, deliberately simpler than a stamped `businesses` column would have been (no idempotent-write guard to get right, no risk of the flag silently diverging from the real ledger).

**Three states, one of them genuinely one-time per business, not a recurring daily state.** `app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s `comparisonText`: no sale ever → "Bienvenue"; `first_sale_at` falls on today (device-local calendar day, `toDateString()` comparison — same technique the old `isBusinessCreatedToday` check already used, so "expires at local midnight" falls out for free) → "Première vente notée ✓"; any day after that → falls through to the pre-existing `Ce mois : {revenue_month}` (evening) / `"Même niveau qu'hier"` / delta-pill logic, completely untouched. Once the first-sale day has passed, "Première vente notée ✓" can never show again for that business — there's no separate "already acknowledged" flag to maintain, since `first_sale_at` staying fixed at its original date is what naturally makes the day-comparison false forever after. The debt card gets no equivalent treatment, deliberately — a debtor's own name already personalizes that card, and this dashboard now has exactly one conditional line, not a growing collection of small celebratory variants (no time-of-day greeting text, no tips, no streaks — an explicit product constraint, not an oversight).

**Verified against a real Postgres instance** (`__tests__/integration/dashboard-first-sale.integration.test.ts`, 6 cases): null with no sales at all; stays null after a credit debt with no real sale; set the instant a real quick sale lands; stays pinned to the *earliest* sale after a later one is added (a `MIN`, not a `MAX`); visible to a vendeur for a sale the admin made (business-wide, not seller-scoped); still set correctly when a debt was recorded before the first real sale. `get_dashboard_kpis` returns `jsonb`, not `RETURNS TABLE` — adding a new key needed no `DROP FUNCTION`/signature dance, just a plain `CREATE OR REPLACE`, unlike the `RETURNS TABLE` column-addition gotcha documented elsewhere in this file (`migration_v175.sql`).

### Carnet ledger rebuild — client detail screen (`app/(app)/clients/[name].tsx`, `src/components/CreditRapideCapture.tsx`, added 2026-09-28)

The customer detail screen's "Historique" used to be day-grouped collapsible sections ("Hier", a "1 crédit · 1000 USD" summary row, a "Voir la transaction" link) — a real UI paradigm, but not the one a shop owner already has in her head: a paper carnet page is a flat, continuous list of lines, each answering "where did we stand here," and days never fold. Rebuilt as exactly that: one row per entry (date · Donné/Reçu + optional sale label · amount · running "Reste"), newest first, no day-grouping, no summary rows, no "Voir la transaction" link — tapping a line itself opens its source.

**`Vente.lines` is never populated by the list fetch — only `loadDetail(saleId)` (`stores/ventes.ts`, used by `ventes/index.tsx`'s own detail modal) fetches `so_lines` with product names.** The old code's `s.lines?.map(...)` had been dead code all along, always falling through to a generic fallback, since `fetchSales()` never touches `so_lines` at all. The rebuild's "Riz, sac de 5kg" second gray line needed a real, dedicated batched fetch (`loadLedgerLines()`, mirroring `loadLedgerPayments`'s exact cache-fallback/`withTimeout` shape) — `so_lines.select('order_id, product_name').in('order_id', creditSaleIds)`, grouped client-side by `order_id`. A credit entry is "bare" (no second line) when every one of its lines is literally named `'Solde reporté'` (`submit_carnet_debt`'s hidden is_system placeholder) — anything else joins its real product names.

**Cash (`'paye'`) sales are excluded from the ledger entirely, not merely hidden — this is a correctness requirement, not a display preference.** `submit_sale()` for a cash sale always creates its own atomic self-payment for the exact same amount at the same instant, so it nets to zero in `totalOwed = totalSold - totalPaid` and was never really a carnet event. Showing it (or its self-payment) as a bare "Reçu" with no matching "Donné" the merchant ever actually wrote would be confusing and wrong for a screen whose whole premise is mirroring paper. Only `status='credit'` sales become "Donné" lines; `ledgerPayments` is filtered to only those whose `order_id` belongs to one of those credit sales before becoming "Reçu" lines — `recordClientPayment` only ever allocates against credit sales anyway, so this filter is really just excluding cash sales' side-effect self-payments, never a real reçu event.

**Running balance ("Reste") is computed in true chronological order by `created_at`, never by the user-editable `date`/`sale_date` field, then reversed for newest-first display.** A payment's `date` can be backdated via `PayModal`; `created_at` never can. Sorting by the immutable timestamp and clamping every entry's running total to `Math.max(0, ...)` (the same clamp `totalOwed` itself already uses) is what guarantees the top (most recent) line's "Reste" is always exactly equal to the header's total — not approximately, not "usually," structurally guaranteed by both values going through the identical clamp.

**"+ Nouveau crédit" is always visible, never gated on `totalOwed > 0`.** A new credit can be the very next thing written on the page regardless of the current balance, including right at a freshly-settled zero — paper has two pen strokes (donné, reçu) and only one of them was previously reachable from this screen. It opens `CreditRapideCapture` pre-scoped to this customer via two new props, `initialClient?: { id?: string; name: string }` and `onDone?: () => void` — skips the client-picker ('pick') phase entirely, seeds `name`/`clientId` from `initialClient`, auto-focuses the amount field on mount, and calls `onDone()` instead of resetting to 'pick' after a successful save (closing the host `FormSheet`).

**A real, pre-existing double-submit bug in `CreditRapideCapture` was found and fixed while adding `initialClient`, unrelated to the new prop itself.** `handleAdd` had no guard against being called again while `success` was still `true` and the form hadn't yet cleared (only cleared inside the same 900ms confirm-window `setTimeout`) — a fast second tap during that window could record the same debt twice, each with its own freshly-generated idempotency key, so the RPC's own dedup guard never saw a repeat. Fixed with `if (success) return;` at the top of `handleAdd`. Also removed the old `queued`-based button-label/color branching (`'⏳ Enregistré hors ligne'`, a background-color override) in favor of a single always-brand-purple `success ? '✓ Ajouté' : 'Ajouter'` — the color override was undocumented, inconsistent with every other quick-capture surface's now-established "brand purple throughout, no color swap" convention, and had no product reason to exist once traced.

### Activation fork flashing for a business that already qualified (`app/(app)/_layout.tsx`, fixed 2026-09-03)

A merchant who had already added a product and recorded a sale still saw `ActivationForkOverlay` ("On enregistre quoi aujourd'hui ?") flash on screen for a split second on every cold start before disappearing — "it's like it's checking," in their words, which is exactly what was happening. `showFork`'s `forkStep2Done`/`forkStep3Done` were computed straight from `useProductStore(s => s.products)`/`useVentesStore(s => s.sales)` — both stores start out as a plain `[]` in fresh JS memory on every app relaunch, and only get replaced with the real fetched arrays once `fetchProducts`/`fetchSales` resolve, asynchronously, sometime after this layout's first render. So for that one render, `forkStep2Done`/`forkStep3Done` read `false` (looks like "no product, no sale yet") even for a business that has both — `forkAgeMs < 24h` was still true (a genuinely new business), so `showFork` briefly evaluated `true` before the real data arrived and corrected it. Same root shape as the variant-stock/`noProductsAtAll` bugs directly above and below this entry: `array.length === 0` can't distinguish "confirmed empty" from "haven't loaded yet," and this codebase has hit that ambiguity more than once.

Fixed by adding `productsFetchedFor`/`salesFetchedFor` (`string | null`, the business id the store last reached a terminal fetch result for — success, cache-fallback, or error; only the interim cache-seed step doesn't count, since the function keeps running past it) to `stores/products.ts`/`stores/ventes.ts`, reset to `null` in each store's `reset()`. `showFork` now also requires `productsFetchedFor === forkBusinessId && salesFetchedFor === forkBusinessId` — fails closed (hidden) until both stores confirm they've actually fetched *this* business's data, same defensive posture as `notifPrimerBlocking` a few lines below it, rather than trusting an array that might just be empty because nothing has loaded yet. This also self-heals across a business switch, not just cold start: switching away sets `forkBusinessId` to the new business's id immediately, which no longer matches the *previous* business's `productsFetchedFor`/`salesFetchedFor` value, so the gate closes again until the new business's own fetch resolves.

### Catalogue post-onboarding empty state (`catalogue.tsx`, added 2026-08-01)

Once the 24h activation-fork window (`showFork` in `app/(app)/_layout.tsx`) closes with the catalogue still completely empty, the fork stops showing anywhere — but `catalogue.tsx`'s own first-run branch used to just render `null` regardless of business age, so a merchant who ignored the fork for 24h landed on a genuinely blank screen (no header count, no visible way to add a product beyond a small corner FAB) with no explanation. `showActivationEmptyState` (`canEdit && !offline && noProductsAtAll && businessAgeMs >= 24h`) now gates a distinct, simplified state: the "N produits" subtitle and the Actifs/Archivés tab chips both hide (nothing to count or switch between), and the product-list area shows a single centered "Ajouter un produit" label above a centered `+` button — replacing the bottom-right FAB entirely rather than duplicating it. Before the 24h mark, or the moment a product exists, behavior is unchanged from before this was added.

**`noProductsAtAll` must check `archivedProducts`, not just `products`, or a business that archived every item gets permanently locked out of its own Archivés tab.** `archivedProducts` was previously only fetched lazily on switching to that tab — so on mount it read its default `[]` regardless of whether real archived rows existed, and a naive `products.length === 0` check alone would have hidden the tab switcher (and the only way to reach it) for any business with zero *active* but some *archived* products, which realistically means every business old enough to have actually used the app, not just brand-new ones. Fixed by also calling `fetchArchivedProducts` eagerly on mount (previously only on tab switch) so the count is reliable before this condition ever evaluates true — if the fetch is still in flight, the condition transiently reads "empty" and self-corrects on the next render once it resolves, the same fail-open-then-self-heal pattern this codebase already uses for variant stock above.

### Activation reminders — first 24h push nudges (`db/migration_v161.sql` + `v162.sql`, `send-activation-reminders`, added 2026-08-01)

Two push notifications, not a drip campaign, aimed at getting someone who left the app mid-onboarding to come back — the actual conversion work is already done by `ActivationForkOverlay` (see above), which forces a choice the instant they're looking at the phone again. A notification's only job in this window is being the reason they look at the phone at all. Sequence and reasoning (research-grounded, not guessed — see session notes): a ~2h "nudge 1" naming one concrete task, a ~20h "nudge 2" with honest urgency framing (the fork genuinely does stop appearing at 24h), mirroring Duolingo's proven "practice reminder" + "streak saver" shape. Both are role-scoped to administrateur/manager only (`target_roles`, same audience `ActivationForkOverlay`'s own `forkIsOwner` already targets) and skip quiet hours (21:00–07:00 UTC ≈ Guinea local, same constant as the Alpha quota-reset reminder and daily digest).

**Only two independent activation signals exist, not three.** `ActivationForkOverlay` offers "Un produit" / "Une vente" / "Une dette", but a "Dette" is just a credit sale under the hood (`submit_carnet_debt` inserts into `sale_orders`) — so `get_and_mark_activation_reminders()` checks exactly `NOT EXISTS(product) AND NOT EXISTS(non-cancelled sale)`, the same two conditions `showFork` itself evaluates client-side.

**Nudge 1's copy deliberately targets the debt action, not the product or quick-sale action.** Many shop owners already track customer debts on a paper carnet notebook, and asking someone to record something they already know, instead of creating new data, is the lowest-friction of the three fork actions. Final copy — "Ah, un client vous doit de l'argent ? Notez-le, ça prend environ 30 secondes" (no trailing period — reads cleaner on a lock screen) — opens with a question rather than a command: "un client vous doit de l'argent ?" is a near-guaranteed internal "oui" for any shop owner who's had the business open more than a day, so the self-recognition happens before the ask, not after it (an earlier draft, "Notez une dette client comme sur votre carnet," led with the instruction and buried the reason to care). The leading "Ah," is a small but deliberate addition — a soft interjection of recognition rather than a cold opener, meant to read as "we already know this happens to you," not a fresh question out of nowhere. Its deep link (`ROUTE_MAP.activation_nudge_1`) skips the wall entirely and goes straight to `/(app)/(tabs)/vendre?mode=credit` — the exact same "Crédit rapide" quick-debt form (`vendre.tsx`, 2 required fields: nom, montant) `ActivationForkOverlay`'s own "Une dette" button opens (`app/(app)/_layout.tsx`'s `onSelectDebt`), not the older, no-longer-wired `onboarding/carnet.tsx` screen. Landing anywhere else would contradict the push's own "ça prend environ 30 secondes" claim by making the merchant choose again first.

**Nudge 2's copy went through several revisions before landing** — worth recording since each revision fixed a real accuracy problem, not just a style preference:
1. First draft said "il vous reste quelques heures pour **activer votre commerce**." Wrong: nothing about the business is actually locked or inactive — Catalogue and Vendre work forever. Only the guided wall itself disappears at 24h.
2. Second draft moved the urgency onto the guide correctly, but still repeated nudge 1's pitch verbatim to someone who had already **not** acted on it — the wrong move for a second touch to the same person.
3. Landed on: open by naming the likely real reason for the non-click (busy, not uninterested) instead of nagging, then a "saviez-vous" framing that reads as a tip rather than a reminder, then the honest guide-ending hook. All three fork actions are named (not narrowed to two) with a single ungimmicked time claim, "quelques instants" — deliberately not a specific number, since "30 secondes" is only actually true for the debt form; "Nouveau produit" (`catalogue.tsx`, 4 fields: nom, prix d'achat, prix de vente, quantité achetée) realistically takes closer to a minute for a first-timer.
Its deep link stays on the wall (`/(app)/(tabs)/`), since it names all three options and the wall is where all three are still offered — unlike nudge_1, which commits to one.

**A live integration test caught a real bug that code review alone would have missed**, the same class this codebase has hit before with cron+RPC pairs (`migration_v149.sql`'s ambiguous-column bug, the `CRON_SECRET`/Vault mismatch under "Alpha WhatsApp reminder"). The first version of `get_and_mark_activation_reminders()` marked both nudges via two data-modifying CTEs in one `WITH` clause, each `UPDATE businesses ... RETURNING`. For a business due for both nudges at once, only nudge_1 ever came back — Postgres's own docs call the result of two sibling WITH sub-statements touching the same row "unpredictable," and in practice the second UPDATE silently didn't take effect for that row. `__tests__/integration/activation-reminders.integration.test.ts`'s "returns both nudge 1 and nudge 2 in the same call" case failed against a real local Postgres instance the first time it ran, with `tsc`/mocked-Supabase unit tests both green. Fixed by computing both due-id arrays up front (`array_agg`) and running the two `UPDATE`s as ordinary sequential statements instead of concurrent CTEs. **Any future RPC that needs to mark more than one independent flag on the same row in one call should default to sequential UPDATEs, not parallel data-modifying CTEs** — the CTE form only reliably works when each sub-statement's target rows are guaranteed disjoint.

### Second-action reminder — the post-24h retention window (`db/migration_v163.sql` + `v164.sql`, `send-second-action-reminders`, added 2026-08-01)

The highest-leverage message in the whole retention sequence, and the first one that isn't part of the 24h activation fork at all: targets a business that took **exactly one** of the three fork actions (product, sale, or debt) and then went quiet. Unlike the never-activated segment, this person has already proven they can use the app — the missing piece is a trigger to come back, not understanding how.
