Fires once, `second_action_nudge_hours` (default 48) after their one real action, capped at `second_action_nudge_max_days` (default 14) — past that, a business whose only action was weeks ago doesn't belong in a "just getting started" message anymore. Both are tunable via `app_config`, same mechanism as the 24h nudges and Alpha's quota limits.

**Classification is sale-first, product-fallback — found by tracing what each real action path actually writes, not assumed.** Originally motivated by one side effect, now true of both sides of the fork: `submit_carnet_debt()` (the "Crédit rapide" quick-debt flow) creates a hidden `is_system` product ("Solde reporté") alongside the credit `sale_orders` row, and — since the quick-sale redesign (`submit_quick_sale()`, `migration_v190.sql`, see "Quick sale redesign" below) — "Vente rapide" now does the identical thing with its own separate placeholder ("Vente rapide") alongside the cash `sale_orders` row. (At the time this reminder was first built, "Vente rapide" instead cloned a **real, visible** product from what was sold — that's what originally motivated the sale-first design below; the redesign changed the *mechanism* but not the *need* for it, since both real actions still write a `products` row.)

So a business whose one real action was "made a sale" would be miscounted as two actions (one product + one sale) if products were counted first. `get_and_mark_second_action_reminders()` checks for a sale first (classifying by that sale's `status`: `credit` → debt, anything else → sale) and only falls back to "product only" when zero sales exist at all — sidestepping the need for any flag to distinguish "real" from "side-effect" products, and `product_count` itself already excludes `is_system` rows, so this classification needed no change at all when the quick-sale mechanism changed underneath it. `__tests__/integration/second-action-reminders.integration.test.ts` exercises both realistic shapes directly (a credit sale + hidden system product; a cash sale + a real, non-system product created around the same time — a pattern ordinary Catalogue/Vendre usage still produces even though "Vente rapide" itself no longer does), not just the simple single-row cases — both passed on the first run, unlike `migration_v161.sql`'s CTE bug above, because the side-effect behavior was traced through the real RPCs before the query was written, not discovered after.

**Copy is keyed on which action they took, not generic** — a generic "add something" message would undercut the whole point of naming this the highest-leverage segment. Product and debt both redirect toward selling (the one core action they haven't tried); sale asks for a repeat, not a redirect, since habit forms from repeating the same action, not from variety:
- Product only: "Vous avez ajouté votre premier produit. Vous pouvez aussi noter vos ventes"
- Debt only: "Vous avez enregistré votre première dette. Vous pouvez aussi noter vos ventes"
- Sale only: "Vous avez enregistré votre première vente. Prêt pour la suivante ?"

Deep link (`ROUTE_MAP.second_action_reminder`) is plain `/(app)/(tabs)/vendre` (no `mode` param, defaults to `'vente'`) for all three variants, since every variant's ask points at selling.

**Still open, not yet built:** whether a business still silent after this one nudge escalates to a one-time WhatsApp message (the signal — one proven real action — would justify the manual Meta-template cost the way it doesn't for the never-activated segment), the calendar-based "still active at Day 7" milestone message's exact copy, the separate, evergreen "was active, then stopped" win-back mechanism (its own detection logic — real activity, then a gap — not bounded to the first week the way this is), and — designed but never actually built, a real gap, not a deliberate cut — the never-activated segment's own third touch past nudge_2 (~72h, "no pressure, door's open" angle, copy already locked: "Toujours pas commencé parce que vous êtes occupé ? Pas de souci — Patron vous aide à gagner du temps.").

### Revenue milestone reminder — achievement, not calendar (`db/migration_v165.sql` + `v166.sql`, `send-revenue-milestone-reminders`, added 2026-08-01)

The "part of something extraordinary" touch — deliberately achievement-based rather than calendar-based. "You've been registered for 30 days" celebrates something that just happened to a merchant; "you've sold 10,000,000 GNF on Patron" celebrates something they actually did. Fires once, ever, the first time a business's real lifetime revenue crosses each threshold in a fixed ladder: 1M, 5M, 10M, 25M, 50M, 100M, 250M, 500M, 1B GNF.

**Ladder spacing widens deliberately, not a flat step.** Early thresholds sit close together (a new, active shop can cross the first one or two within weeks — hooking the habit early); later ones space out, so crossing one stays meaningful once a business is established instead of firing routinely every time revenue ticks up another flat increment. A flat +10M step (the original "1M then 10, 20, 30, 40" idea) would make the 4th and 5th hit feel routine well before a business is actually mature.

**Scoped to GNF only, deliberately, not silently.** Patron supports other currencies (XOF, XAF, ...), but a flat numeric ladder tuned for GNF's denomination would be wrong for a currency with a very different real value per unit — 1,000,000 XOF is roughly 14x the real value of 1,000,000 GNF. Rather than guess at per-currency thresholds with zero real usage data on those currencies yet, `get_and_mark_revenue_milestones()` filters to `currency = 'GNF'` — every other currency is silently excluded from this feature until there's real data to tune against, not an oversight.

**Ratchet, not a log.** `businesses.highest_revenue_milestone_cents` stores only the highest threshold ever notified — a single monotonic value, not a set of "which thresholds fired." If revenue ever dips after crossing one (a cancelled/refunded sale), the ratchet never resets and never re-fires a threshold already passed. If a business jumps past several thresholds between hourly cron runs (a big single sale), only the highest one crossed fires — congratulating on 10M implicitly subsumes 1M and 5M; stacking several congratulation pushes for one moment would just be noise, not celebration. `__tests__/integration/revenue-milestones.integration.test.ts` exercises this multi-threshold-jump case directly, alongside the ratchet-dedupe, discount-netting, credit-counts-as-revenue, and non-GNF-exclusion cases.

**Revenue formula matches `get_reports_snapshot`'s lifetime block exactly** (`total_amount - discount_amount`, `status IN ('paye','credit')`) — never a new, slightly-different formula. This codebase has already been burned by drifting profit/revenue formulas across screens (see the nightly reconciliation notes above); a milestone feature computing its own divergent number would be exactly that mistake again.

**Copy is the one deliberate exception to this codebase's no-emoji push convention.** Final: "🎉 Félicitations. Vous avez franchi {amount} de ventes — soyez fier de vous, patron, vous le méritez." Every other event in `dispatch-notification` is a task-ask, where restraint matters (the calm, Apple/Uber/Tesla register requested for the Day-1 nudges — see above). A milestone is the one message that's pure celebration with nothing asked for — a different psychological register earns different treatment, the same scoped-exception logic as the founder dashboard's health-red color against this app's standing no-red-UI rule. "Franchi" (crossed) over "dépassé" (exceeded) — reads as crossing a real threshold, not just posting a bigger number. The close addresses the merchant directly as "patron" (vocative — "boss," not a description) rather than stating "vous êtes un patron" as a fact about them — a first draft used the descriptive form; this reads more personally, like someone telling them "be proud, boss, you earned it," while keeping the same brand-name pun (patron-the-app / patron-the-title) either way.

**Not yet built:** a parallel transaction-*count* milestone track (50th sale, 100th sale, ...) was raised as a natural extension but deliberately scoped out of this pass — real added scope (a second ratchet column, a second ladder, a second detection branch) for a second, separate achievement dimension, not needed to ship the first one.

### Two sales stores — important distinction

There are **two separate stores** for sales:

| Store | File | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `useSalesStore` | `stores/sales.ts` | Point-of-sale cart: add/remove items, set quantities, bulk pricing toggle, submit checkout (calls `submit_sale` RPC) |
| `useVentesStore` | `stores/ventes.ts` | Sales history: fetch list, detail modal, cancel sale (restores stock), mark credit as paid, update customer name |

Do not conflate them. The POS flow lives entirely in `sales.ts`; post-sale mutations live in `ventes.ts`.

### Notification permission priming (`src/components/NotificationPrimer.tsx`, added 2026-08-24)

`NotificationSetup.tsx` used to call `N.requestPermissionsAsync()` — the raw OS dialog — unconditionally the instant it mounted, which is the very first moment a session exists (`(app)/_layout.tsx` mounts it before even checking whether `activeBusiness` exists, so it fired mid-onboarding, before the user had seen a single screen of real value). Generic system copy, zero context, the worst possible timing — and on iOS this dialog only shows once ever per install; a cold, un-primed denial here permanently forecloses ever asking again except by sending the user to Settings.

Fixed with the standard soft-ask pattern (Facebook/Instagram et al.): `NotificationPrimer` is a custom, branded `AppSheet` shown once, after onboarding actually completes (`activeBusiness` exists, not demo mode) — the first moment there's real context to explain. Only tapping "Activer les notifications" calls the real `requestPermissionsAsync()`; "Plus tard" (or the backdrop, or Android back) just marks the sheet seen (`notif_primer_shown_{userId}` KV flag) without ever touching the OS API, preserving the one real ask for a later session. `NotificationSetup.tsx`'s own mount/foreground-refresh path (`setupAndRegister`) was changed to only ever call the non-invasive `getPermissionsAsync()` — it silently no-ops for someone not yet granted, rather than re-prompting on every foreground return the way it used to.

**Copy went through one real revision, not just wordsmithing — the first draft ("recevez une notification à chaque vente enregistrée") was factually misleading for the majority of this app's real users.** Most real administrateurs are solo sellers (verified against production: 81 of 88 real members with a role, only ~7 are vendeur/manager/investisseur) — for a solo owner, "you'll be notified when a sale happens" is circular: they'd be getting pushed a notification about the exact thing they just tapped through themselves, on their own phone, seconds ago. That's not a real benefit, and it's a bad first impression for someone's very first notification from this app. Tracing this further surfaced a real, separate bug behind the flawed pitch: `sale_completed`'s `notifyEvent()` calls in both `stores/sales.ts` (online path) and `lib/sync.ts`'s `notifyQueuedSaleSynced` (offline-replay path) never passed `excludeUserId` — so a solo admin genuinely did get pushed a notification about their own sale, for every sale, online or offline. Fixed both call sites with `excludeUserId: userId`/`sellerId` (the field already existed on `notifyEvent`'s params and on `dispatch-notification`'s own recipient-resolution logic — nothing new to build, it just wasn't being used here). The primer's final copy leads with two things that are never circular regardless of team size: the daily digest recap (a passive summary, not an echo of an action just taken) and revenue milestones (a threshold the seller isn't necessarily mentally tracking, even though they made every sale that led to it) — **"Ne manquez plus rien" / "Recevez un résumé de votre journée chaque soir, et une alerte le jour où vous franchissez un nouveau cap de ventes."** (the first pass at this line dropped the leading verb and read as a sentence fragment — fixed same session.)

**`canAskAgain` (from `expo-modules-core`'s standard `PermissionResponse` shape) is the load-bearing check that keeps this from looking broken for already-denied users.** Once a real denial has happened (this session's fix, or historically from the old un-primed prompt on an existing install), `requestPermissionsAsync()` resolves immediately with no UI at all — showing "Activer les notifications" to that user would do nothing when tapped, reading as a dead button. `NotificationPrimer` checks `checkNotificationPermission()` first and never shows itself when `canAskAgain` is false (or already `granted`, or the native module isn't linked into this binary yet) — it just marks itself seen and gets out of the way.

**Sequenced ahead of `ActivationForkOverlay`, not just placed alongside it.** Both would otherwise want to show at the exact same instant right after a business is created — the identical "two Modals racing" bug class this codebase has already hit twice (see the `TrialWelcomeOverlay`/fork guard, and the catalogue-form/fork guard via `suppressActivationFork`). `app/(app)/_layout.tsx` holds a `notifPrimerBlocking` flag, defaulted **true** (pessimistic — assume the primer might still show) and only flipped false once `NotificationPrimer` has genuinely resolved (shown-and-dismissed, or determined unnecessary); `showFork`'s own render condition now includes `!notifPrimerBlocking`. The pessimistic default matters: without it, the fork could flash in during the brief async KV/permission check before the primer decides whether to show itself.

### Push notification bodies (`supabase/functions/dispatch-notification/`)

The client stores pass a raw `payload` to `dispatch-notification`, which builds the three-line push (title = business name, optional French subtitle = event category, body = the core fact) in `buildBody`. Conventions that are easy to get wrong and have already bitten:

- **Every monetary figure in a body must be net of discount, and consistent across the sale's lifecycle.** The three sale-related events (`sale_completed`, `sale_cancelled`, `sale_edited`) must all show `total_amount − discount_amount` for the *same* sale, or the same transaction reads back as two different prices. Historically `sale_completed` was fixed to net but `sale_cancelled`/`sale_edited` still showed the catalog gross (`total_amount` alone) — so a sale sold for "45 000" (after a 5 000 discount) came back as "annulée · 50 000". `sale_cancelled` reads the net off the in-memory `Sale` (display units: `total_amount − (discount_amount ?? 0)`); `sale_edited` reads it off the RPC's returned cents (`(total_amount − discount_amount) / 100`). Any new amount-bearing body has the same obligation — reach for the net, never `total_amount` by itself.
- **`sale_completed` reframes product-first when the seller has no name.** `resolveSellerDisplayName` (`src/utils/notifications.ts`) returns `''` — not a `"Vendeur"` placeholder — when a seller has no membership `display_name` and no profile `name`. `buildBody` then switches from "{seller} a vendu {desc} pour {amount}" to "{desc} a/ont été vendu(s) pour {amount}", with the `qty` field (passed by both `stores/sales.ts` and `lib/sync.ts`) driving the singular/plural agreement. Both the online and offline-replay paths must pass `qty` or the plural agreement silently defaults to singular.
- **The business-name title is suppressed per-recipient for single-business members.** `dispatch-notification` fetches each recipient's membership set and omits the `title` (business name) entirely for a recipient who belongs to *this* business and no other — repeating the shop name on every push is noise when they only have one. A multi-business recipient keeps it (disambiguation), and a *non-member* recipient always keeps it — specifically the founder on `support_message`, who is never a member of the merchant's business, so the name is the whole point. Rule: suppress only when `membershipSet.size === 1 && membershipSet.has(business_id)`. This applies to all event types, not just sales.
- **Investisseurs are notified on every `sale_completed`** (added to `targetRoles` in both `stores/sales.ts` and `lib/sync.ts`) to keep them passively in the loop — a busy shop means one push per sale, by design. They are *not* on `sale_cancelled`/`sale_edited` (admin/seller-scoped), only the completed-sale event.

**Tapping a notification never switched active business (fixed 2026-09-04).** `dispatch-notification` always stamped a correct `business_id` into every push's `data` payload (`{ route, event_type, business_id, ...payload }`, `index.ts:535`) — the business the notification is *about* — but `NotificationSetup.tsx`'s tap handler (`handleResponse`) never read it, only `data.route`. For a single-business user this was invisible; for a multi-business one, tapping a notification landed on the right *screen* while every store on it still served whichever business happened to be active before the tap — e.g. the `second_action_reminder` "Vous avez enregistré votre première vente" push for Business B, tapped while Business A was active, opened Vendre still scoped to Business A. Fixed with one new function, `ensureActiveBusiness(businessId)`, called once at the top of `handleResponse` before any of its branches (default tap, expense approve/reject, chat reply) run — it calls `useAuthStore`'s `selectBusiness(businessId)`, the exact same function `BusinessDrawer`'s manual switcher already uses, only when `businessId` is present and differs from `session.activeBusiness.id`. `selectBusiness` already no-ops safely if `businessId` isn't one of the caller's own memberships, which is what makes this safe to call unconditionally for every event type without a founder-only exception: `support_message`'s `business_id` is the merchant's business, not the founder's, so for the founder recipient this silently no-ops (support-inbox isn't business-scoped anyway, so there's nothing to switch into). Also normalized `low_stock`/`price_changed`'s `ROUTE_MAP` entries from the implicit `/(app)/catalogue` to the explicit `/(app)/(tabs)/catalogue` used by every other tabs-screen entry — both resolve identically through the pathless `(tabs)` group, this was a stylistic inconsistency only, not a routing bug.

### Sale editing (`edit_sale()` RPC, `migration_v151.sql`)

Lets admin/manager correct a mistaken `paye`/`credit` sale in place — wrong price, discount, customer, or payment amount — instead of the only prior option, cancel-and-restart. Deliberately **not** an open-ended edit: capped at 2 corrections per sale and a 48-hour window from `created_at` (both read live from `app_config`, same table `migration_v147.sql` built for Alpha's quota — tunable via `set_sale_edit_limit('max_count' | 'window_hours', n)` with no new migration). Vendeur has no path to this at all — `sale_orders`' UPDATE RLS policy has only ever granted admin/manager, so this isn't a new permission, just a finer-grained use of an existing boundary; the fraud vector this closes is a seller quietly editing down their own recorded sale, which structurally can't happen since the seller can't call the RPC.

**Scope is deliberately price/discount/customer/payment only — never quantity or product substitution.** Those two fields never touch `stock_moves`, so reconciliation checks #1/#2/#4 (all keyed on `so_lines.qty` vs `stock_moves`) never see this feature exist. Quantity/product edits would need those checks rewritten to be "modification-aware" the same way they're already "cancellation-aware" for `annule` — real, contained work, just not needed for the actual problem (typos, not wrong quantities), so left undone until proven necessary.

**`total_amount` is always recomputed server-side from `so_lines`, never trusted from the client** — this is what keeps check #9 (total vs. sum of lines) passing after every edit without the check itself needing to change. The one hard money rule the RPC enforces: for a `credit` sale, `SUM(payments)` can never exceed the new owed amount (`total − discount`); for a `paye` sale, it must exactly equal it. An edit that would break either is rejected outright, asking the caller to also adjust the payment in the same call — the same "bundle the money with the mutation" pattern `submit_sale` already uses for cart+payment. This is check #75's condition (credit collected > owed) enforced at write time instead of only caught the next morning.

**Line-price edits apply the same investor profit-share delta math `submit_sale` uses**, or `investor_balance` — an additive ledger credited once at sale time, never recomputed from source — silently drifts permanently wrong the moment a line's `unit_price` changes underneath it. Found by tracing `so_lines.unit_price` through to `investor_balance` before shipping, not by a check catching it after the fact (no check currently verifies the balance is *correct*, only that it isn't negative — check #73).

**Audit trail is a before/after jsonb snapshot per edit call (`sale_order_edits`), not a field-by-field diff table.** One row per `edit_sale()` call holding the whole shape (customer, discount, total, every line's price, every payment's amount) before and after — the UI (`app/(app)/ventes/index.tsx`) diffs the two client-side to show only the fields that actually changed, struck-through old value next to the new one. Avoids a diff-table schema that would need to grow every time a new editable field is added.

**Online-only by design — not queued through the offline `sync_queue`.** The 48h window is checked against a live server clock; a queued edit replayed after a connectivity gap could silently fail that check with no clear signal to the admin (the queue's own retry-then-drop behavior would make it vanish after `MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS`). Same call already made for Alpha's composer, for the same reason — a server-time-validated action needs a live round trip, not a deferred one. This also removes the need for an idempotency key the way `submit_sale` has one: that mechanism exists purely because of offline-queue replay ambiguity, which doesn't apply here.

**Reconciliation gets two additive checks (79-80), nothing existing changes.** #79 (warning) lists every sale edited in the last 90 days for founder visibility, same posture as #78 for capital-injection edits. #80 (critical) is the actual fraud signal — fires only when an edit moved money in the merchant's favor (total down, discount up, or amount paid down); a genuine typo fix is just as likely to go up as down, so a one-directional pattern is the tell, not the edit itself. The 2-edit cap and 48h window are friction, not detection — #80 is what actually tells the founder to go look at a specific sale.

### Purchase orders — cash_on_hand gap (`create_purchase_order()` RPC, `db/migration_v172.sql`, fixed 2026-09-03)

Found via a direct merchant report ("ordering from a supplier doesn't seem to deduct available money"), and it was real: `cash_on_hand` (the `get_reports_snapshot`/`get_period_report` formula behind "Argent disponible") is `payments + capital_injections − expenses − supplier_payments − investor_payouts` — `supplier_payments` is the one table meant to represent real cash actually paid to a supplier. `createCommande`'s old client-side sequence (`stores/fournisseurs.ts`) only ever wrote to `supplier_debts` for the **unpaid** shortfall on a PO (correct — a liability shouldn't touch cash) and never wrote anything at all for the **paid** portion, whether partial or full. Order 500,000 GNF of stock, mark it fully paid at order time, and "Argent disponible" never moved — the money wasn't delayed, it was never recorded anywhere. One of the app's two duplicate "Nouvelle commande" screens (`app/(app)/fournisseurs/index.tsx`) made this worse by having no "Montant payé" field at all, so every order from that screen was silently treated as fully paid with zero cash effect. The old sequence also never checked the debt insert's error, so a failed insert on the shortfall path would have dropped that money from the books entirely, not just delayed it.

**Fix: one atomic RPC, matching `submit_sale`'s "bundle the money with the mutation" pattern**, replacing the old PO-insert → lines-insert → unchecked-debt-insert sequence. `create_purchase_order(p_business_id, p_supplier_id, p_lines, p_amount_paid)` creates the PO + `po_lines`, writes a `supplier_debts` row for any shortfall (unchanged behavior), **and** writes a `supplier_payments` row for whatever was actually paid — the missing half. `p_lines`/`p_amount_paid` are raw display-unit numerics, not cents — `purchase_orders.total_cost`/`po_lines.unit_cost` predate the v24 BIGINT-cents migration and were never converted (still `numeric(15,2)`), while `supplier_debts.amount`/`supplier_payments.amount_cents` (added later, v41/v74) are cents — the RPC does the ×100 conversion internally only where it writes to the cents-denominated tables, so the client-side JS (which already computes totals in display units) needed no unit-conversion changes at all.

**`po_id` (nullable, `ON DELETE SET NULL`) added to both `supplier_debts` and `supplier_payments`** so every debt/payment can trace back to the order that generated it — not used for any new UI in this pass, but it's what makes check 84 possible (see below), and a future "Payé X / Reste Y" display on the PO detail screen would read off it directly.

**Both create-order screens now capture a "Montant payé" amount and warn (non-blocking) if it exceeds current cash_on_hand.** `fournisseurs/index.tsx`'s `CommandeForm` gained the same payment field, owed/paid banners, and styles `fournisseurs/[id].tsx`'s already had (the two screens are still independently-maintained duplicates — see `migration_v159.sql`'s note on the variant_id drift this has already caused once). The overspend check reads `useRapportsStore`'s `yearReport.cash_on_hand`, which is only populated once Rapports has loaded this session — both forms now also fire a best-effort background `fetchYearReport()` call on open so the warning has real data to compare against even if Rapports was never visited; if it's still null, the warning is silently skipped (fail-open, same posture as this codebase's other soft/non-blocking checks) rather than blocking order creation on a fetch.

**Reconciliation gets check 84, additive, not a rewrite of `run_display_checks()`.** Follows the exact pattern `migration_v158.sql`/`v159.sql` established for checks 81-83: a small standalone function (`run_supplier_payment_checks`) inserting into `reconciliation_findings`, called alongside the others from `_shared/reconciliation.ts` — not a `CREATE OR REPLACE` of the ~250-line `run_display_checks()` body, which would risk a transcription error for zero behavior change. Check 84 (critical) flags a PO whose total doesn't match its linked debt+payment sum — but only when at least one linked row exists, since only orders created via the new RPC ever get `po_id` populated; a legacy PO or a genuine consignment/free order has nothing to compare against and is silently skipped rather than flagged as a false mismatch. The "N vérifications OK" clean-run text (`send-reconciliation-report/index.ts`) was already stale at 82 before this fix (checks 81-83 bring the real total to 83) — corrected to 84 alongside this addition.

### Rapports — year view (`get_period_report()` RPC, `db/migration_v154.sql`)

Replaced the old Semaine/Mois/Trimestre three-tab screen (rolling 7/30/90-day windows) with a single unified view: a GitHub/Skool-style Jan–Dec daily heatmap for the selected year, plus a period filter (Jour/Semaine/Trimestre/Semestre/Année/Personnalisé) that only reveals a profit/sales detail panel once a period narrower than the full year is picked. Applies to all four roles, each scoped to what that role has always been allowed to see.

**Calendar-anchored, not rolling — this is the core structural change.** `get_reports_snapshot` (still used by Accueil, untouched) takes `p_period_days` and computes `v_period_start := p_today - p_period_days`, which has no notion of a calendar year boundary. `get_period_report` instead takes explicit `p_period_start`/`p_period_end` dates, so there is no special-cased "year" code path — the year heatmap is just the widest possible range (Jan 1 → today, or → Dec 31 for a past year), and a day/week/trimester/semester/custom filter is the exact same call with a narrower range. `fetchYearReport`/`fetchFilterReport` (`stores/rapports.ts`) both funnel through one shared `loadPeriodReport` helper into two separate state slots (`yearReport` always drives the headline + heatmap coloring; `filterReport` is `null` whenever "Année" is selected, populated only for a narrower filter) — the screen shows a full-screen skeleton only for the year fetch; a filter-panel fetch shows a small inline skeleton instead of re-blanking the whole screen, since narrowing a filter shouldn't feel like a fresh page load.

**Role-scoping mirrors `get_reports_snapshot`'s existing v121 security fix exactly** — role/user_id are derived server-side from `auth.uid()`/`get_role()` for any real session, client-supplied `p_role`/`p_user_id` trusted only for the service-role path. Vendeur gets only personal fields (`my_sales_count`/`my_units_sold`/`my_credit_pending`, own `my_daily` heatmap) — never `cash_on_hand`/`net_profit` — preserving the boundary that a vendeur never sees business-wide financials. `cash_on_hand` and `credit_outstanding` are both all-time live balances (same formulas as `get_reports_snapshot`), shown once in the year-level headline only — the period detail panel never re-shows them, since a balance doesn't have a "for this period" value and repeating it there would misleadingly imply it does.

**The daily-activity subqueries never join `so_lines` onto a query that also sums `so.total_amount`** — that's the exact row-fan-out bug `migration_v114.sql` fixed in `run_display_checks()` (a multi-line order's total counted once per line instead of once per order). Revenue/sales-count and units-sold are computed as two separate subqueries, each grouped by `sale_date`, then joined together on the date — never combined in one query.

**Heatmap rendering (`src/components/ui/YearHeatmap.tsx`) adds `react-native-svg` as this app's first chart/SVG dependency** — every other chart in the codebase (the old bar chart this replaces) was hand-built from `View`s. Because it's a native module, this feature can't ship via a plain `eas update` OTA — per the `runtimeVersion: 'fingerprint'` policy, it needs a real `eas build` for both iOS and Android before it reaches production, same class of constraint as the SMS-retriever module and RevenueCat. Grid logic (Monday-first rows, labeled at rows 0/2/4/6 → "Lun/Mer/Ven/Dim", month labels above the column where that month starts) intentionally mirrors Skool's activity graph layout; the coloring does not — it's 3 flat levels off the app's own `palette.success` token (`palette.border` for no activity, `success` at ~30% and 100% alpha) rather than a copy of any reference palette, per the "never hardcode hex, always use theme tokens" rule.

**Shipping is capitalised into landed cost, so `transport_achat` expenses are excluded from profit — the category *value* is load-bearing, not a mere label.** `get_period_report`/`get_reports_snapshot` sum operating expenses with `category <> 'transport_achat'` (see `migration_v154.sql` ~line 146), because freight is already baked into each product's `so_lines.cost_price_at_sale` via `receive_purchase_order`'s AVCO shipping-allocation (`migration_v101.sql`). Counting the `transport_achat` expense rows in profit *too* would double-subtract shipping (revenue − COGS − expenses, with freight in both COGS and expenses) — on a freight-heavy import business (e.g. "Maillot", whose 17.5M GNF of freight sits inside a ~54k/piece landed cost) that can flip a real profit negative and read to the merchant as "the business is in debt." Freight is still recorded as an expense so it correctly reduces **cash on hand** (the money did leave); it's only kept out of the **profit** sum. The Dépenses screen renders these rows with the display label **"Transport"** (was "Fret", changed 2026-07-25) and a clean `"Nᵉ convoi · N maillots"` description, but the stored `category` string must stay exactly `transport_achat` — both the profit exclusion and `app/(app)/fournisseurs/index.tsx`'s shipping-history query key on that literal. Display label ≠ category value here, deliberately: renaming the value to match the new label silently reintroduces the double-count. (New PO receipts still auto-generate the old verbose `"Frais de transport — {supplier} ({date})"` description from `receive_purchase_order`, `migration_v101.sql` ~line 190 — a backend template not yet simplified to match.)

**Redesigned 2026-09-02, from a from-first-principles critique against Robinhood/Revolut/Cash App conventions (no code changed until explicitly approved).** The original headline gave "Argent disponible" and "Bénéfice cumulé" identical visual weight (same `StatCard`, same 16px value) alongside the always-visible Ventes/Produits vendus row — 4 cards, no hierarchy, no answer to "how am I actually doing." Fixed:
- **Profit hero.** "Bénéfice cumulé {year}" is now its own full-width `Card elevated` (32px amount, success/warning-colored per sign) — the one number this screen is "about." "Argent disponible" demoted to a plain secondary `StatCard`, same tier as Ventes/Produits vendus. Mirrors the dashboard's existing revenue-hero convention (caption → big amount → bottom comparison row divided by a hairline) so the app has one "hero number" pattern, not two different ones.
- **Real vs-last-year delta**, not just a bigger number. `stores/rapports.ts` gained a third `loadPeriodReport` slot (`previousYearReport`/`fetchPreviousYearReport`, alongside the existing `year`/`filter` slots) — a full calendar-year `get_period_report` call for `year - 1`. Kept as its own state slot rather than reusing `yearReport`, since both the current year's headline and its comparison baseline need to be on screen at the same time. The delta renders via the same solid `Pill` (`arrow-up`/`arrow-down`, success/warning tone) the dashboard's revenue delta already uses — one shared "real signal, loud color" pattern, not a screen-specific one-off. Only rendered once `previousYearReport` has actually loaded and is non-null (never implied from an absent/zero baseline), and only when `year - 1` is a genuine past year the business existed in (`hasPreviousYear = year - 1 >= creationYear`) — a business in its first calendar year shows "Première année d'activité" instead of a nonsensical delta off zero. Vendeur never fetches this at all (the profit hero itself is `!isVendeur`-gated, same as before).
- **Year selector** got a bordered pill background (`borderRadius: radius.full`, `borderColor: p.border`) — the bare `‹ 2026 ›` text read as page furniture, not a control, despite already being fully tappable.
- **Period filter chips' active state was already correct** — `periodActive`/`periodLabelActive` (solid dark fill, inverse text) already exist and already read as unambiguous when a chip is selected. The screenshot that prompted this review simply had no filter selected (a valid state — no filter = year view), so there was nothing to fix here; flagged during the critique, verified against the code, left untouched rather than "fixed" for a problem that wasn't real.
- **`YearHeatmap` deliberately untouched** — it's a different data shape (a full-year daily grid) with no equivalent in any of the three reference apps, not a case of "they do it differently and better."

### Store pattern (Zustand, `stores/`)

All domain stores use Zustand with direct Supabase calls — no local write-through cache. Stores hold arrays of fetched records and expose `loading`/`saving` flags. Call each store's `reset()` on logout (handled in `useAuthStore.logout()`).

Stores: `auth`, `chat`, `clients`, `equipe`, `expenses`, `fournisseurs`, `market`, `products`, `rapports`, `sales` (POS), `sync`, `toast`, `ventes` (history).

`useRapportsStore` (`stores/rapports.ts`) — fetches the last 180 days of payments + COGS from `so_lines` for the reports screen. Called from the dashboard on every focus via `fetchPaymentsAndCogs(businessId)`.

**Vendeur cost_price gate (`stores/products.ts`, `migration_v185.sql`, added 2026-09-27) — a real illustration of why RLS is row-level, not column-level.** A vendeur could read `products.cost_price`/`product_variants.cost_price` directly — the base SELECT policy was a blanket `is_member(business_id)`, and Postgres RLS can't hide one column from one role while allowing the rest of the same row: a view that simply omits a column doesn't stop a direct query against the underlying table for that column, since RLS still says "yes, you may read this row." The only way to actually deny it is to change the base table's own SELECT policy to exclude the role, then give that role an alternate path for the columns they're still allowed to see. Fixed by: (1) narrowing the base `products`/`product_variants` SELECT policies to `is_member(business_id) AND get_role(business_id) <> 'vendeur'`; (2) two new `SECURITY DEFINER` RPCs, `get_products_for_vendeur()`/`get_variants_for_vendeur()`, whose own SQL body hardcodes `cost_price` to `0::bigint` — structurally incapable of leaking the real value, not just configured not to ask for it; (3) `fetchProducts()`/`fetchVariants()` branch on `role === 'vendeur'` to call the RPC instead of the direct table query. `fetchVariants()` reads role from `useAuthStore.getState()` directly rather than taking a new parameter, since it has 6 call sites and none of them needed to change. **`investisseur` is deliberately untouched** — `migration_v66.sql` already gives investisseur their own separate, scoped SELECT policy specifically so they can verify their own profit-share math on products they hold a stake in; that's a different, already-intentional feature, not the gap this closes. Doesn't touch cost_price's storage or any of the ~50 SQL functions that read/write it (`submit_sale`, `edit_sale`, `receive_purchase_order`, reconciliation, ...) — all `SECURITY DEFINER`, so they bypass the caller's RLS entirely and are unaffected by narrowing the base policy. Verified this specific point directly: the full integration suite (106 tests, including every sale/PO flow that touches these tables) passes unchanged after the RLS policy edit.

**Real gotcha hit while building this, worth remembering for any future `RETURNS TABLE` function**: Postgres requires the declared return column types to match the underlying column types *exactly*, not just compatibly. `cost_price`/`sale_price`/`bulk_price` are `bigint` (v24's cents-conversion), but the first draft declared them `numeric` in `RETURNS TABLE` — every call failed with `42804: structure of query does not match function result type`, caught immediately by the integration test (not by `tsc`, which has no visibility into actual Postgres column types). `stock_qty`/`reorder_level` genuinely are `numeric` (quantities, never converted by v24) — the fix isn't "make everything numeric" or "make everything bigint," it's matching each column to what `\d products` actually says.

**Invite-code redemption history (`stores/equipe.ts`, `migration_v186.sql`, added 2026-09-27) — a real pre-existing behavior had to change for this feature to be possible at all.** `fetchCodes()` used to delete any consumed code (`uses >= max_uses`) the moment the admin opened the "Codes d'invitation" screen — found while building 1.7's redeemed-by/when tracking, since a redemption stamped onto a row that gets deleted on the very next screen view would never actually be visible to anyone. Fixed by changing the cleanup condition to only delete codes that expired *without* ever being used (`isExpired && !isConsumed`) — a consumed code is now kept indefinitely and surfaced via the new `redeemedCodes` state (redeemer name resolved client-side via the same profiles-lookup-map pattern `fetchMembres` already uses), rendered as a "Codes utilisés" history section below the active-codes list. No retention/pruning added for old consumed rows — not a real concern at this app's current scale, easy to add later if `invite_codes` ever grows large.

`useToastStore` (`stores/toast.ts`) — global in-app toast notifications. Use the `toast` helper (not the store directly): `toast.success(msg)`, `toast.warning(msg)`, `toast.info(msg)`. Mount `<AppToastContainer />` once at the app root; it renders the animated banner automatically.

### Chat system (`stores/chat.ts`)

Dual-mode chat with two room types:

| Room | Key | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Boutique | `boutiqueRoom` | Private per-business room; members only |
| Le Marché | `globalRoom` | Global room (`GLOBAL_ROOM_ID` constant), all users |

`load(businessId, currentUserId)` fetches both rooms and recent messages, then computes `boutiqueUnread` and `marcheUnread`. The boutique last-read timestamp is stored in the `chat_room_reads` DB table (REPLICA IDENTITY FULL for real-time); the marché last-read is stored locally via `getKV(MARCHE_KEY)`.

**Business-switching bug (known):** The guard in `(app)/_layout.tsx` is `if (!bId || !uId || boutiqueRoom !== null) return;`. When the user switches businesses without logging out, `boutiqueRoom` still holds the old value and `loadChat` is never called for the new business — the unread count stays stale. Only a full logout+login resets it cleanly.

`appendMessage` is called from the real-time subscription to push new messages without a full re-fetch.

### Support chat — merchant ↔ founder (`stores/supportChat.ts`, `db/migration_v126.sql`)

A separate system from the "Chat system" above, not an extension of it — deliberately its own tables (`support_conversations`, `support_messages`, `support_ai_drafts`) rather than reusing `chat_rooms`/`chat_messages`, because that schema's RLS (`is_global=true OR is_member(business_id)`) has no concept of "the founder reads across every business," and retrofitting it risked weakening the boutique/marché isolation. Replaces the former `wa.me` WhatsApp link that used to be the Accueil "Support" quick-action button (`app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx`) — the equivalent link in Paramètres → "Contacter le support" is untouched, left as a fallback.

**Human-in-the-loop only.** An AI (Groq, `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` — free tier, open-weight; picked over a paid model because the founder reviews every draft before anything is sent, so a 70B-class open model is an acceptable quality tradeoff at zero marginal cost) drafts a suggested reply after every merchant message, but it is architecturally incapable of reaching a merchant directly:
- The draft lives in its own table, `support_ai_drafts`, whose *only* RLS policy is `USING (is_founder())` — no merchant-visible policy exists on that table at all. This makes founder-only visibility a schema-level guarantee, not something a screen has to remember to filter.
- The only INSERT path for a founder-authored `support_messages` row is `send_founder_support_reply()`, a SECURITY DEFINER RPC gated on `is_founder()` and a real `auth.uid()` — the `generate-support-draft` edge function's service-role key never writes to `support_messages`, only to `support_ai_drafts`.

**Founder identity is a global `profiles.phone` match** (`is_founder()`, `get_founder_id()` in `db/migration_v126.sql`), not a per-business role — Sebastiao is also an `administrateur` of his own business ("Maillot Commerce"), so the check has to be independent of any `memberships` row. Client-side gating (`src/utils/founder.ts`'s `isFounderPhone`) mirrors the same phone-digit comparison for the founder-only screens (`app/(app)/support-inbox/`) — defense-in-depth only, same posture as the investisseur tab-hiding pattern; RLS is the real enforcement, verified directly against a local Postgres instance before any client code was written (a merchant session confirmed to get zero rows from `support_ai_drafts`, a founder session confirmed to see every business's conversations).

**One open conversation per business**, not a multi-ticket system — mirrors the existing one-boutique-room-per-business precedent. A `status` (`open`/`closed`) lets the founder close a resolved thread from the inbox; a new merchant message reopens it via `send_support_message()` rather than creating a new row (enforced by a partial unique index on `business_id WHERE status='open'`, so a race between two concurrent get-or-create calls can't create two open rows).

**Offline queueing is deliberately not the SQLite `sync_queue`** used for `submit_sale`/`create_expense` — support messages aren't financial data, so a lighter KV-backed queue (`getKV`/`setKV`, key `support_pending_messages`) is enough, drained via `drainSupportQueue()` from the same `AppState` foreground listener in `app/(app)/_layout.tsx` that already drains the real sync queue.

Requires the `GROQ_API_KEY` Supabase Edge Function secret to be set before `generate-support-draft` will produce real drafts — without it the function persists a `status='failed'` draft row (founder inbox shows "La suggestion n'a pas pu être générée") rather than blocking the merchant's message send, which always succeeds independently of AI draft generation.

**Founder inbox was silently scoped to only the founder's own business (fixed 2026-07-14).** `migration_v126.sql`'s whole point for the additive `businesses` SELECT policy was "the founder inbox lists conversations across every business" — but `loadBusinessConversations` (`stores/supportChat.ts`) and both call sites (`app/(app)/support-inbox/index.tsx`, the Accueil prefetch in `app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx`) filtered `support_conversations` by `session.activeBusiness.id`, with a comment explicitly defending this as intentional ("a founder must never see another business's threads"). That directly contradicted the RLS design: `is_founder()` already grants cross-business SELECT on `support_conversations`/`support_messages`/`businesses` specifically so the inbox can show every merchant's questions in one list. Net effect: a message from any business other than the founder's own ("Maillot Commerce") never appeared in the inbox — not a demo-account-specific issue, it would happen for any real merchant too. Fixed by replacing it with `loadFounderConversations()` (no business filter, `businesses(name)` fetched via FK embed) and dropping the `businessId` gating from both callers.

### Founder Dashboard (`src/components/FounderDashboard.tsx`, `db/migration_v174.sql`)

A founder-only growth panel, reached from `BusinessDrawer`'s "KPI" footer row (`app/(app)/founder-kpi`). Gated by `isFounderPhone(session?.user.phone)` (`src/utils/founder.ts`), the same client-side mirror of `is_founder()` already used to show "Service client" (the founder's cross-business support inbox) instead of the regular member's "Support" row in this drawer's footer — this is a second, independent founder-only surface in the same component, not a variant of the support-inbox one.

**Fully replaced 2026-09-04, on direct product request** — the original 4-card "First Principles" panel described in this section (D7 active retention, daily active transacting rate, TTFR, average transactions per active user — `src/utils/founderMetrics.ts`, `get_founder_activity_raw()`, `migration_v156.sql`/`v167.sql`) was swapped end-to-end for a business-level Activation/Retention/North-Star framework (see "Current implementation" below). `founderMetrics.ts` is deleted. `get_founder_activity_raw()` is left deployed — still a real, valid, callable function — it's just no longer called from anywhere in the app. The debugging history in this section below (contaminated population, single-calendar-day D7 cohort) documents real bugs already found and fixed in *that* RPC; it's kept as a record for whoever might reuse that function or hit the same bug shape elsewhere, not because the screen still shows those particular numbers.

**Deliberately split into a pure calculation module and a data-fetching component.** `founderMetrics.ts` has zero Supabase/network dependency — every function takes a plain `UserActivityRecord[]` (`{ userId, signupAt, transactionTimestamps[] }`) and returns a number or a `'green'|'yellow'|'red'` status from a fixed threshold, so the metric logic is independently testable and reusable regardless of where the records come from. `FounderDashboard.tsx` is the only piece that knows about `get_founder_activity_raw()` — it fetches, maps the RPC's rows onto `UserActivityRecord[]`, and calls `buildFounderKpis()`. Refetched every time the drawer opens (`active` prop, not a poll timer), so the numbers read as "right now."

`get_founder_activity_raw()` keys transactions on `seller_id`, not `created_by` — TTFR and the daily-active read are meant to measure a specific person's own activity (`submit_sale` already enforces `seller_id = auth.uid()`), not whichever session happened to submit the row. It re-checks `is_founder()` server-side before returning anything (same `RAISE EXCEPTION 'Accès refusé'` pattern every other founder-gated RPC uses) — the client-side check only avoids showing the panel to everyone else, it isn't the real gate. It's also deliberately unwindowed (every user, every sale) rather than scoped to "recent" — D7 retention's cohort match and the daily-active-rate's "total users" denominator both need the true full population; a windowed fetch would silently understate both. Revisit only if this stops being cheap at scale.

**The RED health color is a deliberate, scoped exception to this app's standing "no red in the UI" rule.** That rule (see feedback memory) is about merchant-facing screens — the founder doesn't want to hand a shopkeeper an alarming red signal. This dashboard is founder-only and is *only* useful because red means red: a traffic-light health check where "critical" gets diluted into amber stops functioning as a health check. `src/theme/colors.ts` gives it its own `colors.health.{green,yellow,red}` / `palette.health{Green,Yellow,Red}` tokens — kept separate from the shared `success`/`warning`/`danger` tokens (and at slightly different hex shades, matching the exact values requested for this feature) specifically so a merchant-facing screen can never reach for `healthRed` by habit.

**Numbers looked broken but the `founderMetrics.ts` formulas were never the bug — the input population was contaminated (found 2026-08-24, fixed by `migration_v167.sql`).** `get_founder_activity_raw()` originally selected every row in `profiles` with no filter at all. Two things create real, permanent `profiles` rows that are not real users: (1) every phone-auth attempt, login or register, calls `signInAnonymously()` and upserts a blank-phone `profiles` row *before* OTP succeeds — an abandoned attempt leaves that row behind forever, and even a *successful* login leaves one behind, since login discards the throwaway anonymous session and swaps the user into their real, pre-existing account via magic link; (2) every demo-mode "Essayer" tap is the same anonymous-session pattern, except `seed-demo-business` additionally seeds 12 fully-fake `sale_orders` under that anonymous user's own `seller_id` — including exactly 3 dated "today" and one at exactly "7 days ago" — with nothing to distinguish them from a real sale. Net effect: "Actifs transactionnels (jour)" read ~0.3% (denominator inflated by dead anonymous rows that will never transact), "Rétention D7" frequently showed "—" (the exact-calendar-day cohort is often genuinely empty at this volume), and "Ventes / utilisateur actif" landed suspiciously exactly on **3.0** — the literal constant `seed-demo-business` hardcodes for "today's sales," strong evidence that day's "active users" were mostly demo clicks, not merchants. Fixed with `WHERE p.phone IS NOT NULL AND p.phone <> ''` — the same "real user" filter this codebase already uses elsewhere (`migration_v143.sql`/`v145.sql`/`v149.sql`'s Alpha WhatsApp targeting), since `phone` is only ever set by `upgradePhone()` on a real conversion, never by either contamination source. Doesn't clean up the underlying `sale_orders`/`products`/etc. rows demo businesses and abandoned accounts leave behind elsewhere — only fixes what this one RPC counts. Also fixed alongside: `formatTTFR()` never rolled up past minutes, so a genuinely large average (e.g. ~2.8 days) rendered as a nonsensical-looking `"3985min 3s"` instead of `"2j 18h"` — the number was mathematically correct, just unreadable.

**Second, separate fix the same day: `calculateD7Retention()`'s single-calendar-day cohort was unusable at this app's real signup volume.** After the `migration_v167.sql` fix above, "Rétention D7" *still* showed "—" even though a direct query confirmed 99 real (phone-verified) users were already older than 7 days. The original formula only ever evaluated the cohort that signed up on the *exact* calendar day 7 days before "now" (the textbook single-day Day-N definition) — verified live: 0 real users signed up on that one specific day, because signups here are sparse (0-2 on most days, with one real cluster on 2026-07-26–28). With that few people, a single-day cohort is empty on most days regardless of how many total users have crossed day 7. Fixed by rolling the cohort forward: `calculateD7Retention()` now takes a `windowDays` param (default **90**, not a tighter "last month") and evaluates everyone whose *own* day-7 (signup + 7 days, not a shared calendar date) falls within the last `windowDays` days, checked against their own day-7 date. 90 was picked deliberately generous, not arbitrary: this app's oldest real user is currently ~89 days old, so a 90-day window covers effectively every eligible user today (verified against production: 99/99, identical to true all-time) — full accuracy while the platform is this young, and it'll naturally tighten into a genuine "recent" read on its own once the app has more than ~90 days of history, with no further code change needed. Verified directly against production: **99 eligible, 0 retained → 0.0%** — an honest, computable number instead of a misleading "no data" placeholder that was actually hiding a real, concerning 0% retention rate.

**The real, underlying activation shape (traced straight from production Postgres, not PostHog — see the gap below), for whoever picks this up next.** Of 101 real (phone-verified) users: 19 never finish onboarding at all (verify their phone, then drop at the very last step — creating/joining a business); of the 81 who become a shop owner, only 23 (28%) ever record one sale, and 58 (72%) never do. But of the 23 who do convert: 61% convert within 24h, 87% within 7 days, and only 3 people in this app's *entire history* have ever converted after day 7 — conversion is a first-72-hours phenomenon, essentially decided immediately, not a slow trickle. The reassuring counterpart: 74% of anyone who ever sells something sells again — this is not a retention/habit problem once someone starts, it's entirely a getting-someone-to-their-first-sale problem, concentrated in a window the current activation-nudge cadence (2h/20h, see "Activation reminders" above) only partially covers, since most conversion is already decided before the 20h nudge fires.

**Current implementation: `growth_metrics` view + `get_founder_growth_stats()` (`db/migration_v174.sql`).** A business-level cohort, not a user-level one — matches how the founder actually thinks about growth here ("how many shops are alive") and matches the North Star metric directly. 4 numbers, one RPC call:

1. **Taux d'activation (30j)** — of businesses created in the last 30 days, % that hit `activated_72h`: a product OR a non-cancelled sale within 72h of signup — the same two-signal definition `get_and_mark_activation_reminders()` (see "Activation reminders" above) already uses for the 24h fork-reminder window, just measured against 72h instead.
2. **Rétention Semaine 1** / 3. **Rétention Semaine 4** — of *activated* businesses only, % with a real (`paye`/`credit`) sale during the 7-day window starting exactly 1 (or 4) week(s) after signup. Week-wide, not single-day, from the start this time — the old framework's D7 cohort (below) had to be fixed live after shipping single-day and finding it was almost always empty at this app's real signup volume; this one was designed against that lesson up front.
4. **Commerces actifs / semaine (North Star)** — distinct businesses with a real sale in the last 7 days. Not scoped to the `growth_metrics` cohort at all — a shop transacting this week counts regardless of when it signed up.

**Three-state flags, not booleans defaulting false.** `activated_72h`/`week1_retained`/`week4_retained` are `NULL` while their window is still open, and only resolve to `TRUE`/`FALSE` once it's fully elapsed — a plain boolean defaulting `false` would silently understate every rate for the platform's most recent signups, exactly the premature-false shape `calculateD7Retention()` hit before its windowing fix below.

**Demo/anonymous businesses excluded the same way `migration_v167.sql` already established for the old RPC** — `growth_metrics` joins each business to its creator (`businesses.created_by`) and filters that profile's `phone IS NOT NULL AND phone <> ''`, since `seed-demo-business` seeds fake sales at exactly the date offsets (today, 7 days ago) that would otherwise fabricate both activation and week1 retention for every demo click.

**`growth_metrics` has no client-facing SELECT grant at all** (`REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated`) — reachable only from inside `get_founder_growth_stats()`'s `SECURITY DEFINER` body, same posture as `djomi_pending_payments`/`support_ai_drafts`. It's still a real, named view rather than inlined into the function, so the founder can run the same shape of query directly in the Supabase SQL editor (as `postgres`, which bypasses RLS/grants) to spot-check the app's numbers independently of what the screen shows.

**Card health colors + one-sentence "what's the constraint" callout (`src/utils/growthConstraint.ts`, `db/migration_v175.sql`, added 2026-09-05).** `get_founder_growth_stats()` gained a 5th column, `total_real_businesses` (`count(*) FROM growth_metrics`) — required a `DROP FUNCTION` first since adding a return column changes the function's return type, which `CREATE OR REPLACE` alone can't do (same requirement already documented under `migration_v132.sql`).

- **Benchmarks are cited external figures where one exists, not invented round numbers**: activation (green ≥40%, red <20%) is grounded in "top-quartile B2B SaaS reaches 40%+ activation within 7 days, median SaaS is ~17%"; Week 1 retention (green ≥60%, red <30%) uses "60%+ for business applications" specifically (Patron is one, not a consumer app, which the same source benchmarks lower at 40%+); Week 4/Month 1 retention (green ≥40%, red <20%) is rounded down from the ~48% PLG-average figure, since a single fresh cohort's week-4 number is naturally lower than a mature product's blended average. The North Star's benchmark (green ≥25%, red <10%) has no external citation — there's no standard named metric for "weekly transacting shops ÷ every real business ever created" — and is reasoned directly from the other three instead.
- **The North Star card is benchmarked as a rate, not its displayed raw count.** `weekly_transacting_shops / total_real_businesses` is computed client-side purely to pick a health color; the card still displays the raw count unchanged. A fixed absolute-count bar would need manual re-tuning every time the platform's total business count grows — a rate doesn't.
- **The constraint sentence walks the funnel in causal order (activation → Week 1 → Week 4), not by picking whichever stage has the numerically worst ratio.** `identifyConstraint()` names the *first* stage below its own green benchmark. A later stage can look worse in isolation purely because it inherits an upstream shortfall (Week 4-retained businesses are a subset of Week 1-retained ones) — naming Week 4 as "the constraint" while Week 1 is also unhealthy would point at a stage nobody can actually intervene on directly yet. The North Star is deliberately excluded from this comparison — it's the outcome the other three drive, not a lever of its own; naming it "the constraint" would just restate the symptom.
- **First real run (2026-09-05) surfaced a genuine, non-obvious finding, not a hypothetical**: activation is healthy (64.3%, comfortably green) but Week 1 retention was 4.65% and Week 4 was 2.33% — both deeply red. Confirms this app's actual growth problem right now is retention of already-activated shops, not acquisition or onboarding — shops that try Patron mostly don't come back, even though getting them to try it in the first place is working fine. This is the opposite of what the *original* founder dashboard's activation-funnel breakdown (see above) might suggest in isolation, and only surfaced because this feature explicitly separates activation from retention instead of blending them.
- Thresholds live as plain TS constants in `growthConstraint.ts`, not `app_config` — unlike Alpha's quota limits, these are purely client-side display/coloring logic with nothing to enforce server-side, so a DB-tunable table would be unnecessary machinery. Changing a benchmark is a one-line edit + OTA, same cost as any other JS-only change.

### PostHog analytics — silently disabled on every real native build until 2026-08-24 (`lib/posthog.ts`)

`EXPO_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY`/`EXPO_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST` existed only in the local, gitignored `.env` file — **never in EAS's `production`, `preview`, or `development` environment secrets**, confirmed via `eas env:list`. `lib/posthog.ts` sets `disabled: !process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY`, so every real `eas build` (the App Store/Play Store binaries, built on EAS's cloud infra with no access to a gitignored local file) shipped with PostHog **fully disabled** — not degraded, off. Verified directly: PostHog showed only 6 `user_signed_up` events since 2026-07-13 against **47** real signups in Postgres over the same window (13% capture), and only 27 of 46 real `sale_submitted` events in the last 30 days (59% capture) — a real, systemic gap, worse for signup-moment events than established-session events, not just sampling noise. `eas update` (OTA) is a *separate* mechanism that bundles locally and does pick up the local `.env` automatically — so any device that received an OTA push from a machine with a working local `.env` (this repo's own dev machine) had working analytics despite the native binary being misconfigured. That inconsistency is exactly why capture rates varied by event/period instead of being uniformly zero.

**Practical consequence: don't trust any pre-2026-08-24 PostHog number for this app as representative — cross-check against Postgres directly (`supabase db query "..." --linked`) before treating a PostHog funnel/count as ground truth.** The Founder Dashboard fixes above were only trustworthy because they were verified against Postgres directly, not derived from PostHog.

**Fixed by adding both vars to all three EAS environments** (`eas env:create --name EXPO_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_KEY --value <key> --environment production preview development`, same for `_HOST`) — takes effect on the **next native `eas build`**; already-installed binaries need a fresh build + store update to start reporting, OTA alone can't fix a native-build-time env gap (same class of constraint as every other `EXPO_PUBLIC_*`-at-build-time issue in this file). **`EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_API_KEY_ANDROID` had the identical gap** — present in the local `.env`, absent from all three EAS environments — meaning RevenueCat purchases have likely never worked on any real Android production build (`lib/purchases.ts`'s `ANDROID_API_KEY` resolved to `''`, same `disabled` fail-safe as the iOS key's own documented history under "Native IAP paywall" above). Confirmed zero risk to fix immediately despite touching payments config, since the missing key already meant every Android purchase call was a no-op — there was nothing live to break. Added to all three EAS environments the same way; also only takes effect on the next native Android build. Djomi (Orange Money) remains the actual live subscription path for this app's real userbase regardless.

**Any future `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` var added to this project must be set in EAS env (`eas env:create ... --environment production preview development`), not just the local `.env` — a local-only var is invisible to every real `eas build` from day one, silently.** This is a stronger version of the already-documented "package.json devDependency can shift the fingerprint" and "confirm EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_API_KEY_IOS is actually set in EAS, don't assume" lessons elsewhere in this file — check `eas env:list <environment>` before assuming any `EXPO_PUBLIC_*` var is live in production, every time, not just once.

### Image messages (`lib/chatImages.ts`, `db/migration_v132.sql`)

Images in boutique chat (incl. partner DM rooms, which share `chat_messages` via `room_id`), and merchant↔support chat. **Le Marché is deliberately excluded** — it's a public forum across every business on the platform, not a bounded-audience surface like the other two, and images there would be unmoderated public image hosting; revisit only as its own scoped decision.

Reuses the `message_type` pattern voice messages established in v90/v91/v92 (`'text' | 'voice' | 'image'` on `chat_messages`; `support_messages` gained the same three columns fresh in v132, since it never had voice). One shared public bucket, `message-images`, path-namespaced `{context}/{room_or_conversation_id}/{message_id}.jpg` — not a bucket per surface — because the RLS policy is identical across both (any authenticated user upload/read, access actually gated at the message-row level, same posture as `voice-messages`).

`lib/chatImages.ts`'s `uploadMessageImage()` is the one shared upload path for both surfaces: resizes to a 1600px longest edge via `expo-image-manipulator` (only shrinks, never upscales — the low-bandwidth constraint that's the whole reason this app exists), compresses to JPEG ~0.7, then reads/uploads via the same base64-decode workaround voice messages use (`fetch().blob()` returns 0-byte blobs for `file://` URIs in Hermes). `image_width`/`image_height` are captured at upload time and stored alongside `image_url` so `ImageMessageBubble` can reserve its final layout size before the image loads — no reflow jank.

**`CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` does not edit a function in place when the argument *count* changes**, even if every new parameter is `DEFAULT`-ed — Postgres creates a second overload and leaves the old signature callable alongside it. v132 added `p_image_url`/`p_image_width`/`p_image_height` to `send_support_message`/`send_founder_support_reply`, and applying it left both the old 2-arg/3-arg and new 5-arg/6-arg versions live simultaneously — ambiguous for PostgREST's named-argument RPC calls whenever a caller supplies only the original params (exactly what the plain-text send path does). Fixed with an explicit `DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS <old signature>` before each `CREATE OR REPLACE` in v132.sql. Any future migration that adds parameters to an existing SECURITY DEFINER RPC needs the same explicit drop — `CREATE OR REPLACE` alone is not enough once the parameter count changes.

**Support-chat images are not offline-queued**, unlike text messages (which use the lightweight KV queue, `support_pending_messages`) — a network failure just surfaces an error and the user retries manually once reconnected. Re-uploading a multi-hundred-KB file through that queue on reconnect wasn't worth building for v1; if it's ever needed, it belongs in the real SQLite `sync_queue`, not the KV one.

**Jest mocking:** `lib/chatImages.ts` imports `expo-image-manipulator`, which ships unparsed ESM `export` syntax this project's plain `ts-jest` setup doesn't transform (node_modules isn't transformed by default). Mocked via `__mocks__/chatImages.js` in `jest.config.js`'s `moduleNameMapper` — same class of fix as the `react-native-purchases` mock already documented under "Native IAP paywall" below.

### Transaction proof images (`lib/proofs.ts` + `src/components/ui/ProofControl.tsx`, `db/migration_v155.sql`)

Optional photo proof ("preuve") for money-movement records — capital injections/withdrawals (`apport`), expenses, and purchase orders. One image per row, view-only (no share). Originally shipped fully immutable (add once, never replace or delete); `migration_v168.sql` reversed the delete half of that on direct product request — see below.

**The load-bearing decision: attach is always post-hoc on an already-saved row — no creation path is touched.** `record_injection`/`record_withdrawal`/`edit_injection`, the `expenses` insert, and `createCommande` are all unchanged. A single `attach_transaction_proof(p_kind, p_id, p_image_url, p_image_width, p_image_height)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC stamps the three `proof_image_*` columns on whichever table `p_kind` names. This isn't a shortcut — it's what makes three otherwise-awkward cases fall out of one path for free:
- **Retroactive add** (the user's core ask — "go back and add it") is inherently post-hoc.
- **Expenses can be created offline** (SQLite `sync_queue`); images can't ride that queue. Decoupling means the expense still saves offline and the photo attaches later when online, rather than the whole create failing.
- **Approved expenses have no edit form at all** (only `en_attente` + own is editable) — a post-hoc attach reaches them; threading through the create form never could.

**Replacement is still DB-enforced.** `attach_transaction_proof` refuses when the row's `proof_image_url` is already non-null ("Une preuve est déjà attachée") — you cannot swap a photo for another one, ever; the only way to change it is delete-then-reattach (see below). The UI never normally hits this refusal (once a proof exists, `ProofControl` shows the view state, not the attach button).

**Deletion, added by `migration_v168.sql`, is deliberately much narrower than attach's role gate.** `delete_transaction_proof(p_kind, p_id)` only succeeds for the exact person who ran the original attach call (`proof_attached_by = auth.uid()`, not admin/manager in general, not the record's own `created_by`) and only within 24 hours of `proof_attached_at` — both checked server-side against `auth.uid()`/`now()`, same posture as `edit_sale`'s 48h window. Before this migration, nothing tracked *who* attached a proof or *when* — the original design assumed permanence, so there was nothing to check a delete against. `proof_attached_by`/`proof_attached_at` (nullable, added alongside the existing `proof_image_*` trio on all three tables) exist purely to make this rule enforceable; `attach_transaction_proof` now stamps both. Deleting nulls the `proof_image_*` columns plus these two — it does **not** remove the file from Storage (`transaction-proofs` still has no DELETE policy from v155): reaching into Storage from a SECURITY DEFINER SQL function isn't a plain column update, and an orphaned blob costs storage, not correctness, since nothing renders it once the DB reference is null. `ProofControl`'s viewer shows a "Supprimer" button only when its own client-side mirror of this same check passes (`attachedBy === currentUserId` and within the 24h window) — pure UX (hide the dead-end button), never the real gate.

**Role gate lives entirely in the RPC** (per-kind): admin/manager for `apport` and `purchase_order`; admin/manager **OR the expense's own creator while it is still `en_attente`** for `expense` — the vendeur who actually holds the receipt. `ProofControl`'s `canAttach` prop mirrors this client-side (defense-in-depth); RLS/RPC is the real gate. Viewing rides each table's existing SELECT RLS — anyone who can already read the transaction sees its `proof_image_url` (it's just a column), so no new read policy.

**Storage is a dedicated `transaction-proofs` bucket, not the chat `message-images` one** — keeps receipts out of the chat-image namespace (matters if chat images are ever moderated/pruned). Path `{kind}/{business_id}/{transaction_id}.jpg`. `uploadMessageImage` (`lib/chatImages.ts`) was generalized with optional `bucket` + `upsert` params to serve both surfaces; `lib/proofs.ts`'s `attachTransactionProof` uploads with `upsert:true` so a retry after a partial failure (upload succeeded, RPC then failed) isn't blocked by the deterministic path already existing — DB immutability is the real add-once guard, not the storage layer.

**Reconciliation is untouched** — the three columns are nullable and read by none of the 78 checks.

**Two display patterns.** Purchase orders use `ProofControl` (a labeled `row` control at the bottom of the PO `OrderDetail` modal); `onAttached` passes the `{url,width,height}` back so the PO modal can patch `detailOrder` in place without a refetch that would drop its lazily-loaded `lines`. **Expenses** keep that same `ProofControl` on the expense *card* for adding an image after the fact, **and** additionally embed a `ProofPhotoField` inside the `ExpenseFormModal` so an image can be attached *while creating* the expense — `createExpense` was changed to return the new expense id (`string | null`, not a bare boolean; the id is client-generated in the insert payload) so the picked image attaches to it post-insert, best-effort (an offline-queued expense can't upload, so it gets its image later from the card). **Apports use a different, calmer pattern** (redesigned in a Jony-Ive-style pass — the amount is the hero, the receipt is shown as itself, and adding a photo happens in the form, never as inline chrome): `ProofThumbnail` renders a small real-receipt thumbnail on a list row **only when a proof exists** (tap → full screen; there is deliberately no per-row camera on empty rows — that clutter was the whole reason for the redesign), and `ProofPhotoField` is a form photo-well used inside the add/withdraw/edit/view sheets, so a photo is attached during creation (new entries) or retroactively (past entries). Past **withdrawals** can't be edited, so tapping one opens a read-only `view` mode whose only purpose is to view/attach the photo. `record_injection`/`record_withdrawal` both already `RETURN` the new row's uuid, so `addApport`/`recordWithdrawal` now return that id (not a bare boolean) and a photo picked during creation attaches to the just-inserted row via `attach_transaction_proof`. Attach requires online; offline shows a disabled hint. Sales are deliberately out of scope. The old `ProofControl inline` variant is no longer used by any screen (apports moved off it) but is kept for now.

### Remote image rendering — `expo-image`, not RN's core `Image` (added 2026-07-26)

Google Play's pre-launch report flagged this app's bitmap handling ("manually downloading and decoding images from the network," pointing at Glide/Fresco internals) as a memory/performance/crash risk. Root cause: every remote image (`ImageMessageBubble` for chat, `ProofControl`/`ProofThumbnail`/`ProofPhotoField` for transaction proofs) used React Native's core `Image`, which has no native downsampling hint — Android decodes the *full* source bitmap into memory before the view clips/scales it down visually. Uploads are already capped at a 1600px longest edge (`lib/chatImages.ts`'s `uploadMessageImage`), so it's not multi-megapixel originals, but repeatedly decoding a full 1600px JPEG for a 34–48px list thumbnail (`ProofThumbnail`, `ProofControl`'s row thumb) is exactly the pattern Play's scanner flags, and plausibly the kind of memory pressure that contributes to a low-end-device crash even with zero crash reports yet (only ~20 installs at the time this was caught).

Fixed by switching all four components to `expo-image`'s `Image` (native downsampling/caching built in) — `resizeMode` → `contentFit`, otherwise a drop-in replacement (`expo-image`'s `ImageStyle` is a re-export of RN's, so `imageStyle` prop typing in `ImageMessageBubble` didn't need to change). **This requires a fresh `eas build` for both platforms — it cannot ship via `eas update` OTA.** `expo-image` is a native module, so per the `runtimeVersion: 'fingerprint'` policy it also shifts the computed fingerprint hash the same way any other native dependency addition does (see the SMS-retriever/RevenueCat notes above) — don't publish an OTA from a commit that added this without a matching native build already installed on the target device.

### Le Marché community forum (`stores/market.ts`)

`market_posts`, `market_comments`, `post_likes`, `comment_likes` tables (v37–v40). Posts and comments are gated by `community_level`:
- `community_level` is auto-computed from `profiles.points` via a DB trigger
- Posting requires `community_level >= 2`; admins bypass this
- `administrateur` members always bypass the level gate
- Like velocity cap: max 3 likes/day per unique (liker, post/comment) pair (v40)
- `author_name` is derived server-side from `profiles` — the `create_market_post` and `create_market_comment` RPCs ignore any caller-supplied name (v44)

### Error handling (`lib/errors.ts`)

Always wrap Supabase errors with `translateError(error)` before displaying to users. It maps Supabase/Postgres error codes and messages to French strings. Never show raw Supabase error messages in the UI. Postgres exceptions raised in RPCs with French messages pass through untranslated (used for `join_business` rate-limit, manager-limit, and expired-code messages).

### Forms

Forms use `react-hook-form` + `zod`. Define a Zod schema, derive the TypeScript type with `z.infer<>`, pass the resolver to `useForm`. Validation error messages should be in French.

### Offline queue (`lib/db.ts` + `lib/sync.ts` + `stores/sync.ts`)

`expo-sqlite` opens `patron.db` with WAL mode on first call (`openDb()`). A `migrate()` function tracks versions in `_migrations`.

**`openDb()`'s cached promise used to permanently wedge on a single transient native failure (found via a live Sentry Android crash, `NativeDatabase.prepareAsync` / "Cannot use shared object that was already released", fixed 2026-08-01).** `_dbPromise` is assigned synchronously to the in-flight IIFE's promise so concurrent callers share one open+migrate run — but `if (!_dbPromise)` reads `false` for a *rejected* promise too, so if `SQLite.openDatabaseAsync`/`execAsync`/`migrate` ever threw even once (a real, observed Android-native race, not something caused by app code — this codebase never calls `closeAsync` at all), every future `openDb()` call for the rest of that app session returned the same dead rejected promise instead of retrying, silently breaking the sync queue and every read cache until the app was killed and relaunched. Fixed with `.catch(err => { _dbPromise = null; throw err; })` so one bad open self-heals on the next call instead of poisoning the whole session.

**Offline write flow:**
1. Store attempts the Supabase call normally.
2. On network error (`isNetworkError()` in `lib/sync.ts`), the payload is written to the `sync_queue` SQLite table via `enqueue()`.
3. `app/(app)/_layout.tsx` listens to `AppState` changes and calls `drainQueue()` every time the app comes to foreground. If any items sync successfully, products are re-fetched.
