4. `useSyncStore` (`stores/sync.ts`) exposes `pendingCount`, `syncing`, and `sync()`. `SyncBanner` in `_layout.tsx` renders an amber strip when `pendingCount > 0`.
5. Failed items are retried up to `MAX_SYNC_ATTEMPTS = 5` times, then excluded from the count.

Operations queued: `submit_sale` (RPC) and `create_expense` (table insert).

`stores/sales.ts` exposes `lastSubmitQueued: boolean` so `vendre.tsx` can show "Vente enregistrée hors ligne ⏳" and skip the `fetchProducts` call (which would also fail offline).

The online submit path (`stores/sales.ts`) notifies admins/managers right after a successful `submit_sale` call. `lib/sync.ts`'s `executeOp` fires the same `sale_completed` notification after a queued `submit_sale` replays successfully — this was missing until recently, meaning sales made fully offline (this app's core low-connectivity use case) never generated the notification once synced. The lookup (`notifyQueuedSaleSynced`) rebuilds seller name/currency/description from the raw queued payload since no `CartLine`/`Product` objects survive a trip through SQLite, and is best-effort — a lookup failure there must never affect the sync result itself.

**Web stub:** `lib/db.web.ts` exports no-op stubs for all queue helpers so the app bundles on web without crashing.

**Offline read caches (separate mechanism from the write queue above).** Nearly every store (`products`, `ventes`, `rapports`, dashboard KPIs, `equipe`, `fournisseurs`, `expenses`, `apports`, `investor`, `partnerships`, `chat`, `market`) follows the same pattern, each with its own `lib/db.ts` table (`*_cache`, `cache_key`/`business_id` + `data` + `cached_at`, AES-256-GCM encrypted): seed the UI from the local cache immediately on mount, fetch from Supabase in the background, and on `isNetworkError()` fall back to the same cache (surfacing `offline`/`offlineSince` so the screen can show `<OfflineNotice>` instead of a blank or zero state). This is deliberately real persistent SQLite, not a volatile OS cache — a vendeur who's been online at least once can browse their sales, stock, team, reports, etc. fully offline; only writes (`submit_sale`, `add product`, ...) require a live connection, queued via `sync_queue` above if not. A device that has *never* been online for a given business has nothing to seed from — `fetchSales`'s error message ("Ouvrez l'application en ligne une première fois...") states this honestly instead of showing a misleading blank list, but there's no way around needing one real fetch first.

This read-cache rollout (`e08aec3`, 2026-07-05) was originally bundled into the same commit as push notifications and voice messages, and the first OTA carrying it had to be rolled back ("previous OTA introduced expo-notifications/expo-av/expo-clipboard native deps not present in installed build") — the same native-module-via-OTA failure class documented under "Native IAP paywall" and the SMS-retriever module: a JS-only-looking diff isn't actually JS-only once it imports a package with a native side nobody rebuilt for. It was re-shipped successfully as a JS-only OTA once split apart. **Lesson for any future offline-read work: audit new imports in the same commit for native modules before treating a read-cache change as OTA-safe.**

**Gap found and fixed 2026-07-17: not every screen that reads via a cached store is itself fully cached.** `app/(app)/clients/[name].tsx` (the per-client ledger) calls the already-cached `useVentesStore.fetchSales` for the sale list, but had two of its own direct, uncached `supabase.from(...)` reads: the client record (name/phone/notes) and — more seriously — the payments list used to compute `totalOwed = totalSold - totalPaid`. Offline, the payments query just failed and `ledgerPayments` silently stayed `[]`, so `totalOwed` read as the client's full lifetime sale total instead of their real remaining debt — wrong data, not just missing data. Fixed by adding `client_ledger_cache` (`lib/db.ts` migration v18, same generic `cache_key` shape as `ventes_cache`, shared by both reads via a `payments:`/`record:` key prefix) and wiring the same seed-then-fetch-then-fallback pattern used everywhere else. **Any screen that adds its own direct Supabase read alongside an already-cached store must get the same cache-fallback treatment individually — being downstream of one cached store doesn't cover a second, independent read.**

**Bigger gap, same day: nearly every offline-cached screen was actually broken on a real device, despite every store's fallback logic reading correctly on paper.** Real airplane-mode testing (not just code review) showed Produits/Vendre/Ventes/Crédits/Clients/Fournisseurs/Apports either stuck forever in a skeleton/"Chargement…" loading state, or (Rapports) silently showing a confident "0 GNF, aucune vente" instead of real cached numbers. Root cause: **none of the Supabase read calls had a client-side timeout.** Every store's catch block correctly falls back to its SQLite cache on `isNetworkError()` — but that block only runs if the fetch actually rejects. Under some real-world network conditions (not true airplane mode specifically, but e.g. a dead/captive wifi), the underlying fetch can hang indefinitely instead of rejecting, so `loading` never resolves and the fallback code — however correct — never executes. Fixed with `withTimeout()` (`lib/sync.ts`, 12s, throws a message containing "timeout" which `isNetworkError()` already recognizes, so every store's existing fallback treats a timeout exactly like any other network failure) wrapped around the primary fetch in `products`/`ventes`/`apports`/`fournisseurs`/`rapports` stores. **Static code review of the cache-fallback logic alone cannot catch this class of bug — it requires actually testing on a device in airplane mode**, since the bug is in whether the `catch` block is ever reached at all, not in what it does once reached. Two independent, smaller bugs found the same session: `vendre.tsx`/`catalogue.tsx`'s empty-catalog states didn't check `offline` at all, so "nothing cached yet" rendered as "add your first product" — actively wrong when a real product exists; and `rapports.tsx`'s KPIs all default via `?? 0` when `snapshot` is `null` (exactly the offline-with-nothing-cached state), producing a real-looking all-zero dashboard instead of an honest "no data" message — the same class of bug the `c081e60` "misleading blank/zero state" fix (July 5) was meant to eliminate everywhere, but missed here since `snapshot` being `null` doesn't trip the same guard a `[]` array does elsewhere.

**The `withTimeout()` sweep above only ever covered `products`/`ventes`/`apports`/`fournisseurs`/`rapports` — 5 of the ~13 stores that actually needed it, plus zero of the screen-local fetches. Found and closed 2026-07-18** (reported as "app just stalls, offline banner sometimes doesn't even show" on a friend's iPhone; screenshot showed Accueil stuck forever on `<SkeletonKpiGrid />`). Auditing every store for the same "sets `loading:true`, fetches, catch-block falls back to cache but only if the fetch actually rejects" shape found the identical unprotected pattern in:
- `app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx` — the dashboard's own `loadKpis()`/`loadBestSellers()` (the ones in the actual bug report — these are local functions in the screen file, not in a store, so they were invisible to a search scoped to "which stores")
- `stores/investor.ts`, `stores/chat.ts`, `stores/market.ts` (both `fetchPosts` and `fetchPostDetail`), `stores/partnerships.ts`, `stores/supportChat.ts` (`load`, `loadConversationDetail`, `loadFounderConversations`), `stores/alpha.ts` (`load`) — every one of these is in the "offline read caches" list earlier in this file, so the cache-fallback code already existed and was already correct; it just could never be reached if the fetch hung instead of rejecting
- `stores/auth.ts`'s `initialize()` (`supabase.auth.getSession()`) and `loadSession()` — the highest-severity case, since **this store's `loading:true` unmounts the entire app**, not just one screen (see "Critical: auth store `loading` flag" below). A hang here only matters when there's no local session cache to render first (fresh install, or a cleared cache) — an existing user with cached data was never at risk of a full blank-app hang, only of the one screen whose fetch hung
- `stores/sales.ts`'s `submit_sale`/`submit_carnet_debt` RPC calls — arguably the most severe of all, since a hang here (rather than a clean network-error rejection) would leave a live sale stuck on a "processing" spinner forever instead of falling into the offline `sync_queue`. Safe to add a client-side timeout here specifically because `submit_sale` already has idempotency-key dedup (`migration_v122.sql`) — if the timeout fires client-side while the original request is still completing server-side, the retry/offline-replay shares the same key and can't create a duplicate order. `submit_carnet_debt` has no such key (lower-frequency, manually-correctable onboarding action, accepted as-is)
- `stores/alpha.ts`'s reply fetch (`supabase.functions.invoke('alpha-chat', ...)`) also got wrapped, but with a **45s** timeout instead of the default 12s — a real LLM reply can legitimately take longer than a plain DB read (up to 3 rounds of tool-calling, see "On-demand tool-calling" below), and timing it out at 12s would cut off a genuine in-progress answer, not just a hung one

**Not fixed, deliberately**: several `.then()`-style background enhancement fetches with no `loading` flag at all (client-name autocomplete in `vendre.tsx`, extra-linked-products in `fournisseurs/[id].tsx`, business-phone backfill in `parametres/index.tsx`) — a hang there just means that one optional list stays empty, never an infinite spinner or blank screen, so they're a different, lower-severity class than the bug being fixed here.

**Also fixed while auditing: `withTimeout()` itself (`lib/sync.ts`) never called `clearTimeout` on its internal timer** — every call left a real `setTimeout` running for the full `ms` even after the race already settled via the fetch succeeding normally. Harmless in the app (just a dangling timer per call, GC'd eventually), but with dozens of new call sites added by this fix, it started leaving the Jest worker process unable to exit cleanly after the test suite finished (`"A worker process has failed to exit gracefully"`). Fixed with `.finally(() => clearTimeout(timer))` on the `Promise.race`. One test (`__tests__/alpha.test.ts`) had to be adjusted alongside this — its assertion ran immediately after `await sendMessage(...)`, relying on the detached background reply-fetch happening to finish first purely due to how few microtask hops a bare `await supabase.functions.invoke(...)` took; wrapping that call in `withTimeout()` (a `Promise.race`) adds a couple of extra hops, which is imperceptible to a real user but was enough to flip the ordering the test's timing assumption depended on. Fixed by explicitly flushing the microtask/macrotask queue (`await new Promise(resolve => setImmediate(resolve))`) before asserting, instead of relying on incidental timing.

**Lesson: when a fix like this is scoped by "which stores," it will systematically miss (a) screen-local fetches that never lived in a store, and (b) any store that wasn't on whatever list was checked at the time.** The reliable way to re-audit is `grep -rn "await supabase\.\(rpc\|from\|auth\.\)" app/ stores/ | grep -v withTimeout`, then triage each hit by whether it gates a `loading` flag an unrelated catch block is the only thing that ever clears.

**`stores/investor.ts`'s `fetchBalance`/`fetchPayouts` were exactly this kind of miss — a `withTimeout()` call with no `.catch()` and no wrapping `try` at all, found via a live Sentry unhandled-promise-rejection report, not code review (fixed 2026-07-26).** Unlike every sibling store (`apports.ts`, `rapports.ts`, `ventes.ts`, …), which either chains `.catch(err => ({ data: null, error: err }))` directly onto `withTimeout(...)` or wraps it in a `try/catch`, these two did `const { data, error } = await withTimeout(...)` bare — a timeout throws straight out of the async function with nothing to catch it. That alone would be inert if every caller awaited the result, but both real call sites invoke them fire-and-forget with no `await`/`.catch` (`app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s dashboard load for an investisseur, and `app/(app)/equipe/index.tsx`'s member-detail drawer when the tapped member is an investisseur) — so a 12s timeout became a genuine unhandled promise rejection (`Error: Network timeout after 12000ms`, Sentry `mechanism: onunhandledrejection`), reported for an *administrateur* role because it was the equipe-drawer path, not the dashboard one, that fired in the wild. Beyond the Sentry noise, this also meant no fallback ever ran — `loading` stayed `true` forever on a real timeout, same hung-skeleton failure mode as every other case in this section, just missed by the original sweep since `investor.ts` wasn't on its list. Fixed by adding the same `.catch(err => ({ data: null, error: err }))` used everywhere else. JS-only — shipped via OTA, no fingerprint impact.

**Two more instances of the identical class, found via live Sentry "Network timeout after 12000ms" reports (fixed 2026-08-01):**
- `stores/sales.ts`'s `submitCarnetDebt` (the "Crédit rapide" quick-debt RPC) had the same bare `const { error } = await withTimeout(...)` with no wrapping `try` — and both real call sites (`vendre.tsx`'s credit sheet, `onboarding/carnet.tsx`'s bulk import) invoke it with a plain `await`, no `.catch`. A genuine 12s timeout threw straight through the store call and out of the screen's async handler as an unhandled rejection. Fixed by wrapping the RPC call in `try/catch`, returning `false` (same contract every caller already branches on via `if (!ok) ...`) and showing a "Vérifiez votre connexion" toast on the caught path — mirroring `submit_sale`'s existing try/catch shape in the same file.
- `app/(app)/clients/[name].tsx`'s `loadClientRecord`/`loadLedgerPayments` — screen-local fetches, so outside `findUnprotectedFetchViolations()`'s `stores/*.ts` scope described below — are called fire-and-forget from a mount `useEffect` (`loadClientRecord()`, `loadLedgerPayments()`, no `await`/`.catch`). Both already had correct `if (error) { ...cache fallback... }` handling for a *returned* Supabase error, but neither caught the case where `withTimeout` itself *rejects* (a real timeout, as opposed to a resolved `{data:null,error}`) — that rejection had nowhere to go. Fixed by wrapping each function's body in `try/catch`, falling back to the same cache-read already used for the returned-error case.

**A much larger sweep of the same class, found by systematically re-auditing every `stores/*.ts` action (`saving`-gated write) function for a bare `await supabase.rpc/from(...)` with no wrapping `try/catch` at all — not just a timeout the catch block mishandles, but no catch block whatsoever (fixed 2026-09-23, no live incident yet, caught proactively).** `findUnprotectedFetchViolations()` only checks functions that already contain `isNetworkError(` (the offline-*read*-cache signature) — it has no opinion on a *write* action that never had any error handling to begin with, so this shape slipped past both the automated check and every prior manual sweep. The pattern is always the same: `set({ saving: true })`, a bare RPC/table call, `set({ saving: false })` only on the line immediately after — so a genuine network exception (the global `fetchWithTimeout` in `lib/supabase.ts` aborts any request at 15s, which is a **thrown** rejection, not a returned `{data,error}`) skips straight past `saving: false` and leaves the button spinning/disabled forever, on top of becoming an unhandled promise rejection. Found and fixed across 6 stores: `investor.ts` (`requestPayout`/`confirmPayout` — the exact two sibling functions in the same file whose `fetchBalance`/`fetchPayouts` counterparts were already fixed for this in 2026-07-26, but these two were missed that pass), `apports.ts` (`addApport`/`editApport`/`recordWithdrawal`), `fournisseurs.ts` (`createFournisseur`/`updateFournisseur`/`deleteFournisseur`/`payDebt`/`createCommande`/`recevoirCommande`/`createDebt` — this file's entire write surface had never been covered by any prior sweep, only its read path via `withNetworkRetry`), `ventes.ts` (`cancelSale`'s audit-trail name lookup, which sat *before* its own try block; `updateSaleClient`), `alpha.ts` (`fetchQuota`/`checkWhatsappConsentEligibility`/`recordWhatsappConsent` — lower severity, no `saving` flag involved, but still real unhandled-rejection Sentry noise), and two secondary enrichment fetches inside an otherwise-protected *read* function (`expenses.ts`'s creator-name lookup, `equipe.ts`'s profile-name lookup) that sat between the primary fetch's `withNetworkRetry` and the final `loading: false` — passing `findUnprotectedFetchViolations()`'s "does `withTimeout(` appear anywhere in this function" check without actually protecting the second call. Also found in screen-local handlers, the same blind spot already documented for `clients/[name].tsx` above: `parametres/index.tsx`'s three account/business-deletion flows (`handleLeaveOrDeleteBusiness`, `handleDeleteAccount`, `handleDeleteAccountOtpComplete` — the single highest-stakes surface to get this wrong, now caught), `fournisseurs/[id].tsx`'s `linkProduct`, `onboarding/carnet.tsx`'s bulk-import loop, and `vendre.tsx`'s "Crédit rapide" client-resolution step (`handleCreditAdd`) — this last one is the core quick-debt flow the "radical simplicity" mission above is built around, so a stuck spinner there was the worst-case instance of this whole sweep. **Any new `saving`-gated store action or screen handler must wrap its RPC/table call in `try/catch` from the start** — `findUnprotectedFetchViolations()` cannot catch a missing catch block, only a catch block that forgot `withTimeout`, so this class needs a human re-audit (`grep -rn "await supabase\.\(rpc\|from\|auth\.\)" app/ stores/ | grep -v withTimeout`, then check each hit for a wrapping try) rather than relying on the automated gate.

**This whole class of bug — a store fetch with a correct `isNetworkError()` cache fallback that can still never be reached because the fetch hung instead of rejecting — is now caught automatically, not just by manual re-audit.** `scripts/lib/consistency-checks.js`'s `findUnprotectedFetchViolations()` scans every `stores/*.ts` function body for the pairing "sets `loading: true`" + "calls `isNetworkError(`" (the signature of the offline-read-cache pattern) and fails if `withTimeout(` isn't also present in that same function. Wired into `npm run check:consistency` and mirrored as a Jest test (`__tests__/consistency-checks.test.ts`) so it also runs under plain `npm test`. Deliberately scoped to `stores/*.ts` only — screen-local fetches (the `app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx` `loadKpis`/`loadBestSellers` case from the sweep above) use inconsistent loading-state conventions a regex-based check can't reliably parse, so that gap stays a known, accepted limitation rather than something silently miscovered as "checked."

**`.github/workflows/ci.yml` only ever ran `tsc --noEmit` and `npm test`, never `npm run check`'s consistency-check or eslint steps (fixed 2026-07-28).** Both existed and were documented as part of the "fast, hermetic, no Docker needed" pre-merge gate, but neither was actually wired into the GitHub Actions workflow — they only ran if a human remembered to run `npm run check` locally before merging. Fixed by adding both as explicit CI steps. This also surfaced a second, unrelated pre-existing break: `lint` itself had been silently broken since the crypto-js commit below — `lib/encryption.ts` carried an `eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` comment, but `eslint.config.js` only ever registers `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` (see that file's own comment — this project deliberately runs no `@typescript-eslint` rules), so flat-config ESLint treated the directive as referencing an unknown rule and errored. Nobody caught it because `npm run lint`/`npm run check` were never actually run in CI, and it's easy to forget to run the full gate locally — exactly the failure mode this CI fix closes. Fixed by dropping the stale disable comment and using a plain `as unknown as number[]` cast instead (the array-like `WordArray.create` signature `crypto-js`'s own `.d.ts` doesn't model precisely).

### SQLite cache encryption (`lib/encryption.ts`)

AES-256-CBC (`crypto-js`, pure JavaScript, no native module) application-layer encryption for any sensitive SQLite data. Key is generated once per install and stored in `expo-secure-store` (hardware-backed). The sync queue itself is intentionally left unencrypted (it's transient).

**Previously used `globalThis.crypto.subtle` (Hermes's built-in WebCrypto, AES-GCM) — replaced 2026-07-17, this was the actual root cause of "empty cache" reports (`9a69b56`), not a network-timing issue.** RN/Expo's own docs claim `crypto.subtle` is available on RN 0.76+/Expo SDK 54+, which this app targets, but on real installed production builds it's `undefined` — every single cache write since the offline-read system shipped had been silently failing at the `encrypt()` step, caught by each call site's own empty `catch {}`, for twelve days before an on-device diagnostic (a "Tester écriture cache" button temporarily added to Paramètres) caught it. `crypto.subtle`'s availability is baked into the native binary, not something a JS-only fix can patch — `crypto-js` has zero dependencies and no native code, so switching to it is what made this fixable via OTA at all, without a new native build. Trade-off: CBC has no built-in tamper-detection the way GCM did — accepted, since this cache never leaves the device and every `decrypt()` call site already treats a decrypt failure as "no cache" (falls back to a live fetch), so garbage output on tampering degrades the same way a thrown error would. **If a future "merchant can't see their data" report surfaces again, don't assume this is automatically the cause** — this specific bug is fixed and shipped (confirmed live in every build since); re-verify against the `withTimeout()` sweep above and the consistency check first, since a hung fetch with no exception is the other, still-possible failure mode for the identical symptom.

### Receipt sharing (`src/components/ui/SaleReceiptView.tsx`)

A pure presentation component that renders a branded PNG receipt. Captured via `react-native-view-shot` (`captureRef`) and shared via `expo-sharing` (`Sharing.shareAsync`).

**Critical rendering constraint:** `captureRef` requires the target view to be within the GPU compositor bounds. Off-screen placement (`top: -9999`, `top: 5000`) returns a blank image. The pattern used: receipt at `top: 0, left: 0` inside a Modal → solid white `StyleSheet.absoluteFill` cover hides it from the user → dark overlay Pressable on top. `captureRef` captures the receipt's own pixels, unaffected by overlapping views.

**Share timing:** Call `Sharing.shareAsync` only after the modal close animation completes — use `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 350))` between `setShowConfirmSheet(false)` and the share call. On Android, keeping the modal open during sharing causes both to dismiss if the user backgrounds the app.

### Parametres screen (`app/(app)/parametres/index.tsx`)

Contains three critical flows:
- **Quit a business** (`leave_or_delete_business(p_business_id)`, `migration_v169.sql`, added 2026-09-02) — **one button, "Quitter {business}", shown to every role**, replacing what used to be two separate, role-conditional entries (a non-admin-only "Quitter" and an admin-only "Supprimer"). First-principles fix: "I don't want to be part of this business anymore" is one intent, not two, and what it actually *does* should depend on who else is there, not on the person pre-diagnosing their own situation before tapping anything. The RPC decides for itself: not the admin → just removes the caller's own membership, always allowed, never destructive (the business carries on for whoever's left). Admin, and genuinely alone → quitting **is** deleting, since there's nobody left for the business to exist for — full cascade delete (products, sales, expenses). Admin, but others still present → refused outright, on purpose, with **no auto-promotion and no "pick a successor" prompt** — just a plain instruction naming the business: remove the other members first. A team's admin should never disappear as a silent side effect of one tap. Client-side, an admin's tap opens the same typed-`SUPPRIMER` confirmation used for account deletion (since the outcome can be destructive); a non-admin's tap is the older plain `Alert.alert` confirm (never destructive, so lighter friction is fine). No client-side precheck query — the RPC is the single source of truth and its exception message already names the business, shown to the user as-is.
- **Leave semantics fold into the same button above** — the old standalone "Leave business" flow (checks remaining memberships, auto-switches to the first one if others exist, redirects to `/(welcome)/` if none remain) is still exactly what happens for a non-admin tap; it just isn't a separately-labeled menu item anymore. The confirmation copy is also a real, direct fix, not just rewording: it used to hedge ("if you're alone, X will happen; if not, Y") because the app didn't know which case applied until the RPC ran. `handleQuitterPress` now checks who else is in the business *before* opening any dialog, so the app always says one plain, true thing — either the real delete confirmation, or the block reason, never both wrapped in a conditional.
- **Every error shown in this screen must go through `rpcErrorMessage(error, fallback)`, never raw `error.message`** (found live, 2026-09-02 — a real "Could not find the function public.leave_or_delete_business… in the schema cache" PostgREST error, in English, reached the user directly, because `error.message` was rendered unconditionally). A Supabase/Postgres error here is one of two things: a deliberate RAISE EXCEPTION from `leave_or_delete_business`/`delete_my_account` (French, always SQLSTATE `P0001` — Postgres's default for a bare `RAISE EXCEPTION` with no explicit code) meant to be shown verbatim, or literally anything else (a missing function, RLS, network) which must never reach the screen untranslated. `rpcErrorMessage` shows `error.message` only when `error.code === 'P0001'`, and runs everything else through the existing `translateError()`. This also means **every RAISE EXCEPTION in these two RPCs has to be real French copy, not an internal placeholder string** — an early draft had bare `RAISE EXCEPTION 'not_authenticated'`/`'not_a_member'` for two effectively-unreachable guard clauses, which would have leaked just as badly (P0001, so shown as-is) the one time either ever fired; fixed by giving both real French messages even though real usage should never hit them. Same posture extends to `extractFnError()` (the `create-phone-verification`/`verify-phone-code` edge function helper): its own body-less fallback used to return the raw `fnErr.message` from a genuine network-level failure — changed to a fixed French line, since that fallback path has no French text to fall back on the way a normal edge-function error response does.
- **Delete account** — a 30-day, re-verified, cancellable request as of `migration_v170.sql`/`v171.sql` (added 2026-09-02), not an immediate delete. User types `SUPPRIMER`, then (if the admin/other-members block above doesn't apply — its exception now names every blocking business explicitly, e.g. "Vous êtes gérant de : Boutique A, Boutique B", not a generic "un commerce", same posture as `leave_or_delete_business`'s own message) is sent a fresh WhatsApp OTP to the phone on file — proving they still control the device before anything is scheduled — via `create-phone-verification`/`verify-phone-code` invoked **directly**, not through `stores/auth.ts`'s `loginWithPhone`/`verifyPhoneCode`: those call `supabase.auth.signInAnonymously()` and set the global `loading` flag, both correct for the pre-login screens they were built for but respectively session-destroying and app-blanking (see "Critical: auth store `loading` flag" below) if reused on an already-authenticated in-app screen. Once the code is verified, `delete_my_account()` no longer deletes anything itself — it stamps `profiles.pending_deletion_at = now() + 30 days` and the client immediately logs the user out. A daily cron (`process-scheduled-account-deletions`, `migration_v171.sql`) calls `finalize_account_deletion(user_id)` — the actual irreversible cascade-delete, service-role only — for every profile whose window has passed. **Cancellation is automatic, not a button**: `stores/auth.ts`'s `loadSession()` (the one choke point every real session-establishing path runs through — cold start, phone OTP login, biometric restore, email recovery) clears `pending_deletion_at` and toasts "Bon retour !" the instant the account holder is authenticated again, on the theory that logging back in is itself the unambiguous signal they don't want the account gone after all. No dedicated "cancel deletion" screen exists because the user is never left inside the app in a pending-deletion state to see one — they're signed out the moment deletion is scheduled.
- **Privacy + support links** — "Politique de confidentialité" opens `https://patron.kolilink.com/privacy.html`; "Contacter le support" opens `https://wa.me/16094454809`.

**Any bare `Linking.openURL(...)` for a `wa.me`/`whatsapp://` link needs a trailing `.catch(() => {})`, or a device with no WhatsApp installed throws an unhandled rejection straight to Sentry.** Found live (`app/(welcome)/index.tsx`'s WhatsApp-support corner button on the unauthenticated landing screen, fixed 2026-08-01) — `clients/index.tsx`'s equivalent link already had the `.catch()`, this one didn't, and being on the pre-login welcome screen meant it fired for anyone without WhatsApp, not just an edge case. `tel:` links and the plain HTTPS privacy/terms links (this file, `PaywallScreen.tsx`) are unguarded too but haven't shown up in Sentry — a genuine `https://` URL essentially never fails to resolve to *some* handler, unlike an app-specific scheme.

### Theme (`src/theme/`)

`src/theme/ThemeContext.tsx` provides a `ThemeProvider` (mounted in root `_layout.tsx`) and the `useTheme()` hook. The hook returns `{ palette, colorScheme, resolvedScheme, setColorScheme }`. User preference (`light | dark | system`) is persisted via `getKV/setKV`.

Always get `palette` via `useTheme()` — never import `paletteLight` or `paletteDark` directly in screens. Import spacing/radius/typography from `@/src/theme`.

Primary color is indigo (`#6366F1` / `colors.primary[500]`). Role colors: administrateur=indigo, manager=cyan, vendeur=green, investisseur=amber.

**Never use hardcoded hex values in screens** — always reference `palette` tokens.

### UI components (`src/components/ui/`)

Use the shared components before building ad-hoc ones. Import via `@/src/components/ui`.

| Component | Notes |
|---|---|
| `Button` | Primary/secondary/ghost variants, size sm/md/lg, fullWidth |
| `Card` | Surface container with border + shadow; pass `onPress` to make it tappable |
| `Text` | Semantic variants: h1–h4, body, bodySmall, label, caption, amount, amountLarge — map to `typography` tokens. **`amountLarge` has `lineHeight: 40` baked in — always override with a larger `lineHeight` when using a larger `fontSize`.** |
| `Input` / `DatePickerField` / `PhoneInput` | Form primitives |
| `FormSheet` | The only correct way to build a full-screen form modal — see "Form sheets — Android keyboard flicker" below. |
| `AppSheet` | Animated bottom sheet (spring in/out). Props: `visible`, `onClose`, `title`, `body`, `icon?`, `action?`. |
| `AppToast` / `AppToastContainer` | Top-banner toast; use `toast.success/warning/info()` helper from `stores/toast.ts`, not the component directly |
| `OtpInput` | 6-digit OTP entry with iOS AutoFill (`textContentType="oneTimeCode"`) |
| `SkeletonPlaceholder` / `SkeletonKpiGrid` | Loading skeletons for the dashboard KPI grid |
| `SaleReceiptView` | PNG receipt capture + share (see critical rendering constraint in architecture notes) |
| `SaleSuccessOverlay` | Post-sale confirmation with receipt action |

### Types (`src/types/index.ts`)

All domain types live here. Key ones:
- `Role`: `administrateur | manager | vendeur | investisseur`
- `OrderStatus`: `brouillon | confirme | annule | paye | credit`
- `AppSession`: `{ user, activeBusiness, activeMembership, memberships }`
- `Business`: multi-tenant entity with per-business `currency` field
- `User.recovery_email`: nullable — loaded from `profiles.recovery_email` in `loadSession`

### DB schema & migrations

Run Supabase migrations in order in the SQL Editor. Never skip versions.

| File | What it adds |
|------|-------------|
| `db/schema.sql` | Base schema; `is_member(business_id)` + `get_role(business_id)` RLS helpers; triggers for auto-profile + auto-admin-membership |
| `db/migration_v2.sql` – `v3.sql` | Incremental changes |
| `db/migration_v4.sql` | `clients` table, cancellation fields + reason tracking on `sale_orders` |
| `db/migration_v5.sql` | `payments` gets `date`, `customer_name`, `business_id` columns |
| `db/migration_v6.sql` | `sale_orders` gets `is_credit` boolean flag |
| `db/migration_v7.sql` | `sale_orders` gets `discount_amount`; payments `method` constraint updated |
| `db/migration_v8.sql` – `v18.sql` | Incremental feature additions |
| `db/migration_v19.sql` | RLS: split sale_orders + expenses SELECT into vendeur-only vs admin/manager policies |
| `db/migration_v20.sql` | Fix: drops the old catch-all "Membres: voir les ventes" policy missed by v19 |
| `db/migration_v21.sql` | `invite_attempts` rate-limit table; `validate_invite_code()` SECURITY DEFINER RPC (5 attempts/10 min); admin/manager-only UPDATE on memberships |
| `db/migration_v22.sql` | `get_best_sellers()` RPC; `submit_sale()` + `cancel_sale()` SECURITY DEFINER RPCs; removes vendeur INSERT access on `stock_moves` |
| `db/migration_v23.sql` | `delete_my_account()` SECURITY DEFINER RPC |
| `db/migration_v24.sql` | **All monetary columns → BIGINT cents (×100)** across products, sale_orders, so_lines, payments |
| `db/migration_v25.sql` | `analytics_events` table — founder analytics (INSERT only for merchants; read via service role) |
| `db/migration_v26.sql` | `sale_orders.idempotency_key` UUID column + partial unique index |
| `db/migration_v27.sql` | `phone_verification_attempts` rate-limit table (used by Edge Function) |
| `db/migration_v28.sql` | Fix: restrict product writes to admin/manager only (removes vendeur DB-level access) |
| `db/migration_v29.sql` | Enforce 1 business created per user via RLS policy |
| `db/migration_v30.sql` | Fix: vendeur expenses INSERT locked to `status='en_attente'` only |
| `db/migration_v31.sql` | Enforce max 1 manager per business via memberships INSERT policy |
| `db/migration_v32.sql` | Fix: infinite RLS recursion in memberships INSERT — extracted to `count_joined_businesses()` + `has_manager()` SECURITY DEFINER helpers |
| `db/migration_v33.sql` | `chat_rooms` + `chat_messages` tables; boutique private rooms + global Le Marché room |
| `db/migration_v34.sql` | `chat_room_reads` table — per-user read cursors for unread count |
| `db/migration_v35.sql` | `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL` on `chat_room_reads` so real-time UPDATE events carry payload |
| `db/migration_v36.sql` | `receive_purchase_order()` RPC — atomic stock update on PO receipt |
| `db/migration_v37.sql` | Le Marché: `market_posts`, `market_comments`, `post_likes`; `profiles.points`; `create_market_post` + `create_market_comment` + `toggle_post_like` RPCs |
| `db/migration_v38.sql` | RLS: investisseurs get SELECT on sale_orders, expenses, products |
| `db/migration_v39.sql` | `comment_likes` table; `toggle_comment_like` RPC; posting open to all authenticated members |
| `db/migration_v40.sql` | `profiles.community_level`; 9-tier level ladder; `create_market_post` gated on `community_level >= 2`; 3-likes/day velocity cap |
| `db/migration_v41.sql` | `supplier_debts` table — tracks what business owes each supplier (BIGINT cents) |
| `db/migration_v42.sql` | `sale_orders.client_id` FK to clients; backfill by name match; `submit_sale` updated to accept `p_client_id` |
| `db/migration_v43.sql` | `join_business()` SECURITY DEFINER RPC — now the only way to insert a membership (drops the open INSERT policy) |
| `db/migration_v44.sql` | Fix: `author_name` in `create_market_post` / `create_market_comment` derived from DB, not caller-supplied |
| `db/migration_v45.sql` | Fix: `receive_purchase_order` adds admin/manager role gate; uses `auth.uid()` instead of caller-supplied `p_user_id` |
| `db/migration_v46.sql` | Fix: `join_business` returns specific error messages for expired vs used vs invalid codes (instead of generic NULL) |
| `db/migration_v47.sql` | `businesses` gets `subscription_status` (trialing/active/cancelled/expired), `trial_ends_at`, `stripe_customer_id` |
| `db/migration_v48.sql` | `receive_purchase_order` auto-updates `products.cost_price` from PO unit cost on receipt |
| `db/migration_v49.sql` | Partial receipt support: `p_line_ids` param; PO status → `recu_partiel` when some lines remain |
| `db/migration_v50.sql` | Per-line received quantities (`p_line_ids` / `p_line_qtys` parallel arrays); `qty_received` is additive |
| `db/migration_v51.sql` | Fix: `submit_sale` stock deduction now rolls back the whole sale on failure (was silently swallowed); sole-admin cannot leave their own business |
| `db/migration_v52.sql` | Fix: `submit_sale` idempotency race — catches `unique_violation` (23505) and returns existing order instead of error |
| `db/migration_v53.sql` | Fix: `join_business` no longer burns rate-limit on non-existent codes |
| `db/migration_v54.sql` | Fix: adds `so_lines.product_name` column (missing since v51); reverts non-credit sale status back to `paye` (v51 had incorrectly set `confirme`) |
| `db/migration_v55.sql` | Chat reply threading: `chat_messages` gets `reply_to_id`, `reply_to_content`, `reply_to_sender_name` (denormalised — no JOIN needed to render reply preview) |
| `db/migration_v56.sql` | Message + post editing: `chat_messages` and `market_posts`/`market_comments` get `edited_at` timestamp |
| `db/migration_v57.sql` | `sale_orders.due_date` (DATE) — optional payment deadline for credit sales |
| `db/migration_v76.sql` | Email account recovery: `profiles.recovery_email` (UNIQUE TEXT); `email_verifications` + `email_verification_attempts` tables (service-role only, no user RLS policies) |
| `db/migration_v99.sql` | Nightly reconciliation system: `reconciliation_runs` / `reconciliation_findings` tables + `run_reconciliation()` — 68 SECURITY DEFINER checks across 14 domains (stock, sales, payments, COGS, expenses, credit, suppliers, purchase orders, products, monetary precision, members, cross-aggregates, temporal, referential integrity). Run nightly by the `send-reconciliation-report` Edge Function. |
| `db/migration_v103.sql` | `get_financial_snapshot()` — independent ground-truth revenue/COGS/expenses/net-profit recompute straight from the ledger, grouped by business currency (never blended across currencies). Included in the nightly reconciliation email as a sanity-check reference, separate from the 68 structural checks. |
| `db/migration_v104.sql` | Fix: 3 false-positive sources in checks #1, #2, #9 of `run_reconciliation()` — excludes `is_system` placeholder products (e.g. "Solde reporté") from stock checks; aggregates `so_lines` by `(order_id, product_id)` before comparing to `stock_moves` in check #2; check #9 compares `total_amount` against `SUM(qty * COALESCE(unit_price_paid, unit_price))` instead of catalog `unit_price` alone, since a seller can record a sale above catalog price. Reduced a first live run from 41 critical findings to 18 genuine ones. |
| `db/migration_v109.sql` | Pre-launch scale fix: adds missing B-tree indexes on `business_id` (`products`, `stock_moves`, `sale_orders`, `expenses`), `seller_id` (`sale_orders`), and `order_id`/`product_id` (`so_lines`), plus `created_at` (`payments`). Postgres never auto-indexes FK columns, only PKs/unique constraints — every `is_member(business_id)` RLS check and business-scoped screen query had been doing a sequential scan that grows with total rows across *all* businesses, not just one. |
| `db/migration_v112.sql` | Security fix: OTP codes (phone + email) had no limit on how many times a code could be *guessed* — only on how often a new one could be *requested* (5/10min). Adds `failed_attempts` to `phone_verifications` and `email_verifications`; `verify-phone-code`, `recover-by-email`, and `link-recovery-email` now lock a verification row after 5 wrong guesses and use a constant-time comparison on the token. |
| `db/migration_v113.sql` | Security fix: adds `ip_verification_attempts` — a secondary rate limit (20/hour) scoped per IP address across `create-phone-verification` and `send-email-otp`, closing a cost-abuse path where an attacker rotates phone numbers/emails to bypass the existing per-identity limit and run up WhatsApp/Twilio/Resend charges. `DEMO_PHONE` bypass is unaffected. |
| `db/migration_v114.sql` | Fix: check #74 ("Écart revenu commandes vs lignes") in `run_display_checks()` (added by `migration_v111.sql`) had a row fan-out bug — it joined `so_lines` onto `sale_orders` and then summed `so.total_amount`, so a multi-line order's total was counted once per line instead of once per order, inflating the aggregate by up to 7x for some businesses. Verified against production that no real order/line mismatch existed; every affected order's `total_amount` already matched its own lines exactly. Fixed by aggregating order totals and line totals in separate CTEs before comparing. |
| `db/migration_v115.sql` | Capital injections ("Apports") gain correction + reversal support, admin/manager only: `edit_injection()` RPC corrects a mistaken row in place (amount/contributor/note/date) and stamps `edited_at`/`edited_by`; `record_withdrawal()` RPC inserts a **new negative-amount row** rather than mutating history when capital is taken back out, so `SUM(amount)` across `capital_injections` (used by the cash-on-hand and "capital investi" totals in `migration_v110/v111/v114`) naturally reflects withdrawals without any change to those formulas. `capital_injections.amount` CHECK relaxed from `> 0` to `<> 0` to allow the negative rows. Neither RPC is reachable by vendeur/investisseur — same role gate as `record_injection`. |
| `db/migration_v116.sql` | Adds checks 77–78 to `run_display_checks()` (68 structural + 10 display = 78 total, up from 77) to cover the v115 capital edit/withdrawal RPCs: #77 (critical) flags a contributor whose net `capital_injections` position (apports minus retraits) has gone negative — more recorded as withdrawn than they ever contributed; #78 (warning) surfaces any row edited via `edit_injection()` or any withdrawal recorded via `record_withdrawal()` in the last 90 days, giving the founder nightly visibility on these manual overrides, the same way check #72 already surfaces manual `transport_achat` entries. `supabase/functions/send-reconciliation-report/index.ts` check-count text (77 → 78) updated to match. |
| `db/migration_v117.sql` | Fix: `get_reports_snapshot()` (added by `migration_v110.sql`) raised `column sm.variant_id does not exist` on every call — the stock-losses subquery joined `product_variants` on `sm.variant_id`, but `stock_moves` has never had a variant_id column (only `so_lines` / `purchase_order_lines` carry variant_id; stock moves, including `perte` losses, are always product-level). This made the Rapports screen show no data at all, for every business and every role, since the RPC was deployed. Fix: dropped the bogus join, uses the product's own `cost_price` directly. |
| `db/migration_v118.sql` | Fix: `get_reports_snapshot()` still raised `column so.seller_name does not exist` after v117 — the top-sellers subquery grouped by `sale_orders.seller_name`, but that column has never existed (`migration_v62.sql` already documented the same mistake in `submit_carnet_debt`). Seller display name has always been derived client-side (`stores/ventes.ts`): `memberships.display_name` override, then `profiles.name`, then a fallback. Fix: replicates that resolution in SQL via `LEFT JOIN memberships` / `profiles`, grouped by the real `seller_id` column. This was the second of two RPC bugs (after v117) that together made Rapports show no data since `get_reports_snapshot` was deployed in v110. |
| `db/migration_v119.sql` | Fix: `get_reports_snapshot()`'s `activity`/`my_activity` daily series showed "Invalid…" day labels on the Rapports Semaine chart (and silently misplaced bars on Trimestre) for every role. `generate_series(date, date, '1 day'::interval)` returns `timestamp`, not `date`, so `gs.day::text` produced `"2026-06-25 00:00:00"` instead of `"2026-06-25"` — Hermes' `new Date(...)` (used in `app/(app)/rapports/index.tsx` for both the weekday label and the trimestre week-bucket offset) can't parse that non-ISO format. Fix: cast to `gs.day::date::text` before building the JSON payload. |
| `db/migration_v120.sql` | Perf: `create_business_with_membership(p_id, p_name, p_type, p_currency, p_phone)` — SECURITY DEFINER RPC replacing `createBusiness()`'s old pattern of inserting into `businesses` then polling `memberships` up to 5x (600ms apart, ~3s worst case) waiting for the `on_business_created` trigger. The trigger already fires in the same transaction as the insert, so the poll loop was pure client-side over-caution; the RPC now inserts and reads back the membership + business in one round trip. Re-checks the "1 business per user" rule from the `migration_v29` RLS policy manually, since SECURITY DEFINER bypasses table RLS. |
| `db/migration_v121.sql` | Security fix: `get_reports_snapshot()` (v110+) only checked that the caller is a member of `p_business_id` — it never verified the caller actually holds the caller-supplied `p_role`, nor that `p_user_id` was the caller's own id. Any member could pass `p_role: 'administrateur'` to read full business financials regardless of real role, or pass a teammate's `p_user_id` to read that teammate's personal figures. Fix: for any authenticated caller, role and user id are now derived server-side via `get_role()` / `auth.uid()`; the client-supplied `p_role`/`p_user_id` are ignored. The service-role internal-reconciliation path (`auth.uid() IS NULL`) is unaffected. |
| `db/migration_v122.sql` | Fix: restores the `submit_sale` idempotency-race guard from `migration_v52.sql` (`BEGIN...EXCEPTION WHEN unique_violation`), which was silently dropped when the function was rewritten for vendeur product-scope (`v67`) and never restored through any later rewrite (v78/v81/v86/v93/v96/v107). Without it, two concurrent calls sharing an `idempotency_key` (offline-queue retry racing a live retry, or two devices racing post-reconnect) could both pass the pre-check and one would raise a raw `unique_violation` instead of both resolving to the same order. |
| `db/migration_v123.sql` | Retires the standalone 2am UTC `patron-nightly-reconciliation` cron job (`SELECT cron.unschedule(...)`). Reconciliation is now folded into the single 6am ET combined daily report — see "Nightly reconciliation" below. |
| `db/migration_v124.sql` | Security fix, found via `__tests__/integration/`: `join_business`'s rate limiter (5/10min) never actually triggered against a wrong/expired/already-claimed code guess. Its `INSERT INTO invite_attempts` ran before the validation checks specifically so failures would count toward the limit, but a Postgres function invoked by one top-level statement is atomic — an uncaught `RAISE` rolls back everything the call did, including that insert. Only successful joins were ever actually logged. Fix: attempt-logging moved to its own `record_invite_attempt()` RPC (no conditional `RAISE` inside it, so it always commits as its own top-level statement); `join_business()`'s validation logic is otherwise unchanged. Client (`stores/auth.ts`'s `joinBusiness`) now calls `record_invite_attempt()` immediately before `join_business()`. |
| `db/migration_v125.sql` | Correctness fix, found via `__tests__/integration/`: `cancel_sale`'s stock-restore loop added the cancelled qty back onto the *parent* product's `stock_qty` for every line, variant or not — but `submit_sale` never touches a variant parent's `stock_qty` (documented invariant: "always 0 for variant parents"). Every variant-sale cancellation silently drifted the parent further away from 0. Fix: only restore `products.stock_qty` when the line has no `variant_id`; variant stock restoration is unchanged. |
| `db/migration_v126.sql` | In-app support chat (merchants ↔ founder) — see "Support chat" below. Adds `support_conversations`/`support_messages`/`support_ai_drafts`, `is_founder()` + `get_founder_id()` helpers (global `profiles.phone` match, independent of any business membership — Sebastiao is also an `administrateur` of his own "Maillot Commerce"), and an additive `businesses` SELECT policy so the founder can read business names across every business for the inbox list (RLS OR's multiple permissive SELECT policies together, so this only ever widens founder access, never narrows member access). |
| `db/migration_v129.sql` | Cross-device PIN sync fix (superseded — PIN itself was removed in v131; see "Biometric-only lock" above). Added `profiles.pin_hash`/`pin_updated_at` so the local-only salted PIN hash (previously SecureStore-scoped to a single device) could be opportunistically synced across a user's devices. |
| `db/migration_v130.sql` | Re-launches the subscription paywall via native Apple/Google IAP (RevenueCat) instead of the old in-app Stripe Payment Link flow — see "Native IAP paywall" below. Adds `businesses.payment_provider`/`revenuecat_customer_id`/`bonus_access_until`/`referred_by_business_id`/`referral_code`; `subscription_tier` (gratuit/starter/business/pro) is legacy/unused, left in place but no longer read by `lib/analytics.ts`. |
| `db/migration_v131.sql` | Removes the PIN-lock feature entirely (see "Biometric-only lock" above) — drops `profiles.pin_hash`/`pin_updated_at` (added by v129, now dead) and the separate, pre-existing, never-used `memberships.pin_hash` column. |
| `db/migration_v132.sql` | Image messages in boutique chat (+ partner DMs) and support chat — see "Image messages" above. Extends `chat_messages.message_type` CHECK to include `'image'` (reusing the v90/v91 voice-message pattern) and adds `image_url`/`image_width`/`image_height`; adds the same three columns fresh to `support_messages`, whose two RPCs (`send_support_message`, `send_founder_support_reply`) gain optional `p_image_url`/`p_image_width`/`p_image_height` params and only require non-empty `content` when no image is attached. Rebased those two RPC bodies onto the *live* v127 version (not v126's original — v127 had already added a `merchant_name` refresh-on-send that a naive v126-based rewrite would have silently reverted). Explicitly `DROP FUNCTION`s the old 2-arg/3-arg signatures before recreating — see "Image messages" above for why `CREATE OR REPLACE` alone left both old and new overloads live and ambiguous. Creates the shared public `message-images` storage bucket. Le Marché is out of scope (see above). |
| `db/migration_v133.sql` | The AI business advisor (launched as "Mystic", renamed to "Alpha" by v134 below) — see "Alpha" above. Added `mystic_conversations` (one per business+user), `mystic_messages`, `mystic_quota` (one rolling-24h-window tracker shared by both the free 3/24h and paid 100/24h tiers); `has_ai_access()` (SQL-side mirror of the client's `isSubscriptionExpired`/`hasBonusAccess`); `open_or_get_mystic_conversation`, `send_mystic_message` (the sole quota-enforcement point), `get_mystic_quota_status` (read-only, for the client's countdown/upsell UI). All `mystic_*` names were superseded by v134 — this row is history, not the current schema. |
| `db/migration_v134.sql` | Renames the AI advisor's schema from `mystic_*` to `alpha_*` (tables, indexes, RLS policy labels, and all three RPCs). `has_ai_access()` untouched (never Mystic-specific). Plain `ALTER TABLE/INDEX RENAME` isn't sufficient for the RPCs — plpgsql bodies resolve table/function names as text at execution time, not by OID, so `send_mystic_message`'s internal call to `open_or_get_mystic_conversation()` and its `FROM mystic_messages` queries would have broken the moment the underlying objects were renamed out from under them. The three RPCs are dropped and recreated under their `alpha_*` names with bodies rewritten throughout — same logic as v133, new names. |
| `db/migration_v135.sql` | Stopgap: lowers `send_alpha_message`/`get_alpha_quota_status`'s paid-tier `v_limit` from 100/24h to 20/24h. Groq blocked new Developer-tier signups org-wide starting sometime before 2026-07-13, leaving Alpha stuck on Groq's Free tier (100,000 tokens/day, shared across the whole platform plus `generate-support-draft`) — at realistic per-reply token cost that only sustains ~60-100 total exchanges/day platform-wide, so the original 100/24h-per-business figure could let one active paying customer alone starve every other business's access for the rest of the day. Free tier (3/24h) and the 10-message welcome burst are untouched. Revert to 100 (or reassess) once Groq's Developer tier reopens — see "Billing" under "Alpha" above. |
| `db/migration_v136.sql` | Raises the free tier's `v_limit` from 3/24h to 5/24h in the same two functions, as part of a quota-UX pass distinguishing free-tier-exhausted (upgrade popup) from paid-tier-exhausted (plain wait state) — see "Free vs. paid exhaustion" under "Alpha" above. Welcome burst (10) and paid tier (20, still `migration_v135.sql`'s stopgap value) are untouched. |
| `db/migration_v137.sql` | Fix: `dispatch-notification` had hardcoded `badge: 1` on every push — Apple/Android apply whatever number the payload sends rather than accumulating it, so 3 unread notifications received while backgrounded/killed still only ever showed "1" on the app icon. Adds `profiles.unread_notification_count` (server-tracked per-user running total) and `increment_unread_notifications(p_user_ids)`, a SECURITY DEFINER RPC (execute revoked from `anon`/`authenticated`, granted only to `service_role`) that atomically increments and returns the real count per recipient; `dispatch-notification` now stamps each user's own push with their real total instead of a flat 1. Reset to 0 client-side (`resetUnreadBadge()` in `src/utils/notifications.ts`, a plain table update already permitted by the existing "Modifier son profil" RLS policy) on cold start and on every foreground return in `NotificationSetup.tsx`, alongside the existing local `setBadgeCountAsync(0)` — the local OS badge and the server counter are two separate pieces of state and both need resetting, or the next background push shows a stale, too-high number. |
| `db/migration_v138.sql` | Adds the Alpha quota-reset push reminder — see "Free vs. paid exhaustion" under "Alpha" below. `alpha_quota` gets `reset_notified_at`; `get_and_mark_alpha_quota_resets()` (SECURITY DEFINER, `service_role`-only) finds every user whose fixed 24h window expired while still at their limit and hasn't been notified for *that* window yet, atomically marking each as notified in the same call so an overlapping cron run can't double-send. Paired with the new `send-alpha-quota-reminders` Edge Function (see Edge Functions table) — not yet on a cron schedule; needs a Supabase dashboard Cron Job pointed at it (hourly) with the `CRON_SECRET` header, same setup style as the "Patron — Rapport Quotidien" routine. |
| `db/migration_v139.sql` | Adds the habit-forming afternoon "how did the shop do" digest push — see "Daily digest" below. `businesses` gets `digest_send_hour` (learned per-business send time, not a flat clock time) and `digest_last_sent_date` (dedupe guard). `learn_digest_send_hours()` recomputes the former daily from 14 days of sale history; `get_and_mark_daily_digest_businesses()` finds businesses due *this* hour, computes today's revenue (same formula as `get_reports_snapshot`), and marks them sent atomically. Both `service_role`-only. Paired with two new Edge Functions (`send-daily-digest` hourly, `learn-digest-send-hours` once daily) — neither is on a cron schedule yet. |
| `db/migration_v140.sql` | Adds Djomi (Orange Money) as a second, fully out-of-app subscription path — see "Djomi out-of-app subscription" below. `payment_provider` CHECK gains `'djomi'`; `businesses` gets `djomi_transaction_id`. Adds `resolve_business_for_djomi_checkout(p_phone)`, a `service_role`-only SECURITY DEFINER RPC (phone → the business that phone administers) used by the unauthenticated `djomi-checkout` page to identify who's paying, since there's no Supabase session to read `auth.uid()` from outside the app. Adds `djomi_pending_payments` (`service_role`-only, no client RLS policy) — bridges the gap between generating a payment reference and learning Djomi's own transaction id, is the trust boundary the poll endpoint uses to resolve a client-supplied reference to a transaction/business safely, and (via its `resolved_at` column) is what `djomi-sweep` uses to catch a payment whose merchant never returned to the checkout tab. |
| `db/migration_v141.sql` | Schedules `djomi-sweep` hourly via `pg_cron`, reusing the shared `patron_cron_secret` Vault entry every other cron-triggered function already authenticates with — not a djomi-specific secret. Requires `djomi-sweep`'s own `CRON_SECRET` Edge Function secret to hold the identical string, since Edge Function secrets and the Vault are two separate stores that happen to need to agree here. |
| `db/migration_v142.sql` | Adds `businesses.djomi_checkout_token` (mirrors `migration_v130.sql`'s `referral_code` generation pattern exactly — trigger-on-insert + one-time backfill) and `resolve_business_by_djomi_checkout_token(p_token)`, a `service_role`-only RPC. Backs the personalized-link mode of `djomi-checkout` (`?biz=<token>`) — see "Djomi out-of-app subscription" below. |
| `db/migration_v143.sql` | Adds `alpha_whatsapp_reminders_sent` and `get_and_mark_alpha_whatsapp_candidates()` — the targeting logic for the Alpha WhatsApp re-engagement reminder. See "Alpha WhatsApp reminder" below. |
| `db/migration_v144.sql` | Schedules `send-alpha-whatsapp-reminder` daily (07:00 UTC) via `pg_cron`, same `patron_cron_secret` Vault pattern as `migration_v141.sql`. |
| `db/migration_v145.sql` | Adds `profiles.alpha_whatsapp_consent`, `alpha_whatsapp_reminder_eligible_now(business_id)` (read-only, `authenticated`), and `record_alpha_whatsapp_consent(business_id, accepted)` (`authenticated`) — the in-app consent flow for the WhatsApp reminder. Updates `get_and_mark_alpha_whatsapp_candidates()` to require `alpha_whatsapp_consent = 'accepted'`, closing the consent gap `migration_v143.sql` shipped with. See "Alpha WhatsApp reminder" below. |
| `db/migration_v146.sql` | Reverses `migration_v135.sql`'s stopgap: raises the paid-tier Alpha quota from 20/24h back to **100/24h**. Confirmed `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set before this shipped — the Groq→OpenAI automatic fallback (added 2026-07-13, after v135) means Groq's shared platform capacity ceiling now soft-degrades to OpenAI instead of hard-failing, which was the actual reason v135 lowered this, not per-message cost (negligible either way, ~$0.0002–0.0003/reply even fully on OpenAI). Free tier (5/24h) and the welcome burst are untouched. `PAID_DAILY_LIMIT` also bumped in `PaywallScreen.tsx`, `djomi-checkout/index.ts`, and `abonnement/index.html` to match. |
| `db/migration_v147.sql` | Adds `app_config` (a generic `key TEXT PRIMARY KEY, value INT` table — `service_role`-only, no client RLS policy) and `set_alpha_daily_limit('free' \| 'paid', new_limit)`, a `service_role`-only RPC. `send_alpha_message`/`get_alpha_quota_status` now read `v_limit` from `app_config` instead of a hardcoded `CASE` literal — every future limit change (raise, lower, revert) is one `SELECT set_alpha_daily_limit(...)` call, not a new migration the way `v135.sql`/`v136.sql`/`v146.sql` each were. See "Alpha" below. |
| `db/migration_v148.sql` | `record_alpha_whatsapp_consent` now fires `send-alpha-whatsapp-reminder` immediately on acceptance via `net.http_post` (same mechanism the cron jobs already use to call an Edge Function from Postgres), instead of only waiting for the next daily cron tick. See "Alpha WhatsApp reminder" below. |
| `db/migration_v149.sql` | Fix: `get_and_mark_alpha_whatsapp_candidates()` raised `column reference "business_id" is ambiguous` on every real call since `migration_v143.sql` first shipped it — `RETURNS TABLE(business_id uuid, ...)` implicitly creates a same-named PL/pgSQL variable, and the `marked` CTE's `INSERT ... RETURNING business_id` was the one unqualified reference to it (every other reference in the function was already table-qualified). Fixed with `INSERT INTO alpha_whatsapp_reminders_sent AS awrs (...) RETURNING awrs.business_id`. Went undetected until live testing because it's a PL/pgSQL execution-time ambiguity, not a SQL syntax error — and was additionally masked by the `CRON_SECRET`/Vault mismatch (found the same session — see "Alpha WhatsApp reminder" below), which had every scheduled call 401ing before ever reaching this function. |
| `db/migration_v150.sql` | Security audit fixes (2026-07-14). **(1)** `invite_codes`: migration_v8's `invite_codes_read` SELECT policy OR'd in `(auth.jwt()->>'is_anonymous')::boolean IS TRUE`, which matches **every row** for any anonymous session — and `signInAnonymously()` is a normal call against the public anon key. So anyone could obtain an anon JWT and `SELECT *` every business's invite codes (code value, business_id, role) and then join arbitrary businesses via `join_business()`. That anonymous branch was **dead code** for the app (the join flow uses the `validate_invite_code`/`join_business` SECURITY DEFINER RPCs, which bypass RLS; the only direct client reads are member-scoped team management in `stores/equipe.ts`). Fixed by dropping the policy and recreating it `USING (is_member(business_id))` only. **(2)** `is_member(uuid)`/`get_role(uuid)` — the two SECURITY DEFINER helpers that gate **every** RLS policy — never set `search_path` (unlike every other definer function in the codebase), the Supabase-linter `function_search_path_mutable` class. `ALTER FUNCTION ... SET search_path = public` on both. No behavior change. |
| `db/migration_v151.sql` | Sale editing: admin/manager can correct a mistaken `paye`/`credit` sale in place (price, discount, customer, payment) instead of only cancel-or-keep — see "Sale editing" below. `sale_orders` gains `edit_count`/`last_edited_at`/`last_edited_by`; `sale_order_edits` stores a before/after jsonb snapshot per edit call. Limits (`sale_edit_max_count`=2, `sale_edit_window_hours`=48) reuse the `app_config` table from `migration_v147.sql` rather than hardcoding, via `set_sale_edit_limit()`. `edit_sale()` recomputes `total_amount` server-side from `so_lines` (never trusts the client), rejects any edit that would leave `SUM(payments)` out of sync with the new owed amount, and applies the same investor profit-share delta math `submit_sale` uses so a price correction doesn't silently desync `investor_balance`. `run_display_checks()` gains checks 79 (visibility: any sale edited in the last 90 days) and 80 (critical: an edit that moved money in the merchant's favor — total down, discount up, or amount paid down). Deliberately excludes quantity/product-substitution edits (Phase 2, not built) — those would move `stock_moves` and require rewriting checks #1/#2/#4 the way `annule` already gets special-cased; not needed for the actual problem (price/discount/customer typos, not wrong quantities). |
| `db/migration_v152.sql` | Fix: `get_product_stats()` (backs Alpha's `chercher_produit` tool) filtered `so.status != 'annule'` instead of `so.status IN ('paye', 'credit')` — the only revenue function in the codebase not using that convention. Let unpaid `brouillon`/`confirme` orders count as revenue, which could (and did, live) make a single product's lifetime revenue exceed the business's own lifetime total from `get_reports_snapshot`. See "Reply truncation" / "A number Alpha stated was not a hallucination" under Alpha below. |
| `db/migration_v153.sql` | Fix: `edit_sale()`'s two money-mismatch `RAISE EXCEPTION` messages interpolated `v_new_paid`/`v_new_owed` — both BIGINT cents — with no ÷100 conversion, so a real 6 000 vs 5 000 GNF mismatch surfaced to the merchant as "(600000)" vs "(500000)", reading as if the input had grown two extra zeros. Found live, same day the sale-editing feature itself was first exercised. Every other monetary value in this codebase divides by 100 before reaching a human (client-side via `formatAmount()`); this was the only value ever interpolated into a `RAISE EXCEPTION` and was missed when `edit_sale()` was first written, since neither the integration tests nor a human had tried an actual mismatched edit before. Only the two message lines change (`ROUND(v_new_paid / 100.0)::bigint` etc.) — the exact-cents comparison logic itself is untouched. Signature unchanged, so a plain `CREATE OR REPLACE` is safe (no duplicate-overload risk — see the `migration_v132.sql` note on why that only bites when parameter *count* changes). |
| `db/migration_v154.sql` | Adds `get_period_report()` — the RPC backing the redesigned Rapports year view (see "Rapports — year view" below). Calendar-anchored (`p_period_start`/`p_period_end`) instead of `get_reports_snapshot`'s rolling `p_period_days`, so the same call serves both the full-year heatmap and a narrow filtered period. `get_reports_snapshot` (v121) is untouched — Accueil's dashboard still calls it for month-to-date. Also adds a `sale_orders(business_id, sale_date)` composite index, since a full-year `generate_series` query now runs on every Rapports screen open. |
| `db/migration_v155.sql` | Photo proof ("preuve") for money-movement records — see "Transaction proof images" below. Adds nullable `proof_image_url`/`proof_image_width`/`proof_image_height` to `capital_injections`, `expenses`, `purchase_orders` (read by none of the 78 reconciliation checks → additive/safe); a dedicated public `transaction-proofs` storage bucket (not the chat `message-images` one); and `attach_transaction_proof(kind, id, url, w, h)` — one SECURITY DEFINER RPC for all three tables. Enforces a per-kind role gate (admin/manager for apport & PO; admin/manager OR the expense's own creator while still `en_attente`, for an expense) and immutability (refuses if a proof is already set — add once, never replace/delete). Deliberately NOT wired through any creation path (`record_injection`/`record_withdrawal`/`edit_injection`/expense insert/`createCommande` all untouched) — attach is always a post-hoc step on an existing row, which is what makes retroactive add, offline-created expenses, and locked/approved expenses all reachable by the same one path. |
| `db/migration_v156.sql` | Adds `get_founder_activity_raw()` — see "Founder Dashboard" below. `is_founder()`-gated SECURITY DEFINER RPC returning one row per user (signup timestamp + their full array of non-`annule` sale timestamps, keyed on `seller_id`) — the raw feed `src/utils/founderMetrics.ts`'s KPI calculations run against. Superseded an earlier draft of this same file (`get_founder_live_stats()`, a pre-aggregated 24h snapshot) before it was ever applied to any database — replaced in place rather than left as a stale, unapplied, and now-wrong migration. |
| `db/migration_v161.sql` | First-24h activation reminders — see "Activation reminders" above. Adds `businesses.activation_nudge_1_sent_at`/`_2_sent_at`, seeds `app_config` with `activation_nudge_1_hours`(2)/`activation_nudge_2_hours`(20), and `get_and_mark_activation_reminders()` (service-role only). Fixed in place (not shipped broken — caught by a live integration test before this migration was ever applied to a real database) from an initial version whose two-CTE marking approach silently dropped nudge_2 for a business due for both at once. |
| `db/migration_v162.sql` | Schedules `send-activation-reminders` hourly via `pg_cron`, same `patron_cron_secret` Vault pattern as `migration_v141.sql`/`v144.sql`. |
| `db/migration_v163.sql` | Second-action reminder (the post-24h retention window's highest-leverage segment) — see "Second-action reminder" above. Adds `businesses.second_action_nudge_sent_at`, seeds `app_config` with `second_action_nudge_hours`(48)/`second_action_nudge_max_days`(14), and `get_and_mark_second_action_reminders()` (service-role only) — sale-first, product-fallback classification to correctly handle `submit_carnet_debt`'s hidden system-product side effect and "Vente rapide"'s real cloned-product side effect. |
| `db/migration_v164.sql` | Schedules `send-second-action-reminders` hourly via `pg_cron`, same `patron_cron_secret` Vault pattern as `migration_v141.sql`/`v144.sql`/`v162.sql`. |
| `db/migration_v165.sql` | Revenue milestone reminder — see "Revenue milestone reminder" above. Adds `businesses.highest_revenue_milestone_cents` (a ratchet, not a log) and `get_and_mark_revenue_milestones()` (service-role only) — a fixed, widening GNF threshold ladder (1M/5M/10M/25M/50M/100M/250M/500M/1B), matching `get_reports_snapshot`'s lifetime revenue formula exactly. |
| `db/migration_v166.sql` | Schedules `send-revenue-milestone-reminders` hourly via `pg_cron`, same `patron_cron_secret` Vault pattern as `migration_v141.sql`/`v144.sql`/`v162.sql`/`v164.sql`. |
| `db/migration_v167.sql` | Fix: `get_founder_activity_raw()` (`migration_v156.sql`) had no filter on `profiles`, so it counted every abandoned phone-auth attempt and every demo-mode "Essayer" click as a real user — both leave a permanent `profiles` row via `signInAnonymously()` with `phone` left null. Demo clicks are worse: `seed-demo-business` seeds 12 fake `sale_orders` under that same anonymous user's own `seller_id`, including exactly 3 dated "today" — which is why "Ventes / utilisateur actif" was landing suspiciously exactly on 3.0 instead of a real signal, and why "Actifs transactionnels (jour)" read ~0.3% (denominator inflated by dead anonymous rows). Fixed with `WHERE p.phone IS NOT NULL AND p.phone <> ''`, the same "real user" filter already used in `migration_v143.sql`/`v145.sql`/`v149.sql`. |
| `db/migration_v168.sql` | Reverses part of `migration_v155.sql`'s "add once, never delete" transaction-proof design on direct product request — see "Transaction proof images" above. Adds `proof_attached_by`/`proof_attached_at` (nullable) to `capital_injections`/`expenses`/`purchase_orders`; `attach_transaction_proof` now stamps both. New `delete_transaction_proof(kind, id)` RPC: only the exact person who attached the proof, only within 24h of attaching, both checked server-side — nulls the `proof_image_*` trio plus the two new columns, leaves the Storage object orphaned (no DELETE policy on `transaction-proofs`, unchanged from v155). |
| `db/migration_v169.sql` | Adds `leave_or_delete_business(p_business_id)` — one RPC backing a single "Quitter {business}" button for every role, replacing an earlier admin-only `delete_own_business` (written the same day, never deployed, directly superseded rather than layered on top). Decides leave-vs-delete-vs-refuse itself based on caller role and whether other members exist — see "Parametres screen" above. |
| `db/migration_v170.sql` | Account deletion becomes a 30-day, re-verified, cancellable request instead of an immediate one — see "Parametres screen" above. Adds `profiles.pending_deletion_at`; `delete_my_account()` keeps its name/signature/self-service `auth.uid()` gate but now only schedules (stamps the column) instead of deleting, and its admin/other-members block names every blocking business explicitly (`string_agg`) instead of a generic "un commerce"; new `finalize_account_deletion(p_user_id)` (service-role only) holds the actual cascade-delete body the old `delete_my_account()` used to run inline, re-checks the admin/other-members gate at finalize time, and no-ops (never raises) for a row that's no longer due — so a stray/duplicate cron call can't do anything and one bad row can't abort a batch. |
| `db/migration_v171.sql` | Schedules `process-scheduled-account-deletions` daily (04:00 UTC) via `pg_cron`, same `patron_cron_secret` Vault pattern as `migration_v141.sql`/`v144.sql`/`v162.sql`/`v164.sql`/`v166.sql`. Daily, not hourly — a 30-day deadline has no need for hour-level precision. |
| `db/migration_v172.sql` | Fixes a real cash-tracking gap — see "Purchase orders — cash_on_hand gap" below. Adds `create_purchase_order()` (atomic PO+lines+debt+payment RPC replacing createCommande's old 3-step client sequence), nullable `po_id` on `supplier_debts`/`supplier_payments`, and check 84 (`run_supplier_payment_checks()`, following the same additive-function pattern `migration_v158.sql`/`v159.sql` established for checks 81-83 rather than reproducing `run_display_checks()`'s body). |
| `db/migration_v174.sql` | Replaces the Founder Dashboard's whole metric set — see "Founder Dashboard" below. Adds the `growth_metrics` view (business-level: `signup_at`, `activated_72h`, `week1_retained`, `week4_retained`, three-state not boolean, demo businesses excluded) and `get_founder_growth_stats()` (activation rate, week1/week4 retention, weekly transacting shops / North Star). `founderMetrics.ts` and its `get_founder_activity_raw()`-backed panel are dropped from the app (the RPC itself stays deployed, just unused). |
| `db/migration_v175.sql` | Adds a 5th column, `total_real_businesses`, to `get_founder_growth_stats()` — backs the North Star card's rate-based health color (see "Card health colors" under "Founder Dashboard" below). `DROP FUNCTION` first since a return-column addition changes the function's return type, which plain `CREATE OR REPLACE` can't do. |
| `db/migration_v182.sql` | Security fix (2026-09-27 audit): `submit_sale()` inserted the client-supplied `p_total_amount` verbatim, never reconciled against `SUM(cart qty × unit_price)` — a modified client bypassing the app's own UI could record a real stock deduction against an arbitrary total, only caught the next morning by reconciliation check #9. Fixed by deriving `total_amount` from the cart itself, same posture `edit_sale` already has. Per-line `unit_price` stays fully trusted (negotiated pricing is intentional); only the aggregate total is no longer a separately-asserted number. Covered by a new integration test. |
| `db/migration_v183.sql` | Drops `otp_codes` (`migration_v8.sql`) — a WhatsApp-OTP table superseded since `migration_v9.sql` by the `phone_verifications` flow, whose only two readers/writers (`verify-phone-otp`, `send-whatsapp-otp` edge functions) turned out to have never actually been deployed to production at all (confirmed via `supabase functions list` before dropping). Pure cleanup, zero live impact. |
| `db/migration_v184.sql` | Removes the Alpha WhatsApp reminder feature entirely (product decision, not a bug) — see "Alpha WhatsApp reminder" below for what it used to do and everything this drops. |
| `db/migration_v185.sql` | Security fix (2026-09-27 audit, real enforcement not UI hiding, direct product decision): a vendeur could read `products.cost_price`/`product_variants.cost_price` at the RLS level — see "Vendeur cost_price gate" under Store pattern below for the full mechanism (base-table SELECT policy now excludes vendeur; `get_products_for_vendeur()`/`get_variants_for_vendeur()` are their only read path, cost_price hardcoded to `0::bigint`). None of the ~50 SQL functions that read/write `cost_price` needed touching — all `SECURITY DEFINER`, bypass RLS entirely. |
| `db/migration_v186.sql` | Checklist 1.7's "inviter can see redeemed-by/when" (audit-trail feature, not a vulnerability fix — deliberately scoped down to just this piece, the rest of 1.7 stays deferred). Adds `invite_codes.redeemed_by`/`redeemed_at`, stamped by `join_business()` on a successful join. Two columns, not a redemption table — every real invite code has `max_uses = 1` in practice (`stores/equipe.ts`'s `createCode()` always sets it), so code-row and redemption are already 1:1. Depends on a client-side behavior change made alongside it, not in this file — see `fetchCodes()` below. |
| `db/migration_v187.sql` | Security fix, checklist 1.8 fuzz pass on `submit_sale()`, two real bugs found and confirmed against a live Postgres instance before fixing (see "Fuzz-testing `submit_sale`" below for the full story): a negative cart quantity silently *added* stock instead of being rejected, and a cart line's `product_id` was never checked against `p_business_id` anywhere in the function, letting one business deduct stock from a completely unrelated business's product. Fixed with an upfront validate-the-whole-cart pass (positive qty, product/variant ownership) before any insert happens, plus `business_id` filters added directly to the cost_price lookups and stock-deduction UPDATEs as defense-in-depth. |
| `db/migration_v188.sql` | Security fix, full sweep of every remaining SECURITY DEFINER RPC touching stock/money for the same unscoped-lookup pattern `migration_v187.sql` found in `submit_sale` (see "Sweeping every SECURITY DEFINER RPC" below for the full story) — six real bugs found and fixed: `confirm_reception()` could overwrite another business's product `sale_price` or splice a foreign product/variant into a purchase order; `create_purchase_order()` had the identical unchecked `po_lines` insert; `receive_purchase_order()`'s variant-stock AVCO path never constrained `business_id` (the parallel plain-product path in the same function already did); `cancel_sale()`'s stock-restore loop had the same gap (lower severity, defense-in-depth); `create_product_with_stock()` trusted a caller-supplied `business_id`/`created_by` on the stock_move row instead of the already-validated ones; `upsert_product_variants()` — wrongly assessed safe on a first reading pass, caught only by the live test — could create a real `product_variants` row cross-referencing the caller's own `business_id` with a foreign `product_id`. Also drops a stale, unreachable 2-arg `receive_purchase_order(uuid, uuid)` overload left over from before `confirm_reception` existed (harmless in production — nothing calls it that way — but it broke a test with a PostgREST overload-ambiguity error). Ten other definer functions (`edit_sale`, `record_client_payment`, `pay_supplier_debt`, `record_injection`, `edit_injection`, `record_withdrawal`, `request_payout`, `confirm_payout`, `attach_transaction_proof`, `delete_transaction_proof`) were read and live-tested and are confirmed safe already. |
| `db/migration_v189.sql` | First-run hero action — see "First-run hero action" below. Adds `businesses.first_run_hero_completed_at` (nullable timestamp, backfilled to `now()` for every pre-existing row in the same migration so the gate never appears retroactively on an established shop) and updates `join_business()` to stamp it the instant anyone joins a business whose flag is still unset — the server-side guarantee that a manager/vendeur/investisseur can never see this gate, since they only ever arrive via this function. |
| `db/migration_v190.sql` | Quick sale redesign — see "Quick sale redesign" below.
