Adds `submit_quick_sale(business_id, seller_id, unit_price, qty DEFAULT 1, label DEFAULT NULL, idempotency_key)`, a SECURITY DEFINER RPC for the "Vente rapide" mode: same is_system-placeholder-product pattern `submit_carnet_debt()` already uses for credit debts, mirrored for a paid cash sale with a real qty and an optional free-text label (its own separate placeholder product, "Vente rapide", not shared with the debt side's "Solde reporté"). Also fixes `get_best_sellers()` (unchanged signature) to exclude `is_system` products — a real, pre-existing gap (credit debts have been polluting "meilleurs produits" with "Solde reporté" since that feature shipped) that this migration would otherwise double by adding a second is_system placeholder into the same unfiltered ranking. Revised in place (signature changed from an initial amount-only draft) before ever being applied to any database — see "Quick sale redesign" below for why that revision, and the fact that this migration sat unapplied in production for a time after its dependent OTA already shipped, both mattered. |
| `db/migration_v191.sql` | Adds `first_sale_at` to `get_dashboard_kpis()` (unchanged signature, `jsonb` return so no `DROP FUNCTION` needed) — see "Dashboard 'Bienvenue' line" below. Lifetime `MIN(paid_at) WHERE status='paye'`, business-wide (bypasses caller RLS same as every other field this function returns), deliberately excluding `status='credit'` debts — backs Accueil's one-time "Première vente notée ✓" acknowledgment, replacing the old, wrong `businesses.created_at`-based "Bienvenue" trigger. |

(Note: `migration_v58.sql` through `v75.sql`, `v77.sql` through `v98.sql`, `v100.sql` through `v102.sql`, `v105.sql` through `v108.sql`, `v110.sql`, `v157.sql` through `v160.sql`, and `v173.sql` exist in `db/` but are undocumented here and unrelated to this entry — out of scope. `migration_v111.sql` adds `run_display_checks()` / `refresh_reconciliation_run()`, patched by `v114.sql` above.)

**`REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM PUBLIC` does not lock a function to `service_role` on this stack** — found via new integration tests written for `v137`/`v138`/`v139` before they'd ever shipped (a plain `authenticated` test client could call all three with no error). Supabase's local/hosted Postgres grants `EXECUTE` on newly created functions directly to `anon`/`authenticated`/`service_role` via default privileges, not merely inherited through `PUBLIC`, so revoking from `PUBLIC` alone leaves those three roles' own direct grants untouched. The fix (now applied in all three files) is `REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated;` — every prior service-role-only RPC in this codebase (`increment_unread_notifications` itself included, once fixed) needs the explicit role list, not just `PUBLIC`. `migration_v44.sql`/`v45.sql`'s existing `REVOKE ... FROM authenticated` (there for a different reason — killing an old function overload) happened to already use the correct form; it just wasn't obvious that was load-bearing until this bug surfaced.

**A missing `--no-verify-jwt` deploy flag silently killed every cron-triggered push for 3 weeks (found 2026-08-24, a stronger case of the same blast radius as the Alpha WhatsApp `CRON_SECRET`/Vault mismatch above).** `send-activation-reminders`, `send-second-action-reminders`, and `send-revenue-milestone-reminders` were all deployed with `supabase functions deploy` and no `--no-verify-jwt` flag — unlike `djomi-checkout`/`djomi-webhook`/`send-reconciliation-report`, which already carry it for the same reason: a cron job authenticates with only an `x-cron-secret` header, no `Authorization: Bearer <jwt>`, so Supabase's platform gateway rejected every call with a flat `401 UNAUTHORIZED_NO_AUTH_HEADER` **before the function's own code — and its correct `x-cron-secret` check — ever ran.** Worse, `dispatch-notification` itself (the shared function all of these route through, including the already-"live" `activation_nudge_1`/`activation_nudge_2` events) had the exact same gap, so even a correctly-deployed caller's request still died one hop later. Net effect: the hourly `patron-activation-reminders` and `patron-second-action-reminders` cron jobs had been "running" successfully (pg_cron only sees that `net.http_post` returned *a* response, not that it was a 401) since 2026-08-01 while delivering **zero** real pushes the entire time — a purely infrastructure-layer failure invisible to `tsc`, the test suite, and a code read, since the deployed function source was correct throughout. Diagnosed by manually replaying the exact `net.http_post` call each cron job makes (`net._http_response` gives the real status/body a few seconds later) and comparing against `djomi-sweep` as a known-good control (200, not 401) to confirm the technique before trusting the result. Fixed by redeploying all four functions with `--no-verify-jwt`; safe because each already re-implements its own auth in code (`dispatch-notification`'s `isCronCall` secret check plus a real `Authorization: Bearer` + `userClient.auth.getUser()` path for non-cron events) — identical shape to the Djomi functions, so disabling the platform-level gate doesn't remove any actual protection. **Any new cron- or webhook-triggered edge function must be deployed with `--no-verify-jwt` from its first deploy, and must be smoke-tested with a real `net.http_post` call against the linked project (not just "the deploy succeeded") before considering it live** — a clean `supabase functions deploy` and an active `cron.job` row both look identical whether or not the function is actually reachable. Two real one-shot notification flags (`highest_revenue_milestone_cents` ratchets, one `second_action_nudge_sent_at`) were consumed by RPC calls made while diagnosing this before the fix was complete, with no notification ever delivered — recovered by manually replaying the missed `dispatch-notification` call for each affected business once the fix was confirmed, using the exact payload the RPC would have produced.

**`--no-verify-jwt` is not a persistent setting — it silently resets to the default (JWT required) on every redeploy that doesn't repeat the flag (found 2026-09-27, during the security-audit error-message fix below).** A routine redeploy of 15 edge functions (a code-only change to their catch-all error handling, `supabase functions deploy <name>` with no flags) silently flipped `verify_jwt` back to `true` on every one of the 9 cron-secret-only functions in that batch — `dispatch-notification`, `learn-digest-send-hours`, `process-scheduled-account-deletions`, `send-alpha-quota-reminders`, `send-daily-digest`, `send-revenue-milestone-reminders`, `send-reconciliation-report`, `send-second-action-reminders`, `send-activation-reminders` — silently reintroducing the exact 3-week outage documented above, this time from a completely unrelated code change. Caught immediately (not after weeks) by smoke-testing one of them with a bare `curl -X POST` right after deploy and getting the platform's `UNAUTHORIZED_NO_AUTH_HEADER` back instead of the function's own JSON — the same "verify the real call" discipline the original fix called for, just applied proactively this time instead of reactively. Fixed by redeploying all 9 with `--no-verify-jwt` explicitly, then re-confirmed each one returns its own `{"error":"Non autorisé"}` (reaching the code's cron-secret check) rather than the platform error. **Lesson: `--no-verify-jwt` must be passed on every single `supabase functions deploy` call for these functions, forever, including redeploys that have nothing to do with auth** — there is no way to set it once and have it stick. Before redeploying *any* cron/webhook-secret-authenticated function for *any* reason, redeploy with the flag and smoke-test it, even if the change looks unrelated to auth.

**Security audit hardening (2026-07-14), edge-function-level (no migration).** Alongside `migration_v150.sql` above:
- **OTP codes now use a CSPRNG, not `Math.random()`** (`create-phone-verification`, `send-email-otp` — both had `Math.floor(100000 + Math.random()*900000)`). A shared `generateOtpCode()` in each function rejection-samples `crypto.getRandomValues` for an unbiased 6-digit code. The "don't use `crypto.getRandomValues()`, Hermes lacks it" rule is **client-only** (invite codes) — it never applied to these Deno edge functions.
- **Account-recovery OTP tokens are no longer logged in plaintext** (`recover-by-email`, `link-recovery-email`). Both used to `console.log('verif=', JSON.stringify(verif))` and `console.error('token mismatch stored=', verif.token, ...)`, putting valid in-window recovery codes into edge logs (readable by anyone with dashboard/log access → account takeover during the 10-min window). Now log only `verificationId` + `status`/`rowFound`, never the token.
- **`dispatch-notification` validates `business_id` is a plain UUID** before it's string-interpolated into the partnership `.or()` filter — defense-in-depth against any filter-metacharacter injection reaching that expression.
- **`djomi-webhook` now re-verifies against Djomi before activating** (it previously activated straight off the signed payload, contradicting its own comment) and uses a constant-time HMAC-signature compare (`timingSafeEqualHex` in `_shared/djomi.ts`). It's still the dormant backstop; the poll path was already correct.
- **`djomi-checkout` POST is IP rate-limited** (15/hour, reusing `ip_verification_attempts` with `endpoint='djomi_checkout'`) — it's an unauthenticated public endpoint, so this caps payment-creation spam against Djomi's API.
- **Deferred (not fixed): `recovery_email` is readable by co-members.** The teammate `profiles` SELECT policy is row-level, so a co-member can craft a PostgREST query for another member's `recovery_email`. A clean fix needs the column split into a self-only table (or a column REVOKE), but `loadSession` does `profiles.select('*')` (auth.ts) so a blind REVOKE breaks login, and splitting touches the account-recovery flow — disproportionate risk for a LOW, attacker-only, non-access-granting read (recovery still requires the email OTP). Left as a tracked follow-up rather than destabilize login/recovery.

`discount_amount` convention: `total_amount` always stores catalog total; `discount_amount + amount_paid = total_amount` for a closed discounted sale.

`lib/db.ts` — SQLite local migrations (auto-run on app start, versioned in `_migrations` table). Current schema version: 7 (sync_queue).

### Native IAP paywall (`lib/purchases.ts` + `src/components/PaywallScreen.tsx` + `supabase/functions/revenuecat-webhook/`)

`app/(app)/_layout.tsx`'s gate (`isSubscriptionExpired`) previously hardcoded `return false` — the app was free during early access, and the real paywall used `Linking.openURL` to open Stripe Payment Links from inside the app, which is a Guideline 3.1.1 violation (a digital subscription unlocking in-app functionality must go through platform IAP, not a web checkout). Re-launched via RevenueCat (`react-native-purchases`) instead: `PaywallScreen.tsx` calls `Purchases.purchasePackage()`, and `supabase/functions/revenuecat-webhook/index.ts` (mirroring `stripe-webhook`'s structure) updates `businesses.subscription_status`/`subscription_expires_at`/`payment_provider` server-side on `INITIAL_PURCHASE`/`RENEWAL`/`EXPIRATION` events. `Purchases.logIn(business.id)` is called from `stores/auth.ts` so RevenueCat's `app_user_id` always equals `businesses.id` — same identity convention the old Stripe flow used via `client_reference_id`.

**As of Alpha's launch (`migration_v133.sql`), `isSubscriptionExpired`/`hasBonusAccess` no longer gate the whole app.** `app/(app)/_layout.tsx` used to swap the entire `<Stack>` for `<PaywallScreen>` once a business's trial lapsed — that forced block was removed; every other screen (Rapports, Catalogue, etc.) is free forever now, for every role. The same `subscription_status`/`trial_ends_at`/`bonus_access_until` fields are repurposed to mean "has Alpha access" instead of "has app access" — same RevenueCat webhook, same columns, just read from a different place (`has_ai_access()` in SQL, checked inside `app/(app)/alpha/index.tsx` for every role, not just the administrateur the old whole-app gate special-cased). `TrialBanner` and the manual `showPaywallManually` preview path stay in `_layout.tsx` as a soft upsell surface, re-pointed at Alpha specifically. See "Alpha" below.

**`lib/purchases.ts` is a safe no-op until `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_API_KEY_IOS`/`_ANDROID` are set** (no RevenueCat project/App Store Connect/Play Console products exist yet at the time this was built) — every exported function checks `isPurchasesConfigured()` first, so the rest of the app (trial, gate logic) works fine in the meantime, and `PaywallScreen` falls back to hardcoded display prices when `Purchases.getOfferings()` has nothing configured. **This is stale as a statement about the current environment** — `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_API_KEY_IOS` *is* set as a real EAS production secret (confirmed 2026-07-17 while debugging the crash below), so `configured` is `true` in production and every call in this file actually reaches the live RevenueCat SDK, not a no-op. Don't assume "not configured yet" without checking `eas env:list --environment production` — that assumption directly contributed to the bug below going unnoticed.

**Crash on logout: `Purchases.logOut()` on an anonymous RevenueCat user aborts the whole app natively, not just a rejected promise (found 2026-07-17, actually fixed the same day after a first attempt failed — see below).** `logoutPurchases()` originally wrapped `await Purchases.logOut()` in try/catch, with a comment acknowledging RevenueCat's documented behavior — "throws if no user is currently logged in" — as something safe to swallow. In production that throw doesn't reliably surface as a JS promise rejection: the real crash log (pulled from TestFlight's Analytics Data `.ips` report) showed `SIGABRT` on the `com.meta.react.turbomodulemanager.queue` thread, an uncaught `NSException` rethrown from inside `facebook::react::ObjCTurboModule::performVoidMethodInvocation` — a native-side abort that happens before the promise machinery ever constructs a rejection, so no JS `catch` block can intercept it. **This bug was masked by an unrelated, earlier crash** (see the Rules-of-Hooks bug under "Routing" above) that threw synchronously and immediately on every logout tap — the app never lived long enough to reach this async call until that first bug was fixed, at which point this one surfaced as "still crashing" on the very next OTA.

**First fix attempt didn't work — `Purchases.isAnonymous()` goes through the identical crash-prone native bridge as `logOut()`.** The first fix called `isAnonymous()` before `logOut()` and skipped the call entirely when already anonymous — reasoning that avoiding the call in the state that throws beats catching it after the fact. Shipped via OTA, confirmed live on the correct fingerprint, and the crash still happened — a fresh TestFlight `.ips` pull showed the identical `performVoidMethodInvocation`/`SIGABRT` signature. `isAnonymous()` is itself a native TurboModule call bridged the same "void"-method way as `logOut()`, so guarding one crash-prone call with another crash-prone call just moved the crash one step earlier instead of preventing it — and since this class of failure can't be caught by JS `try/catch` at all (see above), no amount of conditional guarding around it can help. **Actual fix: removed the RevenueCat `logOut()`/`isAnonymous()` calls from the interactive logout flow entirely.** They were never load-bearing for correctness — `loginPurchases(businessId)` already runs unconditionally on every subsequent login/session-restore (5 call sites in `stores/auth.ts`), and RevenueCat's `logIn()` is designed to safely switch `app_user_id` on its own without requiring an explicit `logOut()` first. The lesson generalizes beyond this one bug: if a class of native call is confirmed uncatchable and crash-prone, a guard *around* it (check state, then conditionally call) is not a fix unless the guard itself is provably safe — the guard call must not be another instance of the same crash-prone bridge path. Prefer removing the call entirely over adding a pre-check, when the call turns out not to be necessary in the first place.

**A second, independent near-miss surfaced while shipping this second fix: `package.json` devDependency-only changes silently shift the computed fingerprint hash, even though nothing in `devDependencies` is ever bundled into the app.** Earlier the same day, `eslint`/`eslint-plugin-react-hooks`/`@typescript-eslint/parser` were added to `devDependencies` (see the Rules-of-Hooks fix above) and committed. Re-running `expo-updates fingerprint:generate` on that exact commit produces a *different* hash than the one actually installed on the production TestFlight build — meaning an OTA published from that commit as-is would have gone out under a fingerprint matching no real installed device, silently reaching nobody, exactly the "wasted publish" failure mode the fingerprint policy exists to prevent for native changes. Caught only by explicitly re-fingerprinting the exact tree about to be published and diffing it against the known-good production hash before running `eas update` — not something `tsc`/tests/consistency-checks would ever catch, since the JS is 100% correct either way. **Any future `package.json` change — including devDependencies, scripts, or anything else in the file — must be fingerprint-verified before an OTA-only push**, not assumed safe just because "it's not a real dependency." When in doubt, temporarily revert `package.json` to the last known-good state for the publish step only, then restore it afterward (exactly the workaround used here) rather than trusting that a JS-only diff is automatically OTA-safe.

**Referral bonus (`bonus_access_until`, distinct from `subscription_expires_at`).** "Inviter un ami" in Paramètres grants 30 bonus days to both businesses the first time a referred business converts to a real paid subscription (checked in the webhook's `INITIAL_PURCHASE` handler, guarded by `payment_provider IS NULL` on the referred business so a later renewal never re-triggers it). The bonus is deliberately written to its own `bonus_access_until` column, not `subscription_expires_at` — that field is fully owned by RevenueCat's renewal events and gets overwritten on every `RENEWAL`, which would silently erase the bonus on the referred business's very next billing cycle if it lived there instead. `isSubscriptionExpired()` checks both fields independently so the bonus stacks on top of whatever IAP state exists rather than fighting it.

**`resolve_referral_code(p_code)` is SECURITY DEFINER** because a brand-new user creating their first business isn't a member of the referrer's business yet, so the normal `is_member(id)` SELECT policy on `businesses` would otherwise block looking up the referrer's id from their code — same shape as `validate_invite_code`. The actual write (`referred_by_business_id`) on the new business is a plain client-side update, allowed by the existing "Administrateurs: modifier leur commerce" policy since the new user is that business's own admin.

Jest's plain `ts-jest` setup (no `jest-expo` preset — see "Integration tests" below for why) doesn't transform `node_modules`, and `react-native-purchases` (like `react-native` itself) ships unparsed Flow/ESM syntax — `@/lib/purchases` is mocked via `__mocks__/purchases.js` in `jest.config.js`'s `moduleNameMapper`, placed *before* the generic `'^@/(.*)$'` entry since Jest uses the first matching key.

**App Store rejection, Guideline 3.1.2(c) (2026-07-15, fixed same day, JS-only).** Submission 1.0.4 (15) was rejected: "missing a functional link to the Terms of Use (EULA)" — `PaywallScreen.tsx`'s footer only ever had a Privacy link (`PRIVACY_URL`), never a Terms/EULA one, and there was no terms document to link to at all (only `privacy.html`/`support.html` existed at repo root). Apple requires four things *inside* the purchase-flow UI for an auto-renewable subscription: title, length, price, and functional links to both the privacy policy and the Terms of Use — only the last was actually missing here (title/length/price weren't flagged). Fixed by adding `terms.html` at repo root (same static-HTML-via-GitHub-Pages mechanism as `privacy.html`/`support.html` — see "Alpha WhatsApp reminder"'s `abonnement/index.html` note for how that deploy works), and wiring a `TERMS_URL` link into `PaywallScreen.tsx`'s footer in **both** the full-screen and `inline` variants (the inline upsell card is a real purchase surface too — the mid-chat quota-exhaustion path, not just the full-screen preview), plus an explicit "Renouvellement automatique chaque mois. Résiliable à tout moment." line under the price for extra safety on the "length" requirement. Also added the same Terms link next to the existing Privacy link in Paramètres → À propos for consistency (not required by the rejection, just kept the two in sync). **This alone doesn't close the loop** — Apple's message also requires the App Store Connect metadata to carry a Terms of Use / EULA reference: since this is a *custom* EULA (not Apple's standard one), it has to be pasted into App Store Connect's own License Agreement field (App Information → General → License Agreement → Custom License Agreement), not just linked from the App Description. The code fix here is JS-only (no native module, no Info.plist/config-plugin change), so per the `runtimeVersion: 'fingerprint'` policy above it ships via `eas update` to the already-submitted 1.0.4 (15) binary — no new `eas build` needed before replying to Apple / resubmitting.

### Djomi out-of-app subscription (`supabase/functions/djomi-checkout/`, `supabase/functions/djomi-webhook/`, `db/migration_v140.sql`)

A second path to activate Alpha Pro, alongside RevenueCat, for Guinean merchants paying with Orange Money — added because Apple/Google IAP has no confirmed local payment method for a Guinea storefront, and Djomi (`api.djomy.africa`) provides Orange Money mobile money checkout. This is **not an in-app purchase flow** and must never become one.

**Why it's structured this way:** Apple Guideline 3.1.1/3.1.3 (the same rule RevenueCat's introduction was built to satisfy — see "Native IAP paywall" above) bans any button, link, or other call-to-action *inside the app* that points to a non-IAP purchasing mechanism. As of 2026-07, Apple only exempts the US/EU/South Korea storefronts from this, and Google Play's equivalent alternative-billing program (live since 2026-06-30) only covers US/EEA/UK, expanding to AU/JP/KR through 2026 and the rest of the world (including Guinea) not until ~Sep 2027. A Guinea-storefront user is covered by neither exemption today. The one channel that stays compliant regardless of storefront is one the app itself never touches: a subscription sold entirely outside the app (no in-app link, no in-app mention), recognized after the fact by reading the same `subscription_status`/`payment_provider` columns RevenueCat already writes — the same pattern the Kindle app long used for book purchases.

- **`djomi-checkout`** is a standalone, unauthenticated web page (GET serves HTML, POST starts a payment) — deliberately never linked from anywhere inside the Patron app. It must only ever be reached through channels the app doesn't control (a WhatsApp message, a shared link, word of mouth). The moment any in-app UI links to this page, the whole reason it's compliant disappears.
- Because there's no Supabase session on an unauthenticated web page, the merchant identifies themselves by typing the same phone number their Patron account uses. `resolve_business_for_djomi_checkout(p_phone)` (migration_v140.sql, `service_role`-only) resolves that to the business they administer via a digits-only match on `profiles.phone`, mirroring the comparison style already used by `src/utils/founder.ts`. It returns `NULL` for both "no match" and "ambiguous match" so the page shows one generic error either way, never leaking which case occurred.
- **Two separate phone fields, not one reused twice.** The checkout page has a "Numéro utilisé sur Patron" field (identifies which business is subscribing, via the RPC above) and a distinct "Numéro Orange Money" field (the `payerNumber` Djomi actually charges) — an earlier version used the same field for both, which quietly broke the common real-world case of paying with a different Orange line than the one tied to the merchant's Patron login (a family member's or employee's number, or simply a non-Orange login number). The Orange field is prefilled from the Patron field on blur (only while still empty) purely as a convenience for the common case where they're the same number — always overridable for the "pay with someone else's Orange number" case. A plain (non-boxed) yellow hint under the Orange field states this explicitly, since Djomi/Orange Money only works with genuine Orange-network numbers — no carrier-prefix validation is done client- or server-side; Djomi's own API is the source of truth for whether a number is really Orange Money-enabled.
- **Confirmation is by polling, not by webhook — this is load-bearing, not a preference.** The Djomi account currently in use is shared with a friend's project, and Djomi's dashboard only allows one webhook URL per account; his project already claims that slot, so Djomi has no way to call `djomi-webhook`'s URL under the current setup. Instead, `djomi-checkout` polls Djomi's own `GET /v1/payments/{transactionId}` directly (`?verify=1&ref=...`) every 5s for up to ~2 minutes right after the merchant returns from paying, via `verifyDjomiTransaction()` in `_shared/djomi.ts`. `djomi-webhook` is kept deployed only as a backstop in case the webhook-slot situation ever changes (own account, or Djomi adds multi-webhook support) — if it never fires, nothing breaks, since polling already does the same job independently.
- **`djomi_pending_payments` (migration_v140.sql) exists to solve a chicken-and-egg problem, and doubles as the trust boundary for the poll endpoint.** Djomi's payment-creation call needs a `returnUrl` up front, but its `transactionId` (needed to later call the status endpoint) is only known from that same call's *response* — too late to embed in the URL already sent. Fixed by generating `reference` ourselves before the call, inserting a row keyed by it, embedding only `reference` in `returnUrl`, then updating the row with `transaction_id` once Djomi responds. This table is also why a tampered `?ref=` query param on the poll endpoint can't be used to fake a payment or redirect activation to a different business: the `transaction_id` actually checked against Djomi always comes from our own row for that exact reference, never from anything the client supplies directly.
- **Neither path trusts a webhook/payload's own status field** — Djomi's own integration guidance is explicit that this alone isn't sufficient proof of payment; both `djomi-checkout`'s poll and `djomi-webhook` re-verify via Djomi's transaction-status endpoint before writing anything. `djomi-webhook`'s signature verification uses `X-Webhook-Signature: v1:<hex>` (HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body with the client secret) — same shape as `stripe-webhook`'s signature check, different scheme.
- Djomi's exact webhook/payment-creation JSON field names weren't available from public docs at the time this was written (their developer docs are auth-gated, returned a 403 on fetch) — both functions try several plausible key paths and log the raw payload/response on every call specifically so the first real transaction can be used to correct them if needed.
- A successful Djomi payment sets `subscription_status: 'active'`, `subscription_expires_at: now + 30 days`, `payment_provider: 'djomi'` — the same fields RevenueCat's webhook writes, so `has_ai_access()`/`isSubscriptionExpired()` need no changes at all to recognize it. Unlike RevenueCat, Djomi has no recurring-subscription concept here; each payment is a flat one-month grant, and a lapsed merchant simply revisits `djomi-checkout` to pay again. `activateDjomiSubscription()` is idempotent, so a poll retry and a (currently dormant) webhook landing for the same transaction can never double-write anything harmful.
- **`djomi-sweep` closes the "merchant pays but never returns to the tab" gap** — the one case neither the poll nor the (dormant) webhook covers on their own. Cron job (hourly, scheduled via `migration_v141.sql`): re-checks every `djomi_pending_payments` row with `resolved_at IS NULL AND transaction_id IS NOT NULL`, bounded to the last 24h (`listUnresolvedPendingPayments` in `_shared/djomi.ts`) so a genuinely abandoned attempt from days ago doesn't get polled against Djomi forever. Same `x-cron-secret`/`CRON_SECRET` auth pattern as `send-alpha-quota-reminders`.
- **`confirmDjomiPayment()` (`_shared/djomi.ts`) is the single entry point that activates a subscription from a Djomi payment** — used identically by the poll, the webhook, and the sweep, so there's exactly one place that decides what "confirmed" means and exactly one place that marks a `djomi_pending_payments` row `resolved_at`. All three are idempotent against each other (activation always writes the same fields; `resolved_at` is a no-op the second time), so it doesn't matter which of the three ends up being the one that actually confirms a given payment.
- **Not yet built**: the proactive WhatsApp outreach that tells a merchant this page exists (a trial-ending / quota-exhausted reminder, sent by a backend job — never triggered by an in-app tap, for the same compliance reason `djomi-checkout` itself is never linked in-app). Until that exists, this path has no real acquisition channel. Sent via **Meta's WhatsApp Business Platform directly, not Twilio** (unlike the existing OTP flow) — requires a Meta-approved message template first, a manual step only Sebastiao can do via WhatsApp Manager.
- **Personalized-link mode (`?biz=<token>`).** `businesses.djomi_checkout_token` (`migration_v142.sql`) is a per-business opaque token, generated the same way `referral_code` already is. A link like `patron.kolilink.com/abonnement?biz=<token>` resolves the business from the token alone via `resolve_business_by_djomi_checkout_token` — the "Numéro utilisé sur Patron" field is hidden entirely in this mode, and the merchant only ever sees the Orange Money field. Originally built for the (now-removed, see "Alpha WhatsApp reminder" below) WhatsApp reminder's template button; kept as general-purpose infrastructure for sharing a personalized link through any channel. The generic, no-token URL remains the fallback for anyone who reaches the page some other way (forwarded link, bookmark, typed from memory) — manual phone entry via `resolve_business_for_djomi_checkout` (`migration_v140.sql`) is unchanged for that path. The token is deliberately **not treated as a high-security secret** — same posture as the phone-lookup path: guessing someone else's token only risks paying for the wrong business's subscription, not any data exposure.

### Alpha WhatsApp reminder — removed (2026-09-27)

Used to be a daily cron (`send-alpha-whatsapp-reminder`, `migration_v143/144/145/148.sql`) that messaged an administrateur on WhatsApp via Meta's Graph API once they'd hit the free Alpha cap on 3+ days in a week, pointing them at `djomi-checkout`'s personalized link. Removed entirely on direct product decision — not a pause, a real removal — because messaging merchants to push a paid upgrade isn't something the product wants to do. `migration_v184.sql` unschedules the cron and drops `get_and_mark_alpha_whatsapp_candidates()`, `alpha_whatsapp_reminder_eligible_now()`, `record_alpha_whatsapp_consent()`, the `alpha_whatsapp_reminders_sent` table, and `profiles.alpha_whatsapp_consent`; the edge function and its in-app consent prompt (`app/(app)/alpha/index.tsx`'s "On vous facilite ça ?" `AppSheet`, `stores/alpha.ts`'s `checkWhatsappConsentEligibility`/`recordWhatsappConsent`) are deleted from the client too. `djomi-checkout`'s personalized-link mode (`djomi_checkout_token`, `migration_v142.sql`) and the branded `abonnement/index.html` page are untouched — those are general-purpose infrastructure for reaching the checkout page by any channel, not specific to this one WhatsApp trigger, and the generic no-token URL was always the primary path anyway.

### Djomi branded checkout page (`abonnement/index.html`)

- **Static mirror of `djomi-checkout`'s page, served at `https://patron.kolilink.com/abonnement` via GitHub Pages** (`.github/workflows/static.yml`, `actions/deploy-pages@v5`, uploads the whole repo on every push to `main` — same mechanism that already serves `privacy.html`/`support.html`). **This repo has no server-side hosting at all** — no Cloudflare Pages, no Pages Functions, nothing that can proxy a request. An earlier version of this got that wrong (assumed Cloudflare Pages from a since-misleading commit message on `public/_redirects`, which is actually inert dead weight — GitHub Pages doesn't support that file either) and shipped a Cloudflare Pages Function that would have done nothing and merely published its own source as a static file. The fix: `abonnement/index.html` is genuinely static HTML/CSS/JS whose `fetch()` calls go directly, cross-origin, to `djomi-checkout`'s real `*.supabase.co` URL (already `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`) — there is no proxy, the browser talks straight to Supabase, only the page shell is served from the branded domain.
- **The post-payment redirect can also land back on the branded page**, not just the initial visit: `abonnement/index.html` sends `returnOrigin: location.origin` on the payment-creation POST, and `djomi-checkout` only honors it against a fixed `ALLOWED_RETURN_ORIGINS = ['https://patron.kolilink.com']` allowlist — never an arbitrary client-supplied origin, since that value becomes Djomi's `returnUrl` and accepting anything the client sends would be an open-redirect hole in a payment flow. Falls back to the function's own URL if `returnOrigin` isn't recognized (e.g. hitting `djomi-checkout` directly rather than through the static page still works standalone).
- **`abonnement/index.html`'s markup/copy has to be kept in sync with `djomi-checkout`'s embedded `PAGE_HTML` by hand** — there's no shared template between a Deno Edge Function and a plain static GitHub Pages file. If the price, plan copy, or flow changes, both need editing.

### Alpha — AI business advisor (`stores/alpha.ts`, `supabase/functions/alpha-chat/`, `db/migration_v133.sql` + `migration_v134.sql`)

Pure conversational Q&A — a merchant asks about their own sales/stock/cash and gets advice grounded in real numbers, via the same Groq (`llama-3.3-70b-versatile`) integration `generate-support-draft` already uses. No proactive jobs, no push notifications, no voice/transcription (Groq's Whisper is excellent for French but genuinely weak for Guinea's local languages — Susu, Malinké, Pular — an unsolved industry-wide gap, not a vendor choice; deferred, not built). Originally shipped as "Mystic" — renamed to "Alpha" one migration later (`migration_v134.sql`) since an English mystical-sounding name sat oddly in an all-French UI for a feature whose whole point is grounded, numbers-based trust, not magic.

**Grounding**: `alpha-chat` calls the existing `get_reports_snapshot`/`get_stock_velocity` RPCs with the **caller's own JWT-scoped client**, never service-role — both RPCs derive role/user_id from `auth.uid()` internally (the v121 security fix), so a service-role call would silently hit the internal-reconciliation code path instead of the caller's real role-gated data. Snapshot values are converted from cents to display units before ever reaching the prompt; low-stock/rupture detection is computed in the edge function from `get_stock_velocity`, not left to the model to infer from raw numbers.

**One conversation per (business, user), not per business** — mirrors `support_conversations`' one-row pattern but keyed on `(business_id, user_id)`: a vendeur's answer (personal figures only) and an administrateur's answer (full P&L) must never land in the same shared thread the way the boutique chat room intentionally shares one thread per business.

**Quota model: one rolling-24-hour-window mechanism for both tiers, never true "unlimited."** Free: **5 messages/24h as of `migration_v136.sql`** (raised from 3 so the free-tier counter reads as a real trial rather than an instant wall). A business with `has_ai_access()` (mirrors `isSubscriptionExpired`/`hasBonusAccess`, SQL-side): **100 messages/24h as of `migration_v146.sql`** (temporarily lowered to 20 by `migration_v135.sql` while Groq's Developer tier was blocked, restored to 100 once the Groq→OpenAI automatic fallback made that stopgap unnecessary — see "Billing" below) — marketed as "Alpha Pro" with the real number stated plainly (see the paywall copy note below), not as "Illimité." The first 10 user messages ever in a conversation bypass the window entirely (the "welcome burst") so a brand-new merchant's first session never hits friction. All enforcement is inside `send_alpha_message` (SECURITY DEFINER) — never client-side; `get_alpha_quota_status` is a read-only companion for the client's live countdown/upsell UI. The remaining-count row in `app/(app)/alpha/index.tsx` shows for **both** tiers now, not just free — it used to be gated on `!has_ai_access`, so paid subscribers never saw how many messages they had left in the day.

**Live-configurable via `app_config`/`set_alpha_daily_limit` (`migration_v147.sql`) — but only some surfaces are actually live.** Changing either tier's limit takes effect immediately in enforcement (`send_alpha_message`) and in the in-app remaining-count row above (`get_alpha_quota_status`'s `limit` field flows straight through, nothing cached). It does **not** automatically update the marketing copy that states a number — `PaywallScreen.tsx`'s `PAID_DAILY_LIMIT`, `djomi-checkout/index.ts`'s `PAID_DAILY_LIMIT`, and the plain string in `abonnement/index.html` are still hardcoded constants, unrelated to `app_config`. Changing the real limit without also editing those three leaves the paywall/checkout pages advertising a stale number — a known gap, not yet wired to read live, flagged rather than silently left inconsistent.

**Billing**: `GROQ_API_KEY` needs Groq's **Developer (pay-as-you-go) tier**, not the free tier — the free tier caps `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` at 100,000 tokens/day **shared across the whole Groq org** (every business, plus `generate-support-draft`), nowhere near enough once Alpha is used daily. Adding a card is not enough by itself: as of 2026-07-13, Groq's console shows "Developer tier upgrades are temporarily unavailable due to high demand" org-wide — billing only activates once the org is actually upgraded to a paid tier, so a card on file while still on Free doesn't raise limits or get charged. At ~900-1,600 tokens per Alpha reply (system prompt + history + output), the 100K/day Free-tier ceiling only sustains ~60-100 total exchanges/day across the whole platform on Groq alone — `migration_v135.sql` lowered the paid tier's quota from 100/24h to 20/24h as a stopgap so one active paying business couldn't exhaust every other business's Groq access for the rest of the day. **`migration_v146.sql` restored it to 100/24h** once the Groq→OpenAI automatic fallback (below) made that stopgap unnecessary: Groq exhaustion now soft-degrades to OpenAI per-request instead of hard-failing for everyone else, and per-message cost is negligible on either provider (~$0.0002–0.0003/reply on `gpt-4o-mini`, meaning even a paid user maxing out 100/day all month costs roughly $0.10–0.15 in raw AI spend) — cost was never actually the constraint, Groq's shared capacity ceiling was, and that ceiling no longer causes hard failures.

**Conversation history bug (fixed 2026-07-13):** `alpha-chat/index.ts` fetched prior messages with `.order('created_at', { ascending: true }).limit(N)` — ascending+limit returns the *oldest* N rows, not the most recent. Past N total messages in a conversation, that permanently excludes everything newer, including the question this very call is answering (already inserted by `send_alpha_message()` before the edge function runs) — Alpha would silently stop reading the user's actual question and just keep re-answering frozen early context, forever, for that thread. Fixed to `.order(..., { ascending: false }).limit(10)` then `.reverse()` — fetches the most recent 10 messages, restores chronological order. Also trimmed the window from 20→10: that history is resent in full on every single Groq/OpenAI call, so it's the main lever on cost-per-message alongside the reply token budget in `callChatCompletions` (see "Reply truncation" below). Any future change to this query needs both halves (DESC + reverse) — ascending+limit alone reintroduces the bug.

**Reply truncation — mid-sentence cutoffs in production (fixed 2026-07-18).** `callChatCompletions` hardcoded `max_tokens: 400` with no check on whether the model actually finished — a real reply was observed cut off mid-word ("...ce qui correspond à environ **1000 maill"). The API returns `finish_reason: 'length'` whenever a reply is truncated; the code never read it, just shipped `message.content` as-is. 400 tokens is tight for what the system prompt actually requires per reply (bolded figures + trend comparison + a conditional "Action à faire" line, in French, which tokenizes worse than English) — the cap existed "to save cost," but this codebase's own numbers show that cost is negligible (~$0.0002-0.0003/reply even on OpenAI, ~$0.10-0.15/month for a paid user maxing out their daily quota) — the requirement had outlived its reason. Fixed: raised to `MAX_REPLY_TOKENS = 1000` (not tuned as "how long an answer should be" — that's the prompt's job; this is a backstop against a genuine malfunction, a repetition/degeneration loop, doubly relevant since this function auto-falls-back between two different models), and `finish_reason` is now checked — if a reply still hits the cap, the partial content is fed back as an assistant turn with an explicit "continue exactly where you stopped" instruction, up to `MAX_CONTINUATIONS = 1` times, rather than silently returning a severed sentence.

**A number Alpha stated was not a hallucination — it was a real bug in `get_product_stats()` (found the same session, fixed by `migration_v152.sql`).** The same conversation that surfaced the truncation bug also had Alpha state a single product's lifetime revenue (42,085,000 GNF, via the `chercher_produit` tool) that *exceeded* the business's own lifetime total revenue (38,130,002 GNF, from the fixed data block's `depuis_le_debut`) — a mathematically impossible pair of numbers stated four messages apart in the same thread. The system prompt's grounding rule ("never state a number that isn't in the data") is unenforced prose, not code — but tracing it down, the model wasn't violating that rule at all: `get_product_stats()` (unlike `get_reports_snapshot`/`get_best_sellers`) filtered `so.status != 'annule'` instead of `so.status IN ('paye', 'credit')`, so it was summing revenue from `brouillon`/`confirme` orders that were never actually paid — a real, faithfully-relayed SQL bug, not an invented number. Lesson: an ungrounded-looking number from an LLM tool-calling pipeline isn't automatically a hallucination — check the RPC the tool actually called before assuming the model made it up; a text-level "does this number appear anywhere in the tool output" check would have passed this reply, since 42,085,000 genuinely was in the tool's JSON response.

**Groq-first, OpenAI-fallback (added 2026-07-13):** `generateReply()` in `alpha-chat/index.ts` tries Groq first, and the instant that call fails for *any* reason (daily token ceiling hit, rate limit, outage), the same request falls through to OpenAI (`gpt-4o-mini`) automatically — no manual cutover. This was a deliberate choice over a budget-exhaustion-triggered switch: Groq's 100K-tokens/day ceiling is a shared, same-day pool with no advance warning before it's hit, so there's no reliable moment to "predict" and schedule a cutover around — the failing request itself is the only real signal. Both Groq and OpenAI speak the same OpenAI-compatible chat-completions shape, so `callChatCompletions()` is one shared function parameterized by base URL/key/model rather than two near-duplicate implementations. The `alpha_messages.model` column now records whichever model actually served each reply (previously hardcoded to `GROQ_MODEL`) — check that column to see how often the fallback is actually firing. Free and paid tier both go through the same fallback path; nothing routes them to different providers on purpose. `generate-support-draft` (the founder support-chat drafter, sharing the same Groq token budget) does **not** have this fallback yet — it still fails closed to a `status='failed'` draft row if Groq is unavailable, since a missed AI draft there just means the founder replies without a suggestion, not a broken live conversation. Requires the `OPENAI_API_KEY` Supabase Edge Function secret to be set (Restricted-permission key, "Model capabilities" scope only) or the fallback silently has nothing to fall through to.

**"Aladji" bug — a salesperson described as a product (fixed 2026-07-13):** `get_reports_snapshot`'s `top_sellers` field is a **staff revenue leaderboard** (who on the team sold the most — same data rendered as "meilleurs vendeurs" in the Rapports screen, grouped by `seller_id`), not product data. `alpha-chat/index.ts`'s `buildDataBlock` was mapping it into a field literally called `meilleurs_produits` ("best products") in the JSON handed to the LLM — so when a solo-vendeur business's top (and only) seller was named "Aladji", Alpha confidently described "le produit Aladji" and suggested restocking it, because the data block told it, in effect, that Aladji was a product. Fixed: renamed to `meilleurs_vendeurs`, and added a genuine product-level query (`get_best_sellers(business_id, month_start, limit)`, already used by the Accueil dashboard) mapped to `produits_les_plus_vendus`. The system prompt now explicitly states the two lists are never interchangeable — a name in `meilleurs_vendeurs` is always a person, a name in `produits_les_plus_vendus` is always a catalog item. `get_best_sellers` has no role gate of its own (unlike `get_reports_snapshot`, which derives role from `auth.uid()` internally), so it's only fetched for administrateur/manager/investisseur — fetching it for a vendeur would leak every other seller's product revenue to someone who's only supposed to see their own sales.

**Vague/near-empty messages produced a full generic answer, not a clarifying question:** `send_alpha_message` only rejects a message that's empty after `trim()` — a single character or a stray "ok" passes validation, consumes a quota slot, and (before this fix) the system prompt had no instruction telling the model to recognize a non-question, so it just dumped the same "Données du commerce" summary every time regardless of what was typed. Fixed by adding an explicit prompt rule: on a message too short/vague to be a real question, Alpha must ask a short clarifying question with 1-2 concrete examples instead of restating the snapshot. This is prompt-level, not a stricter server-side length check — the goal was Alpha behaving like it's actually listening, not blocking short input outright.

The first version of that rule wasn't strict enough: on a follow-up vague message ("Autre chose"), Alpha asked a clarifying question but *also* cited real seller names/amounts from "Données du commerce" as part of the examples and still appended an "Action à faire" section — exactly the generic-answer behavior the rule was meant to prevent, just wrapped in a question. Tightened the prompt rule to state explicitly that a clarification response must contain *nothing else* — no figures, no names, no action section, only generic topic examples (ventes/stock/dépenses/trésorerie/crédit) — and that real data may only be cited once the merchant has asked an actual specific question.

**Alpha didn't know today's date, so relative-period questions dead-ended in an ISO-date clarification loop (fixed 2026-07-25).** The single most natural merchant question — "combien de ventes cette semaine ?" — was met with "précisez la période exacte, ex: du 2026-07-18 au 2026-07-24 ?", and when the merchant answered ("Yes", "Juste les 7 derniers jours") Alpha repeated the *identical* question, never answering. Root cause: `buildSystemPrompt` (`alpha-chat/index.ts`) never put the current date in the prompt, and the grounding data block is a fixed 30-day aggregate with no date anchor — so the only tool that can answer a dated question, `ventes_sur_periode`, requires concrete `AAAA-MM-JJ` dates the model had no way to derive from "cette semaine"/"aujourd'hui"/"les 7 derniers jours". It punted by asking the merchant to type an ISO range (which no shopkeeper thinks in) and looped because the plain-language reply still gave it no usable dates. The plausible-looking example dates were gpt-oss half-guessing from its own internal clock, which isn't authoritative — and the OpenAI fallback (`gpt-4o-mini`) gets no date at all. Not the same bug as the vague-message rule above: `send_alpha_message` correctly treats "cette semaine" as a real question; the model just couldn't act on it. Fix: `buildDateContext()` injects today's date **plus precomputed relative ranges** (hier, 7 derniers jours, cette semaine depuis lundi, ce mois-ci, le mois dernier) into the prompt — precomputed, not left to model date arithmetic, same posture as `produits_stock_bas`/`evolution_pct` (LLMs get weekday/week-boundary math wrong). Guinea is GMT year-round with no DST, so the edge function's UTC clock is the merchant's local date — no timezone conversion. A new prompt rule tells Alpha to resolve relative periods from those markers itself and call `ventes_sur_periode` directly, and to **never** ask a merchant for ISO dates; if a period is genuinely ambiguous it must offer plain-language choices ("cette semaine, ce mois-ci, ou le mois dernier ?"), never date ranges. Prompt/edge-function only — ships via `supabase functions deploy alpha-chat`, no migration, no app build.

**Bold key figures (added 2026-07-13):** requested so the 1-3 most important numbers in a reply are visually scannable on a small screen. The prompt now asks Alpha to wrap its most important figures in `**bold**` markdown, capped at 1-3 per reply (over-bolding defeats the point). The chat bubble in `app/(app)/alpha/index.tsx` is plain `<Text>` with no markdown renderer, so a `renderBold()` helper splits assistant message content on `**...**` and renders matched segments as bold nested `<Text>` — applied only to assistant messages (`!isOwn`), since a user's own message is never expected to carry that markup.

**Vouvoiement (added 2026-07-13):** nothing in `STATIC_INSTRUCTIONS` ever told the model how to address the merchant, so it defaulted to "tu" on its own (likely pattern-matching "ton direct, concret" in the tone rule). Added an explicit rule to always address the merchant as "vous" — a basic mark of respect toward someone running their own business, not just a formality.

That first version of the rule didn't survive in an *existing* conversation: the last-10-messages window resent on every turn (see "Conversation history bug" above) still contained several assistant turns from before the fix that used "tu", and the model kept imitating its own established register from that history over the system prompt's instruction — a live conversation doesn't reset just because the system prompt changed underneath it. Strengthened the rule to explicitly tell the model that any "tu" in its own prior messages in this same thread was a mistake to silently correct, never continue. Also tightened the "Action à faire cette semaine" rule the same session: it was appearing as a bare `Action à faire cette semaine : ?` on clarification-question replies — the model held onto the heading text (pattern-completing its own template from real prior answers) while dropping the content it was told to omit. Fixed by naming the exact failure mode in the rule (never emit that heading empty, placeholder, or question-marked) and restating that it belongs *only* to non-clarification replies. A genuinely fresh conversation is the cleanest way to verify either fix — an existing thread's older turns take a few exchanges to age out of that 10-message window.

**Staff-name pseudonymization before hitting Groq/OpenAI (added 2026-07-13):** the only personally-identifying data Alpha ever sends a third party is `meilleurs_vendeurs` — real staff names each tied to their individual revenue. `buildDataBlock` now replaces each name with a positional label ("Vendeur A", "Vendeur B", …) before the data block is built, and returns a `nameToLabel` map alongside it. Three places need that map, not just the outgoing data block, or the protection is incomplete: (1) the fresh data block itself; (2) the resent conversation history (`turns`, fetched from `alpha_messages`) — those rows are stored with real names for the merchant's own chat view, so they're anonymized on the fly via `replaceNames()` right before being sent as context, never mutating the stored rows; (3) the model's new reply, which naturally comes back referring to "Vendeur A" — reversed via the same map (`labelToName`) before that reply is persisted or returned, so the merchant still sees the real name. Only staff names are pseudonymized, not product names or business-level financials — those aren't personally identifying. Label assignment is positional per-request (ranked by revenue that call), not a stable per-person id stored anywhere, so if a ranking reshuffles between turns "Vendeur A" could refer to a different person turn-to-turn — an accepted tradeoff over adding a persistent name↔label table for what's meant to be a lightweight mitigation, not a guarantee.

**Period-over-period trend (added 2026-07-13):** Alpha previously only ever saw a flat 30-day snapshot — no way to say whether a number was actually good or bad without an implicit baseline. `get_reports_snapshot` is now called twice per request: once normally (current 30 days), once with `p_today` shifted back by `PERIOD_DAYS` — since the RPC internally computes `v_period_start := p_today - p_period_days`, passing a shifted `p_today` (with the same `p_period_days`) yields exactly the 30 days immediately before the current window, at zero extra migration cost (no SQL changes at all). `buildTrendBlock` diffs the two into `evolution_vs_periode_precedente` (`evolution_pct` + a `hausse`/`baisse`/`stable` label), computed server-side so Alpha never does its own percentage math — same reasoning as precomputed `produits_stock_bas`. Only period-bound flow metrics are compared (`revenue`, `net_profit`, `period_order_count`, or the vendeur-personal equivalents) — `credit_outstanding`/`cash_on_hand`/`stock_value` are live all-time balances the SQL computes identically regardless of `p_today`, so diffing them across the two calls would just compare a number to itself, not an actual trend. `previous === 0` reports `evolution_pct: null` with `tendance: 'hausse'` rather than a division-by-zero artifact — the prompt tells Alpha to read that as "nothing to compare last period" in words, never print the literal `null`.

**Lifetime totals, "depuis_le_debut" (added 2026-07-13):** requested as "give Alpha access to all their data." Taken literally that's not actually implementable or desirable — a business with two years of history could have thousands of rows, and stuffing per-transaction detail into every single message would blow the shared Groq/OpenAI token budget (see "Billing" above) and send far more to those third parties than necessary. What's cheap and useful instead: a third `get_reports_snapshot` call with `p_period_days: LIFETIME_PERIOD_DAYS` (3650 — no business on this platform is anywhere near that old, so it always captures true lifetime totals without needing to look up the business's real creation date). `buildLifetimeBlock` reduces that to a handful of aggregate numbers (`chiffre_affaires_total`, `profit_net_total`, `nombre_ventes_total`, or the vendeur-personal equivalents) under `depuis_le_debut` — same "aggregate, never raw rows" posture as the 30-day and trend blocks, just a wider window. The prompt tells Alpha to reach for this field specifically for "how's my business doing overall" questions, as distinct from the 30-day `chiffre_affaires` used for "how's this month going."

**Money-first framing:** `STATIC_INSTRUCTIONS` previously only said "cite real numbers instead of generalities." It now states Alpha's mission explicitly and in order — truth first (never invent a number outside "Données du commerce"), then: every answer must help the merchant make/keep more money, reasoned from *this* business's actual figures rather than generic small-business advice that may not apply here.

**Entry point (redesigned 2026-09-02 — removed the bottom pill, kept only the header icon).** Originally a "Parler avec Alpha…" input styled as a fully rounded, detached pill with an animated rotating gradient glow ring, docked to the bottom of the Accueil screen's content, plus a plain header "A" icon as a second entry point. Removed the pill entirely on direct product feedback: it read as too loud/attention-grabbing on the one screen meant to lead with the day's revenue number, and permanently occupied real vertical space above the tab bar — the glow-ring treatment in particular was exactly the "loud but constant" pattern the same-week color-restraint redesign (see the "Real elevation + `Pill`" note above) was arguing against. **Alpha's only dashboard entry point now is the plain, uncolored "A" header icon** (`app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx`'s header, top-right) — jumps straight to `app/(app)/alpha` with no pre-fill. Real tradeoff, accepted deliberately: the header icon is a quieter "go back to the conversation" affordance, not an inviting "type a new question right now" prompt the way the pill was — if Alpha engagement from Accueil measurably drops, that's the first thing to revisit, not assume unrelated. The former "Support icon relocated to make room" reasoning below is now historical — the header slot was freed for Alpha before the pill existed, and stays Alpha's home now that the pill is gone.

**Support icon relocated to make room for Alpha's header icon (unchanged by the above).** The header's headphone/support icon (top-right of Accueil) was moved into the lateral `BusinessDrawer` footer, alongside the founder's existing "Service client" row — same destinations as before (founder → `support-inbox`, regular member → `support`), purely relocated.

**Upsell UX**: when the quota is exhausted and the business has no AI access, the composer disables and an inline card (`PaywallScreen`'s `inline` prop) slides in above it — showing the specific question just typed, a subscribe button, and the honest wait-time alternative — rather than a route-level full-screen swap, so the still-visible conversation stays on screen at the exact moment of intent.

**Free vs. paid exhaustion are two distinct UI states (added 2026-07-13), not one.** `app/(app)/alpha/index.tsx` computes `freeQuotaExhausted` and `paidQuotaExhausted` separately from `quota`. A free-tier user who tries to send past their limit sees the upgrade popup above (they can act on it — upgrading unblocks them immediately). A **paid**-tier user at their 100/24h cap only ever sees a plain `waitCard` ("Vous pourrez reparler à Alpha dans Xh") with no subscribe CTA — showing an upgrade offer to someone already paying would be nonsensical and was explicitly called out as something to avoid. The two states share the same underlying `waitCard`, reached by different paths.

**The upgrade popup shows on every blocked free-tier send attempt, not throttled.** An earlier version of this capped the popup to once per 24h per business (via a local KV timestamp) and fell back to the plain `waitCard` in between, to avoid feeling like spam. That was reversed the same day: a free-tier user who's actively trying to send *should* see the offer every time, since that's the exact moment of intent — the "don't be a headache" instinct instead applies to a separate, still-unbuilt passive reminder (see below), not to this in-the-moment block. `freeQuotaExhausted` now goes straight to `setPendingQuestion(trimmed)` with no KV check in between; the `waitCard` is reachable only via `paidQuotaExhausted`, since a paying user is never shown the upgrade offer at all.

**Home-screen pre-filled auto-send used to bypass the quota check entirely (fixed 2026-07-13).** The "Demandez Alpha…" pill on Accueil navigates to `app/(app)/alpha` with the question pre-filled, auto-sent on mount. That auto-send effect used to call `sendMessage()` from the store directly instead of going through `handleSend`'s `freeQuotaExhausted`/`paidQuotaExhausted` gate — so an already-exhausted free-tier user submitting from the pill hit the raw `send_alpha_message` server rejection instead of the upgrade popup, while the in-chat composer (which does call `handleSend`) showed the popup correctly for the exact same state. Fixed by routing the auto-send through `handleSend` too. That alone wasn't sufficient: `quota` is fetched fire-and-forget inside `load()` (`stores/alpha.ts`), so it's still `null` on the very first render — evaluating the exhaustion check against a `null` quota always reads "not exhausted," race-defeating the fix. The auto-send effect now waits for `quota !== null` before deciding (re-running once the fetch resolves), with a 4s timeout fallback so a failed quota fetch (which leaves `quota` permanently `null`, since `fetchQuota` silently no-ops on error) can't strand the pre-filled question forever.

**Composer didn't account for being offline (fixed 2026-07-13).** Unlike support chat's `sendMessage` (KV-queued on a network error, `stores/supportChat.ts`), Alpha has no offline queue by design — a conversational reply needs a live round trip, so a message sent while offline is a real, immediate failure, not something to defer. The composer previously didn't reflect that: the `TextInput`/send button stayed fully enabled while offline, a doomed send cleared the typed text *before* the RPC even ran (so a network failure erased it, forcing a retype), and the store's `sendMessage` catch block never distinguished a network error from any other failure — no `offline` flag got set from a failed send (only from a failed initial `load()`), and the message fell through to `translateError`'s generic fallback instead of a clear "needs a connection" message. Fixed: `sendMessage` (`stores/alpha.ts`) now returns `Promise<boolean>`, sets `offline: true` and a dedicated French message on `isNetworkError`, and always strips the failed optimistic message; `handleSend` (`app/(app)/alpha/index.tsx`) only clears the composer when that boolean is `true`, so a failed send leaves the question in place for a plain retry. The composer itself (`TextInput` + send button) is now `editable={!offline}`/`disabled={offline}` with a dimmed style and an offline-specific placeholder, so the app doesn't invite a send it already knows will fail.

**Quota-reset push reminder, both tiers (`migration_v138.sql`, `send-alpha-quota-reminders`).** The original product intent here was an in-app-only touchpoint, not a push — reversed on explicit direction: once a user's exhausted quota window resets, they now get a real push, "Vous pouvez parler à Alpha maintenant" (free) / "...Alpha Pro maintenant" (paid), so returning to Alpha doesn't depend on them happening to reopen the app. This only works because `alpha_quota`'s window is *fixed*, not sliding (see `migration_v133.sql`) — `window_start`/`count_in_window` don't advance on their own, only on the user's next `send_alpha_message()` call, so a row that hit its limit just sits there in an "expired but still exhausted" state until either the user tries again (lazy reset) or the reminder cron catches it first via `get_and_mark_alpha_quota_resets()`. That gives a single deterministic "you can send again" instant to notify at — a true sliding window wouldn't have one. Quiet hours (21:00–07:00 UTC, standing in for Guinea local night since there's no per-business timezone column and Guinea has no DST) are a hard skip, not a defer-with-backoff: the cron just doesn't call the marking RPC during that window, so anyone whose reset happened overnight is still caught, unmarked, on the first run after quiet hours end. Distinct from the popup-on-blocked-send behavior above (unthrottled, in-app, fires the instant a blocked send is attempted) — this is the opposite direction, telling someone who's *not currently looking at the app* that they can come back. Not yet wired to an actual cron schedule; needs an hourly Supabase dashboard Cron Job once deployed (see the Edge Functions table entry).

**Paywall copy dropped "Illimité" for "Alpha Pro" + a stated number (2026-07-13).** The tier used to be marketed as "Alpha Illimité" with the actual cap deliberately never stated, specifically so a stopgap quota change (`migration_v135.sql`) wouldn't need a copy update. Reversed on explicit product direction: a paying user discovering a silent cap after being sold "unlimited" is a worse trust break than stating a concrete, generous number up front — Hormozi's "specificity sells better than vague hype" applied literally. `PaywallScreen.tsx` now has a `PAID_DAILY_LIMIT` constant (mirroring `send_alpha_message`'s `v_limit` — currently 100 as of `migration_v146.sql`) used in both the full-screen plan card ("100 conversations avec Alpha, chaque jour") and the inline upsell card copy — **this constant has to be bumped by hand alongside any future change to the SQL `v_limit`**, the tradeoff the old "never state a number" design was avoiding. The same hardcoded copy also appears in `djomi-checkout/index.ts`'s `PAID_DAILY_LIMIT` and as a plain string in `abonnement/index.html` — all three need updating together, there's no shared constant across the Deno function and the static GitHub Pages file. The inline card also stopped repeating the user's just-typed question back to them (it's already visible in the chat transcript right above the card — showing it twice was clutter, not personalization) in favor of a plain outcome-focused headline, "Obtenez la réponse à votre question."

**CTA reads "Investir", not "Payer" or "S'abonner"** — deliberately framing the subscription as an investment in the business rather than a transactional purchase, consistent with the "one stockout avoided pays for months of this" ROI framing already used elsewhere on the paywall. Applied to both the full-screen and inline CTA buttons.

**Breathing CTA animation, on both the full-screen and inline cards.** A fabricated "was $299.99" price-reveal was prototyped and then deliberately dropped (compliance risk — Apple review and some jurisdictions treat a fake reference price as deceptive pricing) in favor of just the CTA breathing motion, kept on its own. The button starts a slow "breathing" scale pulse (`BREATH_HALF_CYCLE_MS = 2000`, ~4s per full cycle) after `BREATHE_START_DELAY_MS`, currently a flat **5s** (an initial reading-time-based estimate of ~18s — based on ~73 words of copy above the CTA at ~240 wpm — was overridden down to 5s on explicit product direction). The cadence itself is deliberately matched to a resting human breath rate (~12-16/min), not a fast attention-grabbing pulse, since a quicker rhythm reads as urgent/alarming rather than inviting. Originally scoped to the full-screen paywall only, then extended to the inline mid-chat card too — the inline card is the one users actually hit in the real quota-exhaustion flow, so scoping the animation out of it left the far more common path with no breathing CTA at all.

**On-demand tool-calling, moving Alpha from a fixed-snapshot chatbot toward an agent (added 2026-07-14).** Until now every reply was built the same way regardless of the question: fetch the same 3 `get_reports_snapshot` calls + `get_stock_velocity` + `get_best_sellers`, stuff them into one fixed data block, one LLM call. That's a real ceiling — a merchant asking about ONE named product, ONE named client, an arbitrary date/period ("why was last Tuesday slow"), or an itemized expense breakdown could only ever be answered by the model guessing from the aggregate, since nothing in that fixed block could actually answer those questions. `alpha-chat/index.ts` now also gives the model 4 real OpenAI/Groq-compatible tools it can call mid-reply: `chercher_produit`, `chercher_client`, `ventes_sur_periode`, `depenses_detail` — `executeTool` runs each one through the CALLER's own JWT-scoped `userClient`, so the existing RLS policies (vendeur sees only their own sales/expenses via `migration_v19`/`v20`, admin/manager/investisseur see the full business) gate every tool result automatically, with **no new SECURITY DEFINER RPC and no migration**. The one exception is `get_product_stats` (used inside `chercher_produit`): like `get_best_sellers` elsewhere in this file, it has no role gate of its own (only `is_member`), so profit/cost figures are withheld from vendeur in code rather than by RLS. The fixed 3-snapshot block is still sent on every turn (cheap — parallel Postgres RPCs, not LLM tokens — and covers the common "how's my shop doing" question); the tools are additive, only invoked when the model decides a specific lookup is needed. `callChatCompletions`'s loop is capped at `MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 3`, with the final round deliberately sent WITHOUT the `tools` param so the model is forced to answer in plain text instead of looping forever. The system prompt also gained an explicit grounding rule: never state a number that doesn't literally appear in "Données du commerce" or in a tool result from THIS reply — the cheap, no-extra-API-call version of a self-check/reflection pass, chosen over a second paid LLM verification call given the already-tight shared Groq token budget documented under "Billing" below. **Unverified assumption, flagged for whoever deploys this next:** the whole design assumes Groq's `llama-3.3-70b-versatile` actually supports OpenAI-compatible tool-calling. If that assumption is wrong, the existing Groq→OpenAI fallback still degrades safely (a rejected `tools` param just throws on round 0 and falls through to OpenAI, per the existing "Groq fails for ANY reason" fallback design) — but every message would then silently route to OpenAI instead of Groq, which is a cost regression, not a correctness one. Confirm with one real deployed test conversation before assuming this is cost-neutral. Not yet deployed as of this writing.

### Daily digest — habit-forming afternoon push (`db/migration_v139.sql`, `send-daily-digest`, `learn-digest-send-hours`)

A recurring "how did the shop do" push aimed at admin/manager, deliberately designed as a habit trigger, not a report — the design constraints came directly from that goal and are easy to get wrong by treating it like just another notification type.

**Every business gets pushed every day it's due, including days with zero sales.** The instinct to skip empty days is wrong for this specific feature: a habit-forming trigger only works if it's *reliable* — the moment it becomes conditional on good news, a missing push stops meaning anything (was it a quiet day, or did the job just not run?), and the exact habit being built collapses. It also means the app goes silent precisely when a merchant most needs the nudge to check in. Contrast with the Alpha quota-reset reminder above, which is conditional by nature (there's nothing to say if the quota never reset) — this one is unconditional by design.

**The message is never a bare number, and never on the same clock time for every business.** Two branches, both starting with the same "La journée..." opening on purpose — a habit-forming push should look the same shape every day and vary only its content, or it reads as a random ping instead of a ritual:
- Revenue > 0 today: **"La journée est bonne, vous avez fait : {amount}."** — "vous avez fait," not "vous avez fait aujourd'hui" or "bravo" — the amount carries the good news, nothing is tacked on top of it.
- Revenue = 0 today: **"La journée était calme. On se retrouve demain."** — deliberately not "était mauvaise" (that would be exactly the kind of unsolicited-lesson tone that undermines trust in a daily push) and deliberately carries zero data — no "0 GNF," no comparison to yesterday, no stock/credit nudge grafted on. Earlier drafts considered a comparative framing ("hier vous aviez fait X") and an actionable nudge (surface a low-stock item or overdue credit instead) — both rejected on explicit product direction: the goal on a quiet day is to make the merchant feel normal, not to teach them something they already know.

**Send time is learned per business, not a platform-wide clock time.** `learn_digest_send_hours()` (run once daily, early morning, by `learn-digest-send-hours`) looks at each business's last 14 days of sales, finds the hour their *last* sale of the day usually lands, and sets `digest_send_hour` to one hour after that — their natural wind-down point, not mid-hustle. Clamped to 16:00–21:00 (Guinea is GMT year-round with no DST, so this UTC window doubles as the local 4pm–9pm window with no conversion needed). A business needs at least 5 sale-days in the trailing 14 to earn a learned time; below that (brand new, or genuinely dormant) it gets a flat 17:00 default until enough history accumulates. `send-daily-digest` runs hourly and just asks "whose learned hour is right now, and who hasn't been sent to yet today" via `get_and_mark_daily_digest_businesses()` — the two jobs are split on purpose so the per-business hour isn't relearned on every single hourly tick.

**Revenue uses the same formula as `get_reports_snapshot`** (`sale_orders.total_amount - discount_amount`, `status IN ('paye','credit')`, filtered to `sale_date`) rather than summing `payments` — so the digest number and the Rapports screen's number for "today" never disagree.

`get_and_mark_daily_digest_businesses()` originally raised `structure of query does not match function result type` on every real call — found via an integration test before this ever shipped. Both `total_amount` and `discount_amount` are `bigint` (v24), so the subtraction itself is `bigint`, but Postgres's `SUM()` aggregate always widens a `bigint` input to `numeric` (overflow protection, per the SQL standard) — that `numeric` didn't match the function's declared `RETURNS TABLE(..., revenue_cents bigint, ...)`. Fixed with an explicit `::bigint` cast on the `COALESCE(SUM(...), 0)` expression. Any future aggregate over a `bigint` monetary column in a function with a typed `RETURNS TABLE`/`RETURNS SETOF` needs the same cast — `SUM()` never returns `bigint` back, even when every input and the arithmetic between them is.

Neither cron job is scheduled yet — both need an hourly (`send-daily-digest`) / daily (`learn-digest-send-hours`) Supabase dashboard Cron Job once deployed, same setup as the other `x-cron-secret`-authenticated jobs above.

### Security & role isolation

Four roles: `administrateur`, `manager`, `vendeur`, `investisseur`.

**RLS is the primary gate.** All writes that vendeurs can trigger go through SECURITY DEFINER RPCs (never direct table INSERT):
- `submit_sale(...)` — creates sale_order + so_lines + payment + stock_moves atomically. Enforces that a vendeur can only submit sales in their own name. Accepts optional `p_client_id` and `p_idempotency_key`.
- `cancel_sale(p_sale_id, p_business_id, p_reason)` — marks annulé, restores stock. Vendeurs can only cancel their own sales.
- `join_business(p_code)` — validates invite code, enforces rate limit (5/10 min), expiry, max_uses, manager limit, join limit (3 non-admin memberships), then inserts membership atomically. Raises specific French exceptions for each failure mode.
- `get_best_sellers(p_business_id, p_month_start, p_limit)` — server-side aggregation.
- `delete_my_account()` — safe self-deletion with admin guard.
- `receive_purchase_order(p_po_id, p_business_id)` — admin/manager only; uses `auth.uid()` for audit trail.
- `create_market_post(p_title, p_content, p_category)` — derives author_name from profiles server-side.
- `create_market_comment(p_post_id, p_parent_id, p_content)` — same.
- `toggle_post_like` / `toggle_comment_like` — enforce 3-likes/day velocity cap.

**Frontend is defense-in-depth** (not the primary control):
- Catalogue + Vendre tabs hidden for investisseur (`href: null` in tabs layout).
- `fetchSales` passes `sellerId` filter for vendeurs so they only see their own sales.
- `ventes/index.tsx` defaults to a 90-day window; "Voir tout l'historique" toggle re-fetches without the date filter.

**Invite codes — three accepted risks, decided 2026-09-27 security audit, not to be re-litigated without new information.** Checklist item 1.7 ("invite codes treated like credentials") wants codes stored as a digest, redemption rate-limiting with no bypass, and one generic error across every failure mode. All three were audited, found genuinely lacking against the letter of that checklist, and deliberately left as-is:
- **Codes are stored raw (`invite_codes.code`), not hashed.** Structural, not an oversight: unlike an OTP ("prove you know this, then forget it"), an invite code is "share this so someone else can prove they know it" — the admin needs to re-open and re-copy it if the first share attempt failed. Hashing it would require a real "show once" redesign (display exactly once at creation, hash for storage, revoke-and-reissue if lost — never re-viewable). Accepted because reading the raw value via the app's own path already requires being an admin/manager of that specific business (RLS-scoped since `migration_v150.sql`); the only scenario where "raw" matters at all is a database-level compromise, at which point an attacker already has every sale, debt, and phone number in the business — the invite codes are the smallest part of that damage, and they're single-use + 24h-expiring regardless.
- **Redemption rate-limiting (5/10min) is bypassable by a caller who skips the app and calls `join_business()` directly**, never calling the separate `record_invite_attempt()` RPC first. This split exists *because* of a real bug (`migration_v124.sql`): a single Postgres function call is one atomic transaction, so an attempt-log insert made before a later `RAISE EXCEPTION` in the same call gets rolled back with everything else — logging had to move to its own top-level call to actually persist on a failed attempt. The unavoidable side effect: nothing forces a caller to make that separate call at all. Closing this for real needs either an autonomous sub-transaction (the `dblink` extension, confirmed **not** available on this project) or restructuring every error in `join_business()` from `RAISE EXCEPTION` into a returned value — a much larger, riskier change to this codebase's whole error-surfacing convention. Accepted because codes carry ~40 bits of entropy (8 chars, 32-symbol alphabet) — even fully unthrottled guessing needs on the order of 10^12 attempts to land one specific code, several orders of magnitude beyond what's practically automatable over a network.
